# WowAuction — Spec (draft v0.7, 2026-11-01)

Two pieces:

1. **AHSync**: a desktop companion app and the **brain**.
   - Start it whenever you're ready to play. It works out where it left off and catches up on auctionhouse.fyi prices.
   - It also reads AHledger's public price table (§2.3), a second community source, and blends it into the same history.
   - It keeps a local 90-day history, works out every recommendation, and shows them in a **local dashboard** (out of game).
   - It also writes the same results into the addon.
   - **Planned (M22–M27):** it ships as a standalone app for Windows and macOS, with a tray icon, a first-run setup page, start at login, the addon installed by the app and updates that install themselves (§3.7).
2. **AHProfit**: an in-game addon that acts as the **eyes and hands**.
   - It records your characters, professions, recipes, inventory and gold.
   - It runs its own auction house scans.
   - In game, it shows the recommendations, adds tooltip lines, and searches the auction house so you can buy with one click.

What it recommends:

1. buy and disenchant
2. craft and sell now
3. a weekly plan: buy or craft now and sell later, plus **when to sell what you already own** for the most profit
4. **planned:** one funded portfolio across all your characters, Today and the next 7 days together, that makes the most timing-adjusted profit within your risk tolerance (§3.4D)
5. what to farm: the farmable materials ranked by AH price, with gold per hour at your own observed pace and when to sell (§3.4E, the Gathering page; **built:** the day-one page, M16, loot capture with observed rates, M17, and the price history with the full detail, M18)

Names are placeholders.

---

## 1. Constraints that shape the design

| Fact | Consequence |
|---|---|
| A WoW addon runs in a Lua sandbox with **no network access**. It reads files only at login or `/reload`, and it writes its SavedVariables to disk only at logout, `/reload` or disconnect. | AHSync does all the HTTP work and the calculations. The two talk through files: the addon's SavedVariables go to AHSync, and generated `MarketIntelligence.lua` and `PortfolioPlan.lua` come back to the addon. |
| WoW: Forever has **no official Blizzard AH API**. auctionhouse.fyi (AHF) prices come from players scanning in game, and so do AHledger's (§2.3). | Data is only as fresh as someone's last scan, so AHSync takes every source it can get and keeps the most granular observation of each item and hour (§2.2). Every price keeps its observation time, and results show their age and a confidence level. |
| AHF CDN retention: public **hour candles 7 days**, public **day candles 7 days**. Premium adds 14 days of hours and 90 days of days. | We build our own 90-day hourly history. See §2.1. |
| Addons **cannot buy or post automatically**. Each action needs a hardware click. | We recommend and pre-fill searches. You click. |
| Forever client: folder `_classic_beta_` (may be renamed at the Nov 4 launch), `## Interface: 16001`, client build 1.60.x. | The WoW path is configurable, and every game-API call goes through a thin adapter. |

## 2. Data sources

### 2.1 auctionhouse.fyi API v1

- Base: `https://auctionhouse.fyi/api/v1`. CDN: `https://cdn.auctionhouse.fyi/` (from `GET /config`).
- Auth is optional: `Authorization: Bearer ak_…`. The key stays in local config and never goes into logs or URLs.
- Units: price is integer **copper per unit**, times are **Unix ms**, hour = `floor(ms/3600000)`, day = `floor(ms/86400000)`. A `null` price means nothing listed, never zero.
- Market id: `forever.<normal|pvp|rp>.<alliance|horde>.us`. Configurable as a list; the default is **`forever.pvp.horde.us`**.
- Item **key**: `item + 2147483648 × v`, where `v` encodes the random suffix. Base item id = `key mod 2147483648`.
- **Item names.** `/items` names base items in `items` and suffix keys in `variants`, but `variants` is incomplete (Grunt's Bracers 15507 has none listed, yet its variant 61486751825043 is on the AH). Its `suffixes` table maps a suffix id to its name (14316 → "of Defense"), and every listed variant is named `<base name> <suffix name>` (all 523 checked, 2026-10-19). `tools/gen_bonus_suffixes.py` writes that table to `ahsync/data/bonus_suffixes.json` (`names`) with the bonus table (`suffixes`); 52 names in client 1.60.1.70009.
- `429` comes with `Retry-After`, and we honour it. `403 {feature}` means our tier lacks it, so we skip that feature.

| Purpose | Call | Without key | With Premium |
|---|---|---|---|
| Capabilities | `GET /me` | `historyDays 7`, 60 requests/min | `historyDays 90`, + `prices.live`, `depth`, `books` |
| File index | `GET /ah/:market/files` → `sealed.h1`, `d1`, `caps.*` | ✔ | ✔ with capability URLs |
| Hour candles | CDN `candles/v1/h1/<m>/<hour>.json.gz` | last 7 days | + days 8–14 via `h1c` |
| Day candles | CDN `candles/v1/d1/<m>/<day>.json.gz` (7 days per file) | ✔ | `d1c` = 90 days in one file |
| Current prices | `GET /ah/:market/snapshot` (as of the top of the hour) | ✔ | live table `GET /ah/:market?since=` |
| Volume (listed/sold/expired per day) | `caps.volume` | ✗ (estimated, §3.3.1) | ✔ |
| Item metadata | `GET /items` (name, quality, ilvl, class/subclass, vendor buy/sell, stack, variants) | ✔ | ✔ |

Rules:

- **Only request files we were told exist** (hours ≤ `sealed.h1`). A guessed future file caches a 404 at the edge.
- **Building 90 days without Premium.** Public hour files cover 7 days, so running AHSync at least once every **6 days** misses nothing. Over time the local DB fills to the full 90 days. AHSync warns when the gap since the last run gets close to 7 days. Premium only speeds up the first fill and adds real volume data and live prices. Premium is also earned for free by uploading scans with the AHF addon (7 days per credited scan).
- **A gap of more than 7 days without Premium loses those hours for good.** Public day candles are also kept only 7 days, so nothing covers them. AHSync reports the lost range on the Status page.
- **Characters map to markets** as `forever.<ruleset>.<faction>.us`. The faction comes from the character. WoW reports the realm's name but not its ruleset, so the ruleset comes from the config: the realm's `[realms]` entry (§3.6) when there is one, otherwise the only market in `markets` for that faction. Only when you sync several markets for one faction (or none) does a realm need a `[realms]` entry; until then its characters have no market and Status says how to fix it (S12, D34). AHF has no neutral-AH market, so the neutral AH is not planned in v1 (§9).

Verified 2026-10-16, without a key: `/me`, `/files`, the snapshot, public h1 and d1 files, and `/items` all work.

**Verified 2026-10-16 with our key.** The key gives the Premium feature set through the **contributor** role, not a paid plan:

| | Without key | With our key (roles `free`, `contributor`) |
|---|---|---|
| `historyDays` | 7 | **90** |
| `apiPerMinute` | 60 | **600** |
| `feedMinutes` | 60 | 1 |
| Features | `prices.snapshot`, `candles.daily`, `candles.hourly` | adds `prices.live`, `candles.minute`, `books`, `depth`, `signals`, `live`, watchlist, alerts and boards, plus the API and upload features |
| `/files` caps | — | `d1`, `h1`, `m1`, `volume` (plus top-level `books` and `live`) |

- **Contributor access expires.** `/me` returns `contributor.until`, which was 2026-10-23 (7 days out), with `scans7d` = 4. Every credited scan uploaded with the AHF addon extends it.
- **So AHSync must re-check `/me` on every run** and degrade cleanly when Premium features disappear (§2.1 `403` rule).
- **The Status page shows the expiry**: "Premium via contributor until 23 Oct · upload scans to keep it". It warns 2 days before.
- **Minute candles (`m1`, `candles.minute`)** are available but not used in v1.

**Response formats** (recorded 2026-10-16 into `tests/fixtures/ahf/`; AHF publishes no API docs, so the URL patterns come from its site's `cdnrules` script):

| File | URL (relative to the CDN) | Rows |
|---|---|---|
| Hour candles | `candles/v1/h1/<m>/<hour>.json.gz`; Premium: `candles/v1/h1c/<m>/<caps.h1[day]>/<hour>.json.gz` (token per day, file per hour) | `items: [[key, [[t, open, high, low, close, qty], …]], …]` |
| Day candles | `candles/v1/d1/<m>/<day>.json.gz` (7 days); Premium: `caps.d1`, the `d1c` file (90 days) | same as hour candles, with day `t` |
| Snapshot | `snap/v1/<m>/latest.json` → `{hour, at, path}`; then `path` | `rows: [[key, price, qty, scanAt], …]` |
| Order book | `files.books[].url` (`books/v1/<m>/<at>-<token>.json.gz`), with `complete` | `entries: [[key, price, stack, band, count], …]`, with bands shifted on Forever (verified 2026-10-17): AuctionHouseFYI's addon reads Forever's time left as retail's 0–3 and uploads it + 1, but Forever returns 1–4, so short arrives as 2, medium as 3, long as 4 and very long as 0, and band 1 never appears. A book without band 1 is mapped back to WoW's codes; one with band 1 is read as WoW's codes, with 0 as very long, the conservative choice for §3.3.2 (V28); `bidEntries` has 8 fields |
| Volume | `caps.volume` | `items` (empty for this market so far, so the row format is still unknown) |
| Items | `GET /items` | `items: {id: {n, q, il, c, sc, sell, buy, st, …}}`, `variants: {key: {n, q, …}}` for suffix keys, plus `suffixes`, `bonusLists`, `bonusTrees` |

- A missing hour file is a 404, which is why only listed files are requested (§2.1 rules).
- Capability tokens are secrets: the recorder replaces them with `cap-…` placeholders.
- `/me` contains the account's id and email; the recorder replaces those too.

**The app works fully without Premium.** Each Premium feature only improves something that already has a free fallback:

| Premium feature | Used for | Without it |
|---|---|---|
| `volume` / `depth` | real daily listed / sold / expired, the main input to the sell probability | our own estimates (order-book diffs, mail, quantity drops; §3.3.1) |
| `historyDays 90` (`h1c`, `d1c`) | filling the 90-day history quickly, and recovering gaps up to 14 days | AHSync builds the history itself by running at least every 6 days (§2.1 rules) |
| `prices.live` | prices refreshed every 15 minutes | the hourly snapshot and your own in-game scans |
| `books` | AHF's full order books | your own scans' order books |
| `apiPerMinute 600` | faster catch-up | 60 per minute: the first sync is slower, but nothing is lost |

The choice is made per run from `/me`, and per item and day for volume (§3.3.1). Losing Premium therefore changes the quality of the estimates, never whether the app works. Scenarios S11 and V14–V17 cover it.

**Coverage warning (beta).** `forever.pvp.horde.us` had 8,929 items, all from **one scan about 7 days old**, with 1 contributor in 7 days. Its public h1 files were empty. Our own scans (§2.2) are therefore a first-class source, and AHledger (§2.3) a second remote one.

### 2.2 The AHProfit addon's SavedVariables

AHSync reads `WTF/Account/*/SavedVariables/AHProfit.lua`. It watches the file and re-reads it whenever WoW rewrites it (on logout or `/reload`). The addon records:

| Data | Captured when |
|---|---|
| **Characters**: id `Name-Realm`, name, realm, faction, class, level, gold | login, money change |
| **Professions** and skill level per character | login, skill change |
| **Recipe definitions per profession** (account-wide): product, yield min/max, reagents with counts, vendor-sold reagents, skill needed, cooldown | profession window opened (once per profession is enough) |
| **Known recipes per character**: spell ids | profession window opened on that character |
| **Inventory per character**: bags, bank, mail, each with a **bound** flag | bag/bank/mail events |
| **Own active auctions**: item, unit price, stack, time-left band | AH events |
| **Own AH scans** (full or partial): scan time, kind, and the order book as rows `[key, unitPrice, stack, band, count]` | AH open + scan button / auto |
| **Posts**: character, item, stack, unit price, duration, deposit, time | a hook on the post call |
| **Purchases**: character, item, quantity, unit price, source (AH or vendor), time | AH: a hook on the buy call (`hooksecurefunc`, which only observes), confirmed by the "auction won" message and mail. Vendor: a hook on the merchant buy call |
| **Crafts**: character, recipe, count, time | spellcast success + bag change |
| **Gathering loot** (built, M17: `Capture/Gather.lua`): character, the loot window's items and quantities, what opened it (mining, herbalism, skinning, fishing or a corpse), time, the login it belongs to (§3.4E.3) | a loot window opened after a gathering or fishing cast, or on a corpse |
| **Mail to your own characters**: from, to, item, quantity, time (matches plan `mail` actions) | a hook on the send-mail call |
| **Auction mail**: sold / expired / cancelled / won, item, quantity, price (from the invoice), time | live system messages; mailbox opened |
| **Item rules** set in game (blacklist, §3.4.2) | a right-click on a row |
| **Reserves**: per character, items you keep for yourself, all or at least N units (§3.4.3) | a right-click on a row: Keep all, Keep at least…, Sell normally |

**You never have to report anything by hand.** The inventory snapshot (bags, bank, mail, own auctions) is the source of truth for *what* you own, so a missed event can't make the app think you own something you don't. Events only say *where* items came from and *what they cost*. Items from outside the AH (farmed, traded, mailed between characters) have no recorded cost and are valued at their opportunity cost. Auctions that sell or expire while you're offline are learned at your next mailbox visit.

The app merges own scans into the price tables as source `local`, next to every remote source's data (AHF, AHledger §2.3, a future provider). **One close per (item, hour):** when several sources have a close for the same item and hour, statistics use exactly one, so a scan that reached two sources (your scan uploaded to AHF, the same server answer read by AHledger's addon, §2.3) is never counted twice. **The most granular observation wins** (**built**, M19: `collect/provider.py` `SOURCE_KINDS`, `KIND_ORDER`, `source_kind`, `source_precedence`; `history.reads._best_per_time`, SRC3, SRC9):

1. **By kind first.** Each source's rows have a kind, derived from their `src` in one table, `collect/provider.py` `SOURCE_KINDS = {"local": "book", "ahf": "hourly", "ahledger": "table", "ahledger_h1": "hourly"}`: `book` (a close taken from a complete order book: your scans) beats `hourly` (per-item hourly observations: AHF's h1 candles, AHledger's hourly history buckets) beats `table` (a per-item aggregate table: AHledger's price table). A source not in `SOURCE_KINDS` counts as `hourly`, so SRC3's providers keep their order; a new provider declares its kind there when it is added. The kind is not stored with the row: the schema stays as it is.
2. **Then by rank** within a kind: `local` always first, then the market's configured `rank` (§3.6; `ahledger_h1` ranks with `ahledger`), then unranked sources by name (SRC3).

So your scan's 45c beats AHF's 50c for the same hour, AHF's hourly close beats AHledger's hourly bucket by rank, AHledger's hourly bucket beats its own price table by kind, and the table fills an hour nobody else observed (SRC9). Analysis never names a source: it reads the one close per (item, hour) the reader hands it. The **current price** keeps its own rule, newest observation wins and `local` on ties (S7), and **sales volume** is untouched: AHledger's sales estimate is stored, never used (§2.3).

Reading rules:

- AHSync re-reads the file only after its modification time has been unchanged for 2 seconds, so it never parses a half-written file.
- Every read is logged at INFO: what was stored and whose `ingestedUntil` moved, or why the file wasn't read (S17, §5.2 rule 6).
- The file carries `formatVersion`, and every event carries a unique `event_id`, so ingest is idempotent. A file that won't parse is retried from WoW's `AHProfit.lua.bak` (§5.2).
- **Pruning handshake.** Order books, events and mail would make SavedVariables grow without limit, which slows login and logout. AHSync writes `ingestedUntil` into `PortfolioPlan.lua` (§3.5) as a **map from character id to time** (ms): for each character, the newest event or scan time stored for that character. On load, the addon deletes each character's scans, posts, purchases, crafts, gather events and mail rows at or before that character's value.
  - **Per character, because `PortfolioPlan.lua` is shared by every account** on the machine: one global time could pass events of another account whose file couldn't be read.
  - **Never past a row that was set aside.** When any of a character's rows (a scan or an event) was not stored, for example a scan from a realm missing in `[realms]` or a row the parser rejected, that character's value stops 1 s before the oldest such row. The addon then keeps it, and it is ingested once the problem is fixed. It keeps unconfirmed attempts younger than 1 h (§5.2), so a confirmation that arrives later still reaches AHSync, which upserts the event by its id.

#### 2.2.1 SavedVariables layout (format version 1)

The addon has one account-wide global, `AHProfitDB` (`## SavedVariables: AHProfitDB`). This layout is the contract between `Capture/*`, which writes it, and `state/savedvars.py`, which reads it. A1 round-trips it. A change to it bumps `formatVersion` and adds a migration on both sides (FM13, FM23).

Field rules:
- Money is integer copper; prices are per unit.
- Times are UTC Unix ms. WoW's clock (`GetServerTime()`, through the Adapter) counts whole seconds, so every time is seconds × 1000.
- `charId` is `Name-Realm`. The market comes from the character's faction and the config's `[realms]` table (§2.1), so it isn't stored.
- `key` is the AHF item key (§2.1). `Adapter.lua` computes it from the item link. A random suffix comes from the link's bonus ID: `v = 2 × suffix id`, with the suffix id looked up in AHF's bonus table (`Data/BonusSuffixes.lua`, §6 item 11). An item whose suffix can't be worked out (a bonus ID not in the table, or a Classic-style suffix field) is written as **`key = −itemId`**: some suffix variant of that item, with `v` unknown. AHSync stores such rows but leaves them out of every valuation and recommendation, so a suffixed green is never priced at its base item's price.
- **Display names** (D60): one function names a key on every page, `engine.views.item_name` (the history reader's `items()` returns an `ItemCatalog` carrying the suffix names, `history.reads.suffix_names_of`). A key in `items` uses its own name; a suffix key missing from it is named from its base item plus the suffix table (`engine.item_key.variant_name`): 61486751825043 with only 15507 in `items` reads "Grunt's Bracers of Defense", "Grunt's Bracers (variant)" when the suffix has no known name; a negative key reads "<base> (unknown suffix)"; an item with no base row shows its key. Quality comes from the base item too. Only names change: a variant missing from `items` still has no metadata for valuation (class, ilvl, vendor price). Neither addon file carries item names (the addon names items from the game), so the exports are unchanged.
- **Unknown is left out, never 0.** A field the client can't tell us (a recipe's `skill` or `cooldown` on Classic APIs) is omitted, and AHSync reads it as unknown (None). `cooldown = 0` means "no cooldown".
- **A snapshot is written only when it is complete.** Bags, bank, mail and professions each replace the previous value, so a partial read would look like lost items. A location is skipped, and the old value kept, when an occupied slot has no item link yet (not cached), when the mailbox has loaded fewer mails than it holds, or when a collapsed Skills header hides profession lines.
- `band` is WoW's time-left code: 1 short, 2 medium, 3 long, 4 very long.
- **Bulk rows are compact strings:** rows are separated by `;` and fields by `,`, in the order listed. Everything else is a plain table.
- **Event ids** are `charId:timeMs:seq`. `seq` restarts at 0 each second for each character. Only one character is logged in at a time, so ids are unique.
- Profession names are the client's English names; only enUS clients are supported in v1.

```lua
AHProfitDB = {
  formatVersion = 1,
  addonVersion = "0.1.0",
  refreshRequestedAt = nil,                    -- ms; set by /ahp refresh (§3.1.1)
  characters = {
    ["Amaya-Grommash"] = { name = "Amaya", realm = "Grommash", faction = "Horde",
                           class = "MAGE", level = 60, gold = 1234567, updated = 1760000000000,
                           login = 1759990000000 },   -- ms of the initial login, kept across /reload (M17, §3.4E.4)
  },
  professions = {                              -- per character
    ["Amaya-Grommash"] = { Enchanting = { skill = 225, max = 300 } },
  },
  recipes = {                                  -- account-wide; profession -> spell id -> definition
    Blacksmithing = {
      [2663] = { product = 2857, yieldMin = 1, yieldMax = 1, skill = 1, cooldown = 0,  -- cooldown in s
                 reagents = "2840,2" },        -- "key,count;key,count"; skill/cooldown omitted when unknown
    },
  },
  vendorReagents = "2320;2321",                -- keys a merchant was seen selling (§3.4B)
  knownRecipes = { ["Brugan-Grommash"] = "2662;2663" },        -- spell ids, ascending
  inventory = {                                -- per character; each location replaced whole, with its own time
    ["Amaya-Grommash"] = { bags = "2840,20,0;6948,1,1", bagsAt = 1760000000000,  -- "key,qty,bound(0/1)"
                           bank = nil, bankAt = nil,           -- nil until first visited; "" = visited, empty
                           mail = nil, mailAt = nil },
  },
  itemNames = { [6948] = "1,Hearthstone" },    -- key -> "quality,name" of every item seen in an inventory, read
                                               -- from its link; names items AHF's list leaves out, e.g. soulbound (S13)
  ownAuctions = {                              -- latest snapshot per character, replaced whole
    ["Amaya-Grommash"] = { at = 1760000000000, rows = "2857,4100,5,3,1",  -- "key,price,stack,band,count"
                           ends = "2857,5,1760039600000,4100" },  -- "key,units,end ms,price" per auction; addon only (A41)
                                               -- an auction without a numeric timeLeftSeconds has no `ends` row and
                                               -- its band from `timeLeft` (retail's enum + 1, 0 = unknown) (A70)
  },
  scans = {                                    -- oldest first; at most 12 un-ingested full scans (§5.1)
    { id = "Amaya-Grommash:1760000000000:0", char = "Amaya-Grommash", at = 1760000000000,
      kind = "full", coverage = 1.0,           -- kind "full" | "partial"; coverage 0..1
      rows = "2589,38,20,3,4;2589,45,20,4,1" },  -- "key,price,stack,band,count"
  },
  events = {                                   -- oldest first; fields per type below
    { id = "Amaya-Grommash:1760000000000:1", type = "post", char = "Amaya-Grommash",
      at = 1760000000000, key = 2857, stack = 5, price = 4100, durationH = 24, deposit = 60,
      confirmed = true },
  },
  rules = { [2589] = { rule = "no_buy", setAt = 1760000000000 } },   -- set in game; "none" = cleared (§3.4.2)
  reserves = { ["Amaya-Grommash"] = "2589,10;4306,-1" },  -- "key,qty" kept for yourself; qty -1 = all (§3.4.3)
  mailSeen = { ["sold:2589:20:48:1760000000000"] = 1762678400000 },  -- auction mails already turned into events (A48)
}
```

| Event `type` | Fields besides `id`, `char`, `at` | Captured by |
|---|---|---|
| `post` | `key`, `stack`, `price`, `durationH`, `deposit`, `confirmed` | `Capture/Posts` |
| `purchase` | `key`, `qty`, `price`, `source` (`ah` / `vendor`), `confirmed` | `Capture/Purchases` |
| `craft` | `spell`, `count` | `Capture/Crafts` |
| `mail_sent` | `to` (charId), `key`, `qty`, `confirmed` (since A75; missing counts as confirmed) | `Capture/Mail` |
| `auction_mail` | `outcome` (`sold` / `expired` / `cancelled` / `won`), `key`, `qty`, `price` (sold and won only) | `Capture/Mail` |
| `skip` | `action` (action id from `PortfolioPlan.lua`) | the row menu's "Mark as skipped" |
| `payout` | `key`, `qty`, `money` (copper taken from the mail), `deposit` (the invoice's, copper; optional) | `Capture/Mail`: an auction's gold collected from the mailbox, so *posted*, *sold* and *paid out* are three separate events |
| `disenchant` | `key` (the item), `results` (`"key,qty;..."`) | `Capture/Crafts`: a disenchant and what it gave |
| `gather` (built, M17) | `source` (`mining` / `herbalism` / `skinning` / `fishing` / `monster`), `login` (ms: the character's initial login, kept across `/reload`), `loot` (`"key,qty;..."`, summed per item, by key, coin left out) | `Capture/Gather`: one loot window of farming loot (§3.4E.3) |

- **`diagnostics`** (written by `Core/Diagnostics.lua` at `PLAYER_LOGOUT`, which also fires on `/reload`): `{ at, client, apis, errors, skillLines, merchant, tradeSkill, namespaces, professions, setupError, scan, traces, ownAuctions }`. It holds the client build, which of the APIs the addon depends on exist (`"name=type;…"`), the handler errors caught this session (at most 10), the raw Skills-tab rows, the last merchant's raw items, the last trade skill window's raw state, the function names inside the modern API namespaces (`"C_X=FnA FnB;…"`), the raw `GetProfessions` slots, any database setup error, and the last full-scan read (`"calls=..|n=..|skipped=..|pet=..|count=..|buyout=..|band=..|link=..|byId=..|left=1:..,2:..|steps=..|maxStepMs=..|readMs=..|sortMs=..|encodeMs=..|saveMs=..|notifyMs=..|kind=..|rows=..|dropped=.."`: skips per reason, auctions keyed by item id, the raw time-left histogram, the slowest step per phase, and the time the redraws after a complete scan took, which run in a frame of their own, A51, §4.2), and a trace of the session's last 10 capture decisions for the captures that hook a click (`traces`, `";"`-joined, oldest first: each money take with the mail's money, invoice and item and whether it became a payout, A54; each Disenchant's target, its loot and whether it became an event, A55). `ownAuctions` is `"timeLeftSeconds=<Lua type>"` of the first active auction the last owned-auctions read saw (absent when none was read), so the report can say whether Forever gives each auction's seconds left (A70). It exists so `tools/savedvars_report.py` can explain a missing capture without Lua error popups (§6). AHSync never ingests it, and each session replaces it.
- **Professions and known recipes follow the file.** The addon writes a character's professions whole and only ever adds known recipes. AHSync replaces that character's professions and known recipes with the file's when the file's `characters[id].updated` is at least the stored one; an older file changes nothing. So an unlearned profession disappears, and a relearned one starts again at its new skill.
- `confirmed` starts `false` when the hook fires and turns `true` when the action is confirmed (§5.2). The same row is updated in place.
- **How the addon captures them** (A44–A47; every call UNVERIFIED on Forever and probed in the diagnostics):
  - `post`: hooks that only observe `C_AuctionHouse.PostCommodity(location, duration, quantity, unitPrice)` and `PostItem(location, duration, quantity, bid, buyout)`. The item is read from the bag snapshot taken before the post (the slot is empty when the hook runs); `durationH` maps the duration 1/2/3 to 2/8/24 h, the engine's durations (verified on Forever 2026-10-18); the deposit is `Engine/Fees`' formula, as AHSync computes it. `AUCTION_HOUSE_AUCTION_CREATED` confirms the oldest unconfirmed post.
  - `purchase` on the AH: a commodity purchase uses the unit price the AH quoted (`COMMODITY_PRICE_UPDATED` after `StartCommoditiesPurchase`), is added on `ConfirmCommoditiesPurchase` and confirmed on `COMMODITY_PURCHASE_SUCCEEDED`; a buyout (`PlaceBid` at the buyout, read with `GetAuctionInfoByID`) is confirmed on `AUCTION_HOUSE_PURCHASE_COMPLETED`. A bid below the buyout is not a purchase.
  - `purchase` from a merchant: `BuyMerchantItem(index, quantity)`, at the merchant's price per unit; confirmed at once.
  - `craft`: `UNIT_SPELLCAST_SUCCEEDED` for the player with a spell among the character's known recipes; one event per cast, `count` 1.
  - `disenchant` (A55): `UNIT_SPELLCAST_START` of Disenchant (13262) for the player notes the item in the locked bag slot (the cast's target); `UNIT_SPELLCAST_SUCCEEDED` marks it cast and `INTERRUPTED` / `FAILED` drop it; the next `LOOT_OPENED` reads the loot (coin left out) into `results`, summed per item and ordered by key. AHSync passes the item's average cost to the results, split by their market value (each result's units × its current lowest price; T20), or by units when a result has no price (T18), and matches the event to a planned DE action like a craft (T19).
  - `gather` (**built M17**: `Capture/Gather.lua`, with `Adapter.LootCast`, `Adapter.LootSourceKind` and `Adapter.ReadLoot`; A85–A91, FM42; §3.4E.3): `UNIT_SPELLCAST_SUCCEEDED` (or `UNIT_SPELLCAST_CHANNEL_START`, for Fishing's channel) of Mining, Herb Gathering, Skinning or Fishing for the player notes the source, matched by the spell's name (`GetSpellInfo`), so every rank counts; the next `LOOT_OPENED` reads every loot slot (coin left out) into `loot`, tagged with it. The cast owns the next window for 10 s (Fishing: 35 s, the bobber is clicked within the channel) or until `LOOT_CLOSED`. A `LOOT_OPENED` whose loot source is a creature (`GetLootSourceInfo`, its GUID's type) with no such cast pending is `monster`. A loot source that contradicts the pending cast (a corpse after a Mining, Herb Gathering or Fishing cast, an object after Skinning) means the cast's own window never opened: the cast is dropped and the window is judged without it. A loot window after Disenchant stays the `disenchant` event (A55); a window after another loot-opening cast (Pick Pocket, Opening) records nothing, and so does any window whose source is not a creature (an object, a container item) with no gathering cast pending. A combat spell is no loot-opening cast, so a corpse looted after it is still `monster`. When the client has no `GetLootSourceInfo`, every source reads unknown: gathering casts are still recorded, no corpse ever is (no event rather than a wrong one). One event per window: a second `LOOT_OPENED` before `LOOT_CLOSED` adds none. `login` is the time of the character's initial login, kept in `characters[id].login`: the first `PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD` of a UI session sets it when `isInitialLogin` is true and keeps it after a `/reload` (`isReloadingUi`); a client without the flag keeps a stored login, so a relog continues it and only the gap ends a session; an event before any `PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD` takes now, kept from then on. So AHSync can tell a logout apart from a pause (§3.4E.4). An unreadable window (no slots, or an error, caught and kept among the diagnostics' `errors`) records nothing (FM42). UNVERIFIED on Forever: the spell names, `GetSpellInfo`, `GetLootSourceInfo`, `GetLootSlotInfo`, `GetLootSlotLink`, `isInitialLogin`, `UNIT_SPELLCAST_CHANNEL_START` for Fishing, the 10 s and 35 s windows; the diagnostics probe `GetSpellInfo` and `GetLootSourceInfo` and trace each loot decision like A55's ("gather mining loot 2 -> gather", "gather mining loot error -> none", "loot after 'Pick Pocket' -> none"; a Disenchant's window is traced by A55 only). `/ahp perf` prints the last and the slowest loot window's handling time.
  - `auction_mail` (A48): read from the mailbox when it shows (`MAIL_INBOX_UPDATE`). A seller invoice (`GetInboxInvoiceInfo`) is `sold` at its sale price ÷ count, a buyer invoice or "Auction won: …" `won`, and the subjects "Auction expired: …" and "Auction cancelled: …" (the client's `AUCTION_EXPIRED_MAIL_SUBJECT` and `AUCTION_REMOVED_MAIL_SUBJECT`) `expired` and `cancelled`, keyed by the attachment's link, or, for a sold invoice (which has no attachment), by the item's name in `itemNames`. Each mail becomes one event: a fingerprint (outcome, item, quantity, price and its send time, from the days left of a 30-day auction mail) is kept in `mailSeen` until 31 days after it was sent. The event's `at` is when the mail was first seen, so the pruning handshake never drops it before AHSync reads it; `sentAt` carries the send time.
  - `mail_sent` (A75): `MAIL_SEND_INFO_UPDATE` keeps a cache of the send-mail attachment slots (`GetSendMailItem(i)` count, `GetSendMailItemLink(i)` key, i = 1..`ATTACHMENTS_MAX_SEND`); a hook that only observes `SendMail(recipient, subject, body)` runs right after the call, while the cache still holds the attachments, and writes one unconfirmed event per attached item, `to` = the recipient capitalised + "-" + the sender's realm (a "Name-Realm" recipient on another realm, a send without attachments or without a name writes nothing). `MAIL_SEND_SUCCESS` confirms the oldest unsettled send's events, `MAIL_FAILED` removes them. AHSync stores `confirmed` like posts and purchases (a later confirmed copy upgrades the row); only confirmed sends match a plan mail (§3.4.1), count as mail on its way (§3.4B) and show in History. UNVERIFIED on Forever: `SendMail`, `GetSendMailItem`, `GetSendMailItemLink`, `ATTACHMENTS_MAX_SEND` and the three events; the diagnostics probe the names.
  - `payout` (A54, T17): a hook that only observes `TakeInboxMoney(index)` and `AutoLootMailItem(index)` reads the mail before the take lands (its header still shows the money): a seller invoice with money becomes one `payout` of that money, with the deposit the invoice reports, keyed like a sold mail, deduplicated by a `paid:` fingerprint in `mailSeen`. AHSync links each payout to the oldest sold mail of the same character, item and quantity not yet paid, and realizes its profit once: money − units × cost basis − the deposit (the invoice's; without one, the post's for those units), because the money includes the deposit coming back (§3.4.1).
- Untracked changes (FM21) are not events. AHSync derives them by comparing consecutive inventory snapshots with the events between them (**built**, D51: `engine/untracked`, `state/untracked`): when a file brings a newer bag snapshot, each item's change in bags, bank and mail together, less what vendor buys, crafts and disenchants since the earlier one explain, is stored in `untracked` and listed in History. Items moved by AH buys, posts, auction mail or mail between your characters in that time are left out, because those places are captured at different moments. They compare the bag snapshots AHSync actually saw, so a file read after a newer one (its bag snapshot is skipped) adds none; FI2 holds for every other table.

### 2.3 AHledger

**Built: M19 (the price table), M21 (the history backfill).** AHledger (`https://ahledger.com`) is a second community price source for WoW: Forever, built from in-game scans like AHF's. AHSync reads its public API as source `ahledger` (the price table) and `ahledger_h1` (the per-item history backfill), next to AHF and your scans. It is additive: nothing but the data-source layer changes, and a market with no AHledger rows (the W1 world) gives a byte-identical whole-plan snapshot (WP1).

- Base: `https://api.ahledger.com`. Read only. **No key needed**: 300 requests a minute per address, `429` with `Retry-After` past it. A key (`ahlk_…`, sent as a bearer token or `x-ahl-api-key`) only lifts that limit; AHSync never needs one. If one is configured later it follows the AHF key rules (an environment variable, never in logs, URLs, exceptions, fixtures or dashboard responses).
- **Every response carries `Cache-Control`, and AHSync honours it**: polling faster returns the same bytes. Breaking changes keep the old version for 30 days.
- Market ids use dots and are **exactly AHSync's** (`forever.pvp.horde.us`); the price table's header and the tooltip endpoint write them with slashes (`forever/pvp/horde/us`). `[sources] addresses.ahledger` maps a market that AHledger names differently (§3.6).
- Item ids are **Blizzard's**, which are AHSync's keys for unsuffixed items. A suffix key (§2.1 key) never comes from AHledger: its rows are always the base item.
- Prices are integer **copper**; an empty field or `null` means unknown, never zero.
- AHledger's addon scans with `C_AuctionHouse.ReplicateItems`, the same server answer our addon listens for (§4.2), so its table and our scans can be the same observation: the one-close rule (§2.2) keeps only one. **AHledger's SavedVariables are not read** (the user's decision, 2026-10-22: they would duplicate our own scans); reading them is a later option.

| Purpose | Call | Returns | Cached | Used |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Markets | `GET /v1/markets` | every market: id, game, ruleset, faction, region, label | 5 min | checks the market exists (SRC6) |
| Price table | `GET /v1/pricetable/{market}` | every priced item as plain text, the addon's format (below), gzip-compressed when asked (SRC17); sends an `ETag`, and an unchanged table answers `304` | 1 min | the live source `ahledger`, every 30 min (M19) |
| Item history | `GET /v1/prices/{market}/{itemId}?range=24h\|7d\|30d\|90d` | the item's `latest` price and its `series` (below), plus `sales.estimatedPerDay` (null until enough hours of scans back it) | 1 min | the backfill, `7d` and `90d` (M21) |
| Tooltip | `GET /v1/tooltip/{market}?items=2589,2592` | latest price, 7 and 30 day medians and salesPerDay for up to 100 items at once; the market comes back with slashes | 1 min | not used |
| Item | `GET /v1/items/{itemId}?game=forever` | name, quality, class, level, requirement, vendor value, icon | 5 min | not used (`/items` from AHF names items, §2.1) |
| Economy | `GET /v1/economy/{market}` | market size and daily history, including `scans` per day | 10 min | not used |
| Market tools | `/v1/deals`, `/v1/crafting`, `/v1/structure`, `/v1/signals`, `/v1/disenchant` | AHledger's own analyses, 60 requests a minute each (busy: `503` + `Retry-After`) | 10–15 min | never: AHSync computes its own |

**Formats** (from AHledger's developer page, pasted 2026-11-01, and live calls on 2026-10-22; recorded into `tests/fixtures/ahledger/` by `tools/record_fixtures.py`, trimmed to tens of rows):

- **Price table**: a header line `AHL1|forever/pvp/horde/us|1793000000|18422` (format `AHL1`, the market with slashes, `updated` in Unix **seconds**, the row count), then rows `itemId:median:minBuyout:quantity:median7d:median30d:low30d:high30d:soldPerDayx10` joined by `;`. An empty field means unknown. `soldPerDayx10` is tenths: "3600 is 360 a day, 4 is 0.4". On 2026-10-22 our market's table had 2,207 rows, about 92 KB, updated 26 h before.
- **Item history** (`/v1/prices/forever.pvp.horde.us/2589?range=7d`): `{"market", "item": {"id", "name", "quality"}, "latest": {"observedAt": "2026-11-05T14:02:11.000Z", "source": "Addon scan", "minBuyout": 18, "median": 20, "quantity": 3420, "auctions": 212, "median7d": 22, "median30d": 21, "median90d": null}, "range": "7d", "series": [{"t": "2026-11-04T14:00:00.000Z", "min": 17, "median": 21, "p25": 19, "p75": 24, "quantity": 3100, "auctions": 190}, …]}`. `latest` is null until the market has been scanned. `series` holds **hourly** buckets for `7d` and **daily** buckets for `30d` and `90d`; for `24h` the endpoint table says one point per full scan and the prose says hourly buckets (unused by AHSync, so it doesn't matter).
- Market tool responses carry `marketStatus`, `pricesAsOf` (the newest **full** scan; a partial scan never sets it) and an `attribution` block (`"Prices from AHledger"` with its URL); AHSync doesn't call them.

**Attribution** (AHledger's terms: free for personal or commercial use on two conditions). (1) **Credit AHledger with a link to ahledger.com wherever its data is shown**: AHSync names the source "AHledger", linked to `https://ahledger.com`, on the Status page (its line on the market card and its row in the Data coverage card, §3.5), wherever a page names the source of a shown price (the item detail's source mix, "seen 26 h ago (AHledger)"), and in the top bar's "numbers from" pill when `primary = "ahledger"`, the default (§3.6). Campaign tags AHledger puts on links in its responses are kept. (2) **Never present the data as our own scan, and never resell it as a feed**: AHledger's rows keep `src` `ahledger` / `ahledger_h1` and are labelled as AHledger's, never as "your scan"; AHSync re-publishes nothing. The addon shows no AHledger data of its own: closes are blended into AHSync's statistics, and the dashboard's source labels carry the credit.

**The price table: the live source** (M19, `collect/ahledger.py`). While AHSync runs it polls each market's table every 30 minutes (the steady state, §3.1) with `If-None-Match: <the last ETag>`:
- `304` writes nothing (SRC8).
- A changed table is parsed and checked (header `AHL1`, the market, a whole `updated`, the row count; a bad header writes nothing, FM44; a bad row is skipped, FM45), then written in one transaction, every write an idempotent upsert:
  - `current` (src `ahledger`): price = `minBuyout`, qty = `quantity`, at = `updated × 1000`; a row without `minBuyout` writes none.
  - one `candles_h1` close per item (src `ahledger`) at the hour `floor(updated ÷ 3600)` (stored as that hour's start in ms, like every hour row): close = `minBuyout`, qty = `quantity`, open, high and low NULL.
  - `ahledger_rows` (§3.2): its `median`, `median7d`, `median30d`, `low30d`, `high30d` and `soldPerDayx10` as they came. **Stored, used nowhere** (the user, 2026-10-22: "only stored for now, use our own calculation"): analysis reads its own statistics from the closes (SRC11).
  - `observed_hours` (§3.2): the hour of `updated`, for the Data coverage card, only when the table has a priced row. AHledger stamps an empty table (a market nobody has scanned) with its publish time, so it observed nothing (SRC19).
  - `source_state` (§3.2): the ETag, `updated` and the fetch time, so a restart sends the right `If-None-Match`.
- Volume is untouched: AHledger's `soldPerDayx10` is never a sales source (§3.3.1).

**The history backfill** (**built**, M21: `collect/ahledger.py` `parse_prices`, `collect/ahledger_backfill.py` `AhledgerBackfill`, the scheduler's `ahledger_backfill` hook every 5 s, one slice of 10 keys (about 4 s of paced requests) per tick; after a `Retry-After` over 300 s or an unreachable AHledger it pauses an hour and resumes from the checkpoint; while it runs, the Status line "AHledger backfill: 1,200 of 2,207 items" (info, with the link), "· resumes at the next run" after a pause, gone when done; on only with `ahledger_backfill = true`, the default, §3.6). On the first run, and after a gap, AHSync fills the hours it is missing from AHledger's per-item history:
- **What.** For every key in the market's latest price table, `GET /v1/prices/<market>/<key>?range=7d`: each hourly bucket becomes a `candles_h1` close under src `ahledger_h1` (close = `min`, qty = `quantity`, at the bucket's hour `t`), **only for hours with no `local` or `ahf` close for that item**, so it fills gaps and never overwrites (SRC12). Then `range=90d`: each daily bucket becomes a `candles_d1` close under src `ahledger` for days with no d1 close of any source. Every received bucket's hour goes to `observed_hours` under `ahledger_h1`, whether or not its close was written (it observed that hour).
- **When.** At most once per local calendar day per market, after the price table poll, and only when there is something to fill: on the first run (no `ahledger_h1` close yet), or when some hour of the last 7 days has no complete observation from any source in `observed_hours` (a gap). A 2,207-item market is about 15 minutes of requests at 300 a minute (two per item: 4,414 requests, the last one 882.6 s after the first); the collector runs it in the background between hourly cycles, chunked, and it never blocks the dashboard, a sync or a refresh.
- **Limits.** At most 300 requests a minute per address, counted across every AHledger call (SRC13); `Cache-Control` honoured (a response still fresh is not requested again); a `429` waits its `Retry-After` (up to 300 s; a longer one ends the backfill for this run, FM47).
- **Resume.** A checkpoint in `source_state` (the last key done and the range) is committed with each chunk of 100 keys, in ascending key order, so a crash, a stop or a `429` resumes at the next key, never from the start (SRC14).

**Not used, on purpose:** AHledger's aggregates (`median`, `median7d`, `median30d`, `low30d`, `high30d`, `median90d`), its sales estimates (`soldPerDayx10`, `sales.estimatedPerDay`, `salesPerDay`) and its percentiles (`p25`, `p75`): stored where noted, never in a statistic. The tooltip, item, economy and market-tool endpoints are not called.

## 3. AHSync (companion app)

### 3.1 Lifecycle

```
start
 ├─ load config.toml; open SQLite data/ahsync.db
 ├─ GET /me → features; warn if > 6 days since last run (risk of missing hours)
 ├─ refresh item metadata if > 24h old
 ├─ read AHProfit SavedVariables (characters, recipes, inventory, own scans, events, item rules)
 ├─ match events to plan actions and posts to outcomes (§3.4.1)
 ├─ CATCH-UP per market
 │    last = sync_state.last_hour
 │    target = files.sealed.h1
 │    for each missing hour in (last, target]:
 │        ≤ 7 days old   → public h1
 │        7–14 days old  → h1c (if Premium)
 │        older          → d1c day candles if Premium; otherwise lost (§2.1)
 │    day candles: public d1 (7 days) or d1c (90 days); current: snapshot or live table
 │    volume: once a day if Premium
 │    commit and advance sync_state after each hour, so a crash resumes there
 ├─ AHLEDGER per market (price table **built**, M19; backfill **built**, M21; §2.3): price table with If-None-Match (304 → nothing);
 │    then the history backfill in the background, at most once a day, from its checkpoint
 ├─ prune > 90 days
 ├─ FULL REFRESH (§3.1.1) → market intelligence, recommendations, plan, dashboard data, the addon files
 ├─ serve dashboard at http://localhost:8765
 └─ STEADY STATE (mostly idle; see §3.1.1)
      every hour at :06 → store the new h1 hour + snapshot (+ AHF order book if Premium `books`); no recompute
      every 15 min (Premium) → store the live table; no recompute
      every 30 min → AHledger price table per market (§2.3); no recompute; the backfill continues between cycles
      every hour at :00 → STATUS PASS (§3.1.1): late / missed transitions, missed-peak re-plans; no history reads
      SavedVariables changed → re-read → INCREMENTAL UPDATE of the changed items only (§3.1.1)
      first run after `daily_refresh_time`, or "Refresh now" → FULL REFRESH
      daily → d1/volume refresh, prune; weekly VACUUM
```

Every write is an idempotent upsert, so re-running any range is harmless.

#### 3.1.1 Refresh model: heavy work rarely, small updates often

All the heavy lifting happens in AHSync, and only in a **full refresh**. Everything else is a small, targeted update.

**Full refresh** (`engine.refresh_all`). It runs at start, at the first run after `daily_refresh_time` (default 06:00 local), on the dashboard's **Refresh now** button, or when the addon sets a refresh request (`/ahp refresh`, picked up on the next `/reload` or logout). This is the only time AHSync reads through history:
1. **Builds `item_stats`**, one materialized row per item and market with everything §3.3 defines: medians, percentiles, trend, `how`, `sales_how`, `sell_score`, best windows, volume per source, calibration and confidence components. (Tier sell-through and the undercut share are not built yet: their defaults apply, §3.3.2, §6.) It works item by item and streams the candle and book tables in key order, so memory stays flat.
2. **Precomputes each item's options:** sell values and hold values (§3.4), disenchant EV, the crafting options with their sourcing, flip candidates, and the sell-probability curve (below).
3. **Builds the recommendations, the plan, the views and the addon files** (`MarketIntelligence.lua`, `PortfolioPlan.lua`).

**Incremental update** (`app.refresh.replan_all`, IU13). It runs when SavedVariables changes (inventory, posts, purchases, crafts, mail, rules, reserves or a new scan), after the tracking pass and before `PortfolioPlan.lua` is written.
- It re-plans every market from its **published** intelligence and the new character state, and rebuilds the pages: the plan, Today, Week, Flip, Inventory, Craft and Disenchant. No history is read and the intelligence is not rebuilt, so it costs the recommendation half of a full refresh, not the analysis half.
- The plan is re-planned whole rather than item by item, because budgets and each selling window's capacity are shared across items (§3.4C.4); a per-item update would let two items spend the same gold.
- It is published like a full refresh (staged, then swapped in), keeping the live intelligence; a failure leaves the previous plan and pages live.
- A new in-game scan updates `cur`, `curQty`, the book and the current supply for the scanned items only (`analysis.market.with_scan`, IU6): the re-plan applies your scans made after the intelligence was built to the items they priced, in memory, for the plan and the pages; the stored intelligence stays as analysis built it.
- Hourly and live remote data (AHF, AHledger) are **stored but not applied** until the next full refresh, so the numbers stay stable through the day. The exception is items you scanned in game, whose prices update immediately. The dashboard shows "prices newer than numbers · Refresh now" when newer data is waiting: each `/api/views` response carries `prices_newer`, true when the newest stored price of the market's primary source (`[sources]` primary: `"ahledger"` by default (**built**, M19), §3.6; SRC2) is newer than the published intelligence (IU2).

**Status pass** (`recommend/status_pass.py`, run by `app/scheduler.py`). Time passing changes the plan even when no data does, so AHSync runs this once an hour at :00. It runs on precomputed options only, with no history reads and no simulation:
- It moves `pending` actions to `late` or `missed` by the rules in §3.4.1.
- For each post that just became `missed`, it runs the missed-peak re-plan (§3.4.0), which only compares the item's precomputed windows.
- It re-runs the cheap budget allocation and rewrites the views and `PortfolioPlan.lua` if anything changed.

**Sell-probability curve.** The queue simulation (§3.3.2) runs **only in AHSync**, during a full refresh. The chance of a post made now depends on the hour you post it, because the window catches different buyers (P21, P22). So the curve is a grid:
- **Rows:** one for each of the 24 clock hours, starting at the refresh hour. Each row is `P` for a post made at the start of that hour over the posting-now window.
- **Columns:** 8 points of units ahead: 0, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100.
- It is computed at the current price tier, and it carries the inputs needed to blend it.
- Analysis computes the full 24 × 8 grid for **every** item and stores it in the intelligence (§5.0), because it is market-wide and doesn't know your lists. The export then trims it: items on the shortlist keep 24 × 8, and other items, which only need the tooltip, get **one row of 3 points**: the refresh hour's row at 0, 5 and 20 ahead, unchanged (EX19; 24 × 3 until M11's budget fix), to stay within the `MarketIntelligence.lua` budget. Such a **tooltip-only item has an hour-independent chance**: AHSync and the addon use its one row at any hour (`lookup` / `SellProb.Lookup` take the row `curve_row mod rows`), as its `chance1000` already is.
- **Encoding: one character per point.** Each `P` is quantized to 2% steps, `n = floor(P × 50 + 0.5)` (0–50), and written as `string.char(35 + n)`, row by row, in the item's string. Codes 35–85 contain no `"` and no `\`, so no escaping is needed. A 9k-item market then needs about 320 KB of curves (1k shortlisted items × 192 + 8k × 3, EX19), which leaves room in the 2 MB budget for everything else. **AHSync uses the quantized curve too**, not the raw simulation result, so Python and Lua read identical values (IU11).

In each column the units ahead are modelled as one block that stays the whole window, which is conservative: the curve can't know which future competitors expire when. Each value is stored in 2% steps (below).

When a price or the competition changes, AHSync and the addon both pick the row for the current hour and interpolate linearly between the units-ahead points, in O(1) time. More than 24 h after the refresh, they reuse the row for the same clock hour, and the age badge already warns that the data is old. A tooltip-only item's curve has one row, used at every hour (EX19). The full simulation is never run live.

**Idle means idle.**
- Between events AHSync sleeps on timers. It checks the SavedVariables modification time every 5 s, which is a single file stat.
- The dashboard serves precomputed JSON and does no computation per request.

A full refresh builds into staging tables and swaps them in with one transaction, so a failure halfway leaves the previous numbers in place (§5.2).

#### 3.1.2 Performance budgets (AHSync)

| What | Budget |
|---|---|
| CPU when idle | ≈ 0% (timers and one file stat every 5 s) |
| Memory when idle | ≤ 150 MB |
| Memory during a full refresh | ≤ 400 MB peak (streaming, item by item) |
| Full refresh time | ≤ 120 s for one market of about 9k items with 90 days of hourly history (it runs once a day in the background; weekly patterns and the 24 × 8 curve for every item, §5.0) |
| Incremental update | ≤ 1 s (a whole re-plan from the published intelligence: 0.96 s measured on a real 15k-item market, 2026-10-18; applying a full in-game scan's 12k prices adds 0.13 s, IU6). With the portfolio optimizer (M10), measured 2026-10-19 on a copy of the live database: 0.99–1.00 s per re-plan once the published intelligence is parsed (it is kept between re-plans until a refresh publishes another), 1.33 s for the first re-plan after a refresh. SPEC GAP: that is at the budget, not inside it; the rest is the plan's facts for every flip candidate (0.26 s), each flip candidate's first batch for the pool (0.23 s), the crafts (0.13 s), the kept flips (0.10 s), selection (0.04 s), the other pages and the publish (about 0.1 s) |
| Dashboard request | ≤ 50 ms (precomputed JSON, paged at 100 rows) |
| `MarketIntelligence.lua` size | ≤ 2 MB, the candidate universe and dependency graph included (§3.5, FM40); `PortfolioPlan.lua` ≤ 200 KB, the portfolio snapshot and positions included. Measured 2026-10-19 after EX19 (tooltip-only items carry one window of each kind and a one-row curve): the 9k-item bench world (`tools/bench.py`, every item priced within the hour, noisy patterns: the worst case) `MarketIntelligence.lua` 1.74 MB with its 150 candidates, `PortfolioPlan.lua` 34 KB (120 positions); a copy of a live 14k-item database 1.43 MB (11,180 items exported) and 7.4 KB; the WP1 world 15,458 B and 2,788 B. Before EX19 the bench world's file was 2.61 MB. After M14 (positions from the positions table, next actions as text) the bench world's `PortfolioPlan.lua` is 37.2 KB with its 120 positions |
| Portfolio selection (§3.4D.4, built M10) | inside the full refresh's 120 s and the incremental update's 1 s; `tools/bench.py` measures the full refresh with the whole universe of the 9k-item world (§6 item 16). Measured 2026-10-19 with the generation pre-filters and selection bounds (§3.4D.3, §3.4D.4): full refresh 104.6 s and 264 MB peak (before M10: about 102 s, 253 MB); selection and its swap pass take 0.04 s of a re-plan on the live database. SPEC GAP: on the generated world with 14 days of history, where nearly every item is a flip candidate, a re-plan takes 1.6-1.9 s (before M10: 2.3 s) |

Storage rules:
- SQLite runs in WAL mode.
- Every write batch is one transaction.
- Indexes on `(market, key, t)`; candle tables are `WITHOUT ROWID`.
- AHF files are streamed and decompressed one hour at a time, never all at once.
- `item_stats` is replaced atomically, so the dashboard never sees half a refresh.

`tools/bench.py` runs a full refresh on a generated 9k-item world with 90 days of hourly closes, listed units and sales rates for every item (the worst case), in a fresh process without memory tracing, and reports its time and the process peak memory against these budgets. The incremental update is not benchmarked yet.

### 3.2 Storage: SQLite

**Why SQLite rather than Postgres:**

- one user, one machine, one writer process
- a single file, built into Python, nothing to install or run
- backup = copy the file
- handles tens of millions of rows

All SQL lives in `db/sqlite.py` and the store modules mixed into its `Database` (`history/reads.py`, `history/writes.py`, `state/reads.py`, `state/writes.py`, `state/journal.py`, `state/positions.py`, `state/gathering.py`, `intel/store.py`, `recommend/store.py`), each behind a reader protocol. Moving to Postgres would only mean replacing that package, if this ever becomes multi-user or hosted.

```
items(key PK, item_id, name, quality, ilvl, class, subclass, vendor_buy, vendor_sell, stack, updated)
candles_h1(market, key, t, open, high, low, close, qty, src,   PK(market,key,t,src))   WITHOUT ROWID
candles_d1(market, key, t, open, high, low, close, qty, src,   PK(market,key,t,src))   WITHOUT ROWID
volume_d1(market, key, day, listed, sold, expired, src, conf, method, PK(market,key,day,src))
volume_cover(market, day, src, kind, covered,                PK(market,day,src))      WITHOUT ROWID   -- how a source observed a day (§3.3.1)
sales_h1(market, key, t, sold_per_hour,                      PK(market,key,t))        -- book-pair sales rate per hour start t (§3.3 sales_how)
current(market, key, price, qty, at, src,                    PK(market,key,src))
scans(sid PK, scan_id UNIQUE, market, src, char_id, at, kind, coverage)   -- src: local (your scan) | ahf (Premium books) | a provider; char_id null unless local
book_entries(sid, key, price, stack, band, count,            PK(sid,key,price,stack,band))   WITHOUT ROWID   -- sid: the scan's integer id (V38)
auction_mail(event_id PK, char_id, key, qty, price, outcome, at)   -- index (char_id,key,at,outcome)
payouts(event_id PK, char_id, key, qty, money, at, mail_id, realized, deposit)   -- gold taken from a sold mail; realized copper, set once (T17)
disenchants(event_id PK, char_id, key, results, at, action_id, expected_net)   -- results "key,qty;..." by key (T18)
posts(post_id PK, char_id, market, key, stack, price, cost_each, p_at_post, duration_h, deposit, at, outcome, resolved_at, action_id,
      event_id, confirmed, expected_net)
purchases(event_id PK, char_id, key, qty, price, source, at, action_id, confirmed, expected_net)   -- index (char_id,key,at)
crafts(event_id PK, char_id, spell_id, count, cost_each, at, action_id, expected_net)   -- index (char_id,spell_id,at)
-- expected_net on posts, purchases, crafts, disenchants: the matched action's expected net × matched units ÷ its units,
-- copied by the tracking pass when the event matches (REAL copper; NULL = matched no action; T23, schema 19)
cost_basis(key PK, qty, total_cost, untracked_qty)
actions(action_id PK, run_at, slot_ms, char_id, kind, key, qty, price, expected_net, conf, status, done_qty, done_at, parent_id,
        window_start_ms, window_end_ms, anchor_net, anchor_window_ms, replaces_id, week_start_ms, peak_ms,
        source,   -- buys: 'vendor' or 'ah' (D41)
        duration_h, to_char,   -- posts: auction hours (EX9); mail: the recipient, char_id being the sender (C22)
        cand_id)               -- the funded candidate whose opportunity made it; NULL for your stock's rest (schema 22, M14)
item_rules(key PK, rule, set_at, source)
reserves(char_id, key, qty, set_at, source,  PK(char_id,key))   -- qty -1 = all, 0 = cleared (kept for newest-wins); set_at NULL from an older addon (§3.4.3)
characters(id PK, name, realm, faction, market, class, level, gold, updated)
professions(char_id, profession, skill, max_skill,           PK(char_id,profession))
recipes(profession, spell_id, product_key, yield_min, yield_max, skill_req, cooldown_s,  PK(profession,spell_id))
reagents(profession, spell_id, key, count,                   PK(profession,spell_id,key))
known_recipes(char_id, spell_id,                             PK(char_id,spell_id))
inventory(char_id, location, key, qty, bound,                PK(char_id,location,key,bound))
own_auctions(char_id, key, price, stack, band, count, seen,  PK(char_id,key,price,stack,band))
snapshots(char_id, location, at,                             PK(char_id,location))   -- capture time of each place, incl. auctions
skips(event_id PK, char_id, action_id, at)                   -- "Mark as skipped" from the addon; applied when the action exists
vendor_reagents(key PK)                                      -- seen at a merchant (add-only)
sync_state(market PK, last_hour, last_d1, last_volume, live_since, last_run)
meta(name PK, value)                                         -- e.g. savedvars_ingested_until; built (M25, §3.7.8, §3.7.9; app/update_check.py KEYS): update.checked_ms, etag, tag, url, prerelease, failure, failed_since_ms, downloaded, downloaded_path, download_failed, download_tried_ms, left_beta; planned (M27): cant_replace, updated_to, updated_ms, pending
mail_sent(event_id PK, char_id, to_char, key, qty, at, action_id, confirmed)
status(topic PK, level, kind, data, at)                      -- Status page lines; data = JSON fields, rendered by views (§8.5)
gather_events(event_id PK, char_id, at, source, login_ms, loot)   -- built (schema 23, M17): one loot window (§3.4E.3); loot "key,qty;..." by key; index (char_id,at)
farming_sessions(session_id PK, char_id, start_ms, end_ms, units, ended)   -- built (schema 23): confirmed, ended sessions only (§3.4E.4); ended 'gap' | 'logout'; index (char_id,end_ms)
farming_session_items(session_id, key, qty,                  PK(session_id,key))   -- built (schema 23): units per item, catalog or not
manual_rates(char_id, key, items_per_hour, set_at,           PK(char_id,key))      -- built (schema 23): rate overrides from the dashboard (§3.4E.5)
fetch_failures(market, hour, failures, last_error, last_at,  PK(market,hour))   -- hour files to retry (§5.2)
ahledger_rows(market, key, updated, median, m7, m30, lo, hi, per_day_x10,  PK(market,key))   WITHOUT ROWID   -- **built** (schema 24, M19): the price table's aggregates, as they came; read by nothing (§2.3)
source_state(market, src, etag, updated_ms, fetched_ms, checkpoint,  PK(market,src))   -- **built** (schema 24, M19): per remote source: last ETag, the table's `updated`, last fetch; the backfill's resume point (§2.3)
observed_hours(market, src, hour,                            PK(market,src,hour))   WITHOUT ROWID   -- **built** (schema 24, M19: written by `db.add_observed_hours`; its reader `history/reads.py` `observed_hours` **built**, M20): hours with a complete observation, per source (§3.5 Data coverage)
```

**Portfolio tables.** `alternatives` is built (schema 20, M9): the plan's unfunded candidates with their D.6 reason, staged in `alternatives_new` and swapped in by `publish_refresh` with the plan, on a full refresh and a re-plan alike (`recommend/store.py` `stage_alternatives`, `alternatives`). The portfolio plan writes it since M10 (`recommend.portfolio.alternative_rows`): one row per unfunded candidate batch, its list (today or week) by its opportunity's first batch. **Built (schema 21, M11):** what the addon files carry of the portfolio, staged in `export_candidates_new` and `portfolio_snapshot_new` and swapped in by `publish_refresh` with the plan (`RecommendStore.stage_export`, written by `recommend.portfolio.portfolio_export`): `export_candidates` holds the exported candidates in export order (§3.4D.3, `candidate_export` in all), each with its status ("" when funded, else its D.6 status), adjusted profit and `candidate_to_json`; `portfolio_snapshot` holds one JSON document per market (`recommend.portfolio.snapshot_data`: the run's time, the settings the addon scores with, the config version, capital base, committed capital, remaining capacity, the gold allocated, the reservation per character and the positions with their plan action). The export reads them, so a `PortfolioPlan.lua` written after a SavedVariables read or the status pass carries the last plan's snapshot (the status pass swaps in its re-evaluated positions, §3.4D.9). **Built (schema 22, M14):** the position tables (§3.4D.9; `state/positions.py`, written by `recommend/positions_pass.py`), and `actions.cand_id` (and its staging twin's), so a matched purchase or craft knows the funded opportunity it opens a position for:

```
positions(position_id PK, char_id, market, cand_id, strategy, key, state, opened_at, units, units_left, cost_total, cost_left,
          expected_net_open, expected_net_now, target_window_ms, latest_window_ms, next_action, realized, closed_at)   -- built (schema 22)
position_lots(position_id, event_id, units, unit_cost, at,    PK(position_id,event_id))   -- built (schema 22): a matched purchase or craft event
alternatives(market, cand_id, char_id, list, reason, data,    PK(market,cand_id))          -- built (schema 20): list 'today' | 'week', data = candidate_to_json
export_candidates(market, pos, cand_id, status, adjusted, data, PK(market,pos))           -- built (schema 21): pos 0 first; status '' = funded
portfolio_snapshot(market PK, data)                                                        -- built (schema 21): snapshot_data as JSON
```

- A position's `state` is one of §3.4D.9's eight. `cost_left` is what you paid for the units still held, listed, in the mail or sold but not yet paid out; the sum over open positions is the committed capital (§3.4D.1). Positions are kept 90 days after they close, like actions (`history/prune.py`, `delete_closed_positions_before`, their lots with them). `position_id` is the opening event's id (`charId:timeMs:seq`); `char_id` and `key` are who holds the units and what they are now (a mail moves them, a craft turns Processing mats into the product); `char_id`, `key`, `state`, the units, the costs, `realized`, `closed_at` and `next_action` are the replay's (tracking pass), `state`, `expected_net_now`, `latest_window_ms` and `next_action` then the latest evaluation's (plan run, status pass).
- Lots are upserted by `event_id`, so reading the same SavedVariables twice never adds a lot twice (PO8).

- Candle and `current` `src` is `local`, `ahf` or, `ahledger` (**built** M19; the backfill's `candles_d1` **built**, M21) (the price table: `current`, `candles_h1`, and the backfill's `candles_d1`) or `ahledger_h1` (the backfill's hourly closes, `candles_h1` only), or a future provider's name. Which close statistics use for an (item, hour) follows the source's kind, then its rank (§2.2).
- **AHledger and coverage tables** (**built**, **schema 24**, M19; `SCHEMA_VERSION` is 24, and the migration to 24 adds the three tables below and nothing else):
  - `ahledger_rows` holds one row per market and item, the latest price table's `median`, `median7d` (`m7`), `median30d` (`m30`), `low30d` (`lo`), `high30d` (`hi`) in copper and `soldPerDayx10` (`per_day_x10`, tenths), NULL for an empty field, with the table's `updated` (Unix s). A newer table replaces the row; an item missing from a newer table keeps its last row. No reader, no analysis and no page uses it (SRC11); it is kept for a later decision and pruned with the market's other rows after 90 days.
  - `source_state` holds, per market and remote source (`ahledger` today): the last `ETag`, the table's `updated` in ms, the last fetch time (ms) and the backfill's `checkpoint` (JSON: range, last key done, the day it started), so a restart polls with the right `If-None-Match` and the backfill resumes (§2.3). A failure never clears it.
  - `observed_hours` holds, per market and source, each UTC hour (`floor(ms ÷ 3600000)`) with at least one **complete** observation (§3.5 Data coverage: a complete local scan, an AHF hour file with rows, an AHledger table's `updated`, an AHledger hourly bucket). Each writer upserts it in the same transaction as the data, so it stays idempotent; it is pruned at 90 days like candles. It exists because order books (`scans`) are kept only 30 days and the hourly tables are too big to read at each status pass (§3.1.1). The migration fills it from what is still there: the complete local scans kept (30 days) and the hours of stored `ahf` hour closes; older local hours are not recoverable and count for nobody.
- Volume `src` is `ahf` (Premium depth), `books` (own order-book diffs) or `mail`, ranked in that order of trust; `qty_drop` rows hold listing turnover, never sales (§3.3.1). `conf` is `high`, `medium` or `low`.
- Scan `kind` is `full` or `partial`. Mail `outcome` is `sold`, `expired`, `cancelled` or `won`. Post `outcome` is null while pending, then `sold`, `expired` or `cancelled`.
- `location` is `bags`, `bank` or `mail`. Items on the AH live in `own_auctions`.
- Action `kind` is `buy`, `craft`, `mail`, `post`, `hold`, `de` or `vendor`. `status` is `pending`, `late`, `partial`, `done`, `missed` or `skipped`. A replacement post points to the missed one with `replaces_id` and carries the original `anchor_net` and `anchor_window_ms` (§3.4.0). `parent_id` links a sell action to the buy or craft that created the stock.
- `cost_basis` is **what you actually paid**, as an average per unit across all your characters. It is account-wide, so mailing an item to an alt keeps its cost. It is used for display (Inventory, On the AH, "below what you paid"). Planning uses opportunity cost instead (§3.4). The tracking pass rebuilds it each run (`engine/cost_basis`): confirmed purchases (AH and vendor) and crafts in time order, a craft taking its mats out at their average cost and adding its product at that cost (T10); then reconciled with what you hold now, every character and place including your auctions: units that left take their average cost with them, units held beyond what was bought or crafted count at 0 (T9). An item never bought or crafted gets no row and shows "before tracking" (T11, T12). A post or vendor sale planned below it says "below what you paid (40c)" and is still planned, because what you paid is spent (T7).
  - A purchase (AH or vendor, `purchases.source`) adds `qty × price`.
  - Items you found (looted, farmed, gifted) add their quantity at cost 0.
  - A craft adds its reagents' own cost basis, so a craft from found mats costs 0. The cost per product is stored in `crafts.cost_each`.
  - Selling, vendoring, disenchanting or using an item as a reagent removes units at the average cost. A post records the average at posting time in `posts.cost_each`.
  - Items you already held when tracking started count as cost 0, and `untracked_qty` marks them, so the UI can say "before tracking".
- **Gathering tables** (**built, schema 23, M17**; §3.4E; `state/gathering.py`, the `GatheringStore` mixed into `Database`): `gather_events` are upserted by `event_id` like every event (GA13, FM41); a stored event never changes. `farming_sessions` and `farming_session_items` are derived: `session_id` is the session's first event id, and a character's sessions are rebuilt from its stored events (`rebuild_sessions`, `engine.sessions`) inside the read's transaction whenever a read brings new events for it (`state/importer.apply_data`), so the same events always give the same rows. Only confirmed sessions that have ended are stored; the open candidate, and a candidate that ended since the last read because time passed, are derived at each page build from the events after the character's last stored session (`events_after_sessions`, `recommend/gathering_page.farming_state`), never written. A changed `session_confirm_units` or `session_gap_minutes` re-cuts every character's sessions at the next start (`ensure_session_settings`, the settings kept in `meta.gathering_session_settings`). Retention (90 days) deletes gather events older than the cutoff and sessions that ended before it, with their items (`history/prune.py`, `delete_farming_sessions_before`). `ended` says what ended it (the next event 15 min or more later, or another login). `manual_rates` holds the overrides; a clear deletes the row (no tombstone: the dashboard is its only writer). **Rates are not stored**: each Gathering page build computes them from the sessions and overrides (§3.4E.5).
- `item_rules.rule` is `no_buy`, `ignore`, `keep` or `none` (§3.4.2). `source` is `dashboard` or `addon`. Clearing a rule writes `none` with a new `set_at` rather than deleting the row. That tombstone is what lets a clear beat an older rule from the other side. Tombstones older than 90 days are pruned.
- **Failure handling (§5.2):**
  - **Event ids.** Every event table (`posts`, `purchases`, `crafts`, `disenchants`, `auction_mail`, `payouts`, `mail_sent`, `skips` and `gather_events`) is keyed by `event_id` (`charId:timeMs:seq`, unique), and ingest upserts on it. A natural key such as (character, item, time) would merge two identical buys in the same second.
  - **Snapshots.** `snapshots` holds each place's capture time, so an older file never overwrites a newer snapshot, not even an empty one.
  - **Skips** follow the 90-day retention. A skip whose action doesn't exist yet is applied when matching creates it (§3.4.1, M4).
  - **Attempts.** `purchases` and `posts` have `confirmed` (0/1); unconfirmed rows older than 1 h are ignored.
  - **Scan coverage.** `scans` has `coverage` (0–1); only `kind = full` with coverage 1 feeds volume diffs.
  - **Materialized stats.** `item_stats` (§3.1.1) is the per-item stats table that refresh writes. It has `item_stats_new` and `plan_new` as staging twins.
  - **Format version.** `meta` stores `schema_version`, and migrations run on start.
  - **Status lines.** `status` stores a topic, a level and **structured fields** (ms ranges, counts, hour numbers) as JSON, never pre-formatted text, so the dashboard can show them in local time (§3.3) through `views` (§8.5). A line is deleted when its condition clears (Premium back, the hour recovered, the endpoint working again). Log lines may carry English text; they never carry secrets.
  - **Placeholders until M3.** `item_stats` and `item_stats_new` hold `(market, key, stats JSON, updated)` until the engine defines their columns.

**Retention: 90 days**, matching AHF's longest window, for candles, volume, posts, purchases, crafts, mail and actions, and for gather events and farming sessions (so a rate covers the last 90 days). `cost_basis` and `item_rules` are kept until they no longer apply, `manual_rates` until cleared. Order books (`scans`, `book_entries`, every source) are kept **30 days**, which is what the undercut share and the price-tier sell-through need (§3.3.2). Hourly AHF books for about 9k items are the largest table; the size estimate below assumes them.

**Size estimate** for one market of about 9k items, at about 40–60 bytes per hour row:

| Scan frequency | 1 week | 90 days |
|---|---|---|
| Today (about 1 scan a week) | < 1 MB | about 10 MB |
| About 5 scans a day (likely post-launch) | about 20 MB | about 250 MB |
| Every item every hour (worst case) | about 90 MB | about 1.2 GB |
| Hourly AHF order books (Premium), kept 30 days | about 250 MB | about 1 GB at 30 days (about 5 price groups per item, about 30 bytes per row) |
| Raw snapshots with auction ids (a future provider, hourly), kept 30 days | on disk, not in SQLite | about 68M auction rows; one gzipped JSON file per snapshot under `data/snapshots/<market>/<src>/` with an `index.json` (`collect/snapshots.py`, V36). Only their grouped books go into `book_entries` |

Scale linearly per extra market.

### 3.3 Pricing primitives (per item, per market)

**Numeric rules** (Python and Lua must follow them exactly, or the two engines drift apart):

- Prices, costs, deposits and budgets are **integer copper**. Deposits are floored.
- Anything multiplied by a probability or a ratio (`P`, expected values, `how`-adjusted prices) is a **float in copper**. It is rounded half up, `floor(x + 0.5)`, only when shown or exported. Never use Python's `round()`, which rounds half to even.
- **Median** of an even count = floor of the mean of the two middle values.
- **Percentile** p = nearest rank: sort ascending and take the value at 1-based index `ceil(p × n)`.
- **Time** is UTC ms internally. The hour of week uses the UTC date: `weekday = (floor(ms/86400000) + 3) mod 7` with 0 = Monday (1970-01-01 was a Thursday), and `hour = floor(ms/3600000) mod 24`. Display uses the machine's local time. The ±2-hour smoothing absorbs daylight-saving shifts.

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `cur`, `curQty`, `curAge` | newest lowest buyout (of every source, whichever is newest; `local` on ties, S7), quantity, age; nil when nothing is listed |
| `curOthers` | lowest buyout that is not one of your own auctions (`own_auctions`). It uses the next price in the newest local book if you hold the lowest one; nil if unknown |
| `med7`, `med30` | median of hourly closes in the window (one per item and hour, §2.2). With fewer than 24 hourly closes, it uses the median of daily closes instead, or the few hourly closes when there are no daily ones; nil when there are neither |
| `p10_30`, `p90_30` | nearest-rank 10th and 90th percentiles of hourly closes over 30 days |
| `trend7` | least-squares slope of the daily medians over the last 7 days ÷ `med7` × 100, in % per day. It needs at least 4 days of data, otherwise it is 0 |
| `sold/day`, `sellThru` | **sales volume**; see §3.3.1 for its sources |
| `how[7×24]` | **hour-of-week profile**: median close per (weekday, hour) ÷ `med30`, smoothed as the mean of the slot and the 2 slots on each side. A slot with data from at least 3 different weeks is used with confidence high. With 1–2 weeks it falls back to the weekday-only ratio (median of that weekday ÷ `med30`), confidence low. With no data it is flat (1.0), or takes its weekday's value for smoothing, but **a slot with 0 weeks of data never qualifies for a selling or buying window** (W16) |
| `sales_how[7×24]` | **hourly sales profile**: units sold in each hour-of-week slot ÷ the average hour. Its mean over the 168 slots is 1.0. It is measured from consecutive hourly order books (AHF `books` with Premium, or your scans), smoothed like `how`. **Hourly rate:** each pair of consecutive complete books of a market (either source) at most 2 h apart (`volume.SALES_PAIR_MAX_GAP_MS`; longer gaps blur the hour) gives `sold ÷ gap in hours` (§3.3.1 source 2), counted for the clock hour of the earlier book. Books of different sources less than 15 min apart never pair: the later one is dropped from the walk, as for daily volume (§3.3.1 source 2, V40). An hour with several pairs takes their total sold ÷ their total gap. Rates are computed at ingest and stored per hour in `sales_h1`, so analysis never diffs books. It needs data from at least 3 weeks per slot (else the weekday-only value, else flat 1.0). It drives demand in the sell-probability simulation (§3.3.2) |
| `sell_score[7×24]` | **how good each hour of the week is for selling**: `how[slot] × sales_how[slot] ÷ max(sales_how)`, i.e. the price you get there × how busy buyers are relative to the busiest hour. A high price with no buyers scores low |
| `sell_windows` | the item's **best selling windows**, derived from `sell_score` (§3.3.4), e.g. "Tue 18:00–21:00, Wed 19:00–22:00" |
