| `n`, `conf` | `n` = hourly closes in the last 30 days. `conf` = a **confidence percentage** (0–100%) for each result, defined in §3.3.5. Its level (high ≥ 75%, medium ≥ 45%, low below that) is only for the dot colour; rankings use the percentage itself |

#### 3.3.1 Sales volume

The free API gives only the **quantity listed**, not sales. Sales volume is the main input for the probability that something sells, so it comes from the best source available. Each value carries its source.

Every daily row (`volume_d1`) has a source name (`src`) and a **cover** (`volume_cover`, one per market, day and source): its **kind**, by method, and the seconds it observed in each UTC hour of the day (`covered`, 24 values; NULL = the whole day). The engine ranks rows by kind and coverage, never by provider name, so a new source (a Blizzard API job, say) is used as soon as its rows carry a kind (V32).

**Kinds, most trusted first:**

1. **Auction-id diffs (`ids`)**: snapshots with auction ids (a retail-shaped API, §5.0). Between two snapshots at most 6 h apart (`engine.volume.estimate_volume_ids`, V33):
   - an id still listed with fewer units sold the difference (a partial commodity buy, measured exactly);
   - an id that is gone expired with its band's chance of running out in the gap (as below), else it sold;
   - an id not seen before is newly listed.
   - Known error: a cancel still counts as sold, since cancels and buys both remove an id. A repost at a new price is a cancel plus a new id, so it counts as a sale only through that cancel.
2. **Group diffs of order books (`groups`)**: every complete book of a market, whatever its source (your full scans, AHF Premium `books`, a provider's grouped snapshots), pairs with the next complete one, at most 6 hours apart (`volume.MAX_GAP_MS`, V29). A Blizzard snapshot at 18:00 pairs with your scan at 18:40, and that scan with the 19:00 snapshot.
   - **Books of different sources pair only at least 15 minutes apart** (`volume.CROSS_SOURCE_MIN_GAP_MS`, V39). Two sources build a book differently (AHF's addon and ours skip different auctions: bid-only, pets, band handling; a grouped snapshot keys commodities by units left), so a pair taken moments apart shows that difference as phantom sold and new units. Same-source pairs keep the plain rule (gap > 0 and at most 6 h). The rule (`volume.book_pairs`): walk the complete books in time order (ties: scan id), keeping a current book. A next book of a different source less than 15 min after the current one is **dropped**: it is part of no pair, and the current book stays. Any other next book pairs with the current one when 0 < gap ≤ the max gap, and becomes the current book either way (a too-long gap just restarts the walk). So an interval between kept books is covered by exactly one pair, and the day's cover counts only the pairs counted. A day's walk starts at the first complete book 6 h before the day, an hour range's walk (hourly sales) 2 h before its first hour, so every recompute drops the same books unless books of different sources alternate less than 15 min apart for that long. With A (AHF 18:00:00), B (your scan 18:00:40), C (your scan 20:00): B is dropped and A pairs with C. AHF's Premium `depth` volume file is this kind too, for a whole day (its row format is still unknown, §6 item 1, so nothing stores it yet). Units are diffed **per price level** `(item, unit price)` (V42, V43):
   - Neither stack size nor time left is part of the level. Forever's AH is retail-style with commodities: a buyer taking 50 of a 200-unit auction leaves an auction of 150 at the same price, so a level keyed by stack size would count the whole 200 as sold and the 150 as new. Time left ticking from long to medium would likewise count as gone plus new (V6).
   - `before` = Σ stack × count over the level's entries in the earlier book, `after` = the same over the later book; `g` = time between them.
   - Each time-left band covers `[lo, hi)`: short 0–30 min, medium 30 min–2 h, long 2–8 h, very long 8 h to the longest duration. These are the Classic bands and must be verified for Forever (§6). An auction is assumed to sit anywhere in its band with equal chance. So the expected units of the earlier book's level that ran out during the gap are `expiring = Σ stack × count × clamp((g − lo) / (hi − lo), 0, 1)` over its entries.
   - `gone = max(0, before − after)` and `new = max(0, after − before)`
   - `expired = min(gone, expiring)` and `sold = gone − expired`. All in units; they can be fractional. Item totals sum their levels.
   - A partial fill is measured exactly (Copper Ore 30c ×200 → 30c ×150: 50 sold, nothing new, V42), and a stack re-split at the same price is nothing (×200 ×1 → ×100 ×2, V43). Known errors: a cancelled auction counts as sold; a repost at a **new** price counts as sold at the old level and listed at the new (30c ×100 → 28c ×100: 100 sold, 100 listed, V43); a repost at the **same** price counts as nothing; a buy and a new listing of equal size at one level between two books cancel out.
   - A pair counts for the day of its later book; the day's cover adds up the time each pair spans, per clock hour (a pair that starts the day before folds into the same hours, `engine.volume.covered_seconds`).
3. **Your own sales (`own`)**: "auction successful" and "auction expired" mail, per item and day (`collect.own_mail`, V9). It is ground truth for your own auctions only, so it is a **lower bound** on the market: the last-resort source for a day nothing else observed (V35), and a floor under the others (V34). It never calibrates them, because a ratio of your sales to the market's is not a correction (V18).
4. **Listing turnover, not sales.** `max(0, qty_prev − qty_next)` between consecutive AHF quantity observations a few hours apart counts units that left the AH: sold, expired or cancelled, which totals alone can't tell apart. It is stored as `observed_listing_turnover` (§3.3.6) and shown for information, but it has no kind and is **never used as sales**. An item whose only data is quantities has sales volume **unavailable**, and its chance is the default for its position (`default_sell_prob`, or `default_sell_prob_cheapest` when it would be the cheapest seller), marked "volume unknown" (§3.3.2).

Group diffs are an explicit estimate, not confirmed sales: they are labelled "est." and carry a lower confidence (§3.3.5).

**A partly observed day.** Two scans 2 hours apart that saw 40 sold did not see a day of sales. Each day counts as the share of a day's sales its source observed: `share = Σ_h sales_how[weekday, h] × hours observed in h ÷ Σ_h sales_how[weekday, h]` (`engine.volume.day_shares`), so a sample from the busy evening hours isn't stretched flat across the night (V31). With a flat or missing pattern for the day, the share is hours observed ÷ 24 (V30). A whole-day source has share 1.

**Choosing the source, per item and per day:**
- **All available sources are always computed and stored**, one row per source. The estimates keep running whatever else is available, so there is no gap when a source lapses.
- Each day uses its best row: the most trusted kind, then the most of the day observed (sales share), then the source name (V11, V32). A 30-day `sold/day` can mix days of different kinds.
- `sold/day = (Σ sold + prior/day × prior days) ÷ (Σ share + prior days)` over the window's days that have a source: the sold units seen, per day-equivalent observed, pulled toward a modest prior (below). Listed per day is the same with the hours observed (time shares) in place of the sales shares; expired per day is the plain `Σ expired ÷ Σ time share` (it only feeds the informational sell-through). Days without a source are left out, never counted as zero.
- **A short sample** (`engine.volume.VolumePrior`, V41, VI4). Two hours with no sales would otherwise give exactly 0 a day, and two hours with one sale 12 a day. The rate is the Gamma-Poisson posterior mean: the prior counts as `volume_prior_hours` (2 h = 1/12 of a day) of observation that saw `volume_prior_per_day` (1.0) units a day. Examples with a flat pattern: 2 h, 0 sold → (0 + 1 × 1/12) ÷ (1/12 + 1/12) = 0.5/day; 2 h, 40 sold → 40.083 ÷ 0.1667 = 240.5/day (raw 480, V30); 7 whole days at 40/day → 280.083 ÷ 7.083 = 39.5/day. The prior's weight fades as observed days grow. It applies only when at least one day has a source: an item with no volume source stays "volume unknown" (None), never the prior. Confidence is unchanged, since coverage already lowers it (§3.3.5).
- **Id diffs calibrate the group diffs.** On days with both, `factor = Σ id sold ÷ Σ group sold scaled to whole days` (each kind's best row of the day), computed from the stored rows of the item's whole history and applied to its group-diff days (V16). With no such day, the factor is 1.
- **Your sales are a floor.** On a day whose chosen row is not your mail, its sold units are at least your mail's sold units × that day's share (V34).
- Every `sold/day` shows its source mix on the item detail and in the Status page, e.g. "sold/day 38 · 22 days AHF, 8 days estimated".
- Rows stored before covers existed have none: `ahf` and `books` count as group diffs for a whole day and `mail` as your own (`engine.volume.LEGACY_KIND`).

#### 3.3.2 Sell probability and expected value

`P` = the expected fraction of your `q` units that sell **within the post's sell window**. It depends on *when* you post, because demand changes through the day and the week.

**The sell window** is always made of whole clock hours (the current hour counts in full):

| Context | Window |
|---|---|
| Posting now: Today, Disenchant (selling the mats), Craft (craft now and sell), a sell value (§3.4) | the next `sell_window_hours` (8) from now |
| A planned post in the Week plan, including the sale half of a flip or a craft-to-sell | from the post time (`post_lead_hours` = 8 before the best hour of its target selling window, §3.3.4) through the end of that window. If the post time has already passed, it starts now. If the window has ended, the peak was missed (§3.4.0) |

- The auction duration is the shortest one that covers the window.
- Sales after the window are not counted, which is conservative: the auction may still sell later.

`P` combines two views:
- a **queue simulation** of what happens to your auction given today's competition;
- the **historical sell-through** of auctions priced like yours.

Both use the same formulas with or without Premium. Premium only supplies better inputs.

**Inputs** (per item and market):

| Input | Meaning | With Premium | Without |
|---|---|---|---|
| `S` | units sold per day (7-day average) | AHF `volume` | estimates (§3.3.1) |
| `X` | units expired per day | AHF `volume` | estimates |
| `L` | units newly listed per day | AHF `volume` | estimates |
| book | every competing auction: unit price, units, time-left band | AHF `books` (latest) | your newest scan, if under 24 h old |
| `u(p)` | **undercut share**: of the units newly listed over the last 7 days, the share priced at or below your price relative to the lowest price at the time they were listed. **Not built: the default applies** (`default_undercut_share`, 0.5, everywhere; `sellprob.undercut_share` exists but nothing calls it, §6) | AHF book history | your scans' history, else `default_undercut_share` (0.5) |
| `ST_tier` | **sell-through by price tier**: of the auctions that disappeared in the last 30 days, the share that sold rather than expired, grouped by how their price compared with the cheapest competitor. **Not built: the default applies** (no tier history, so `e` = 0 and `P = P_queue`, §6) | AHF book history | your scans' history, else none |

**Market facts shown with every result** (informational):
- **Market sell-through** = `S ÷ (S + X)`, e.g. "72% of auctions sell".
- **Supply on the AH** = the total units listed now, `N`.
- **Days of supply** = `N ÷ S`, e.g. "3.1 days of supply".

**1. Queue simulation** (`sellprob.simulate`). It runs over the window's clock hours `k = 0 … W−1` (slot `t_k`) and is deterministic:
- **Demand.** In hour `k`, buyers take `S ÷ 24 × sales_how[t_k]` units from the cheapest listings available, oldest-cheapest first. At an equal price the book's order stands in for "oldest"; the order never depends on the time-left band, so a competitor expiring sooner can't reorder the queue (PI12). `sales_how` is the hourly sales profile (§3.3), so a window that covers the evening rush sees more buyers than one overnight.
- **Who is ahead of you.** Competing auctions priced at or below `p` are ahead. At an equal price they are also ahead of you, which is the conservative choice.
- **Competitors expire.** An auction with band midpoint `m` is available during hour `k` only while `k < m`. The midpoints are: short 0.25 h, medium 1.25 h, long 5 h, very long `(8 + longest duration) ÷ 2`. When an auction expires, its unsold units leave the queue and stop absorbing demand. The band bounds are the same as §3.3.1 and still need verifying (§6).
- **New undercutters.** At the start of each hour, `L × u(p) ÷ 24` new units appear priced below you. They are the cheapest listings from then on and stay up for the rest of the window.
- **Your sales.** Demand left over after everything ahead of you goes to you, until your `q` units are gone.
- **Result.** `P_queue = your units sold ÷ q`.

**2. Price-tier sell-through.** Your tier is `r = p ÷ curOthers`:

| Tier | Your price vs. the cheapest competitor |
|---|---|
| T1 | `r ≤ 1.00` (you are the cheapest) |
| T2 | `r ≤ 1.05` |
| T3 | `r ≤ 1.15` |
| T4 | above that |

`ST_tier = sold ÷ (sold + expired)` for that item's auctions in the tier, using the order-book diff method of §3.3.1. `n_tier` is how many auctions it is based on.

**3. Blend.** *Not built yet: `ST_tier` is never computed, so `e` = 0 and the blend returns `P_queue` (§6).*
- `e = 0.5 × min(1, n_tier ÷ 30)`, with `e = 0` when there is no tier history.
- `P = (1 − e) × P_queue + e × ST_tier`.
- The history never counts for more than half. The simulation sees today's competition, and the history catches what the simulation misses, such as buyers who skip an item at any price.

**4. Your own record** is applied after this: `P'` (§3.3.3).

**Fallbacks:**
- **No volume data at all** (`S` unknown): `P` is a default chosen by where the post would stand (`sellprob.default_chance`, PD1, P25): `default_sell_prob_cheapest` (0.5) when nobody else lists the item or `p ≤ curOthers` (you'd be the cheapest seller, a tie included), else `default_sell_prob` (0.25). It is marked "volume unknown". Your own record is blended in after it as usual (§3.3.3). `curOthers` here is the lowest price in the book, else `cur` without a book.
- **Demand when posting now** uses the item's own `sales_how` over the next `sell_window_hours`, as the curve and the plan do, so an item whose buyers come in the next hours gets a higher posting-now chance (P26); with a flat profile it is the flat result.
- **No usable book:** there are no band or price details. The simulation uses a single block of `curQty` units ahead of you, but only when `p ≥ cur`, and that block stays available for the whole window.
- **Confidence:** every `P` carries a `conf` percentage (§3.3.5), which drops as inputs weaken: estimated instead of real volume, an old or missing book, a default undercut share, a short pattern.

**Why the old one-line formula was not enough:** `clamp((S·W/24 − ahead) ÷ q)` assumed three things: demand is the same every hour, every competing listing absorbs buyers for the whole window, and no one undercuts you. The simulation is identical to it with a flat sales profile, nothing ahead expiring, and `u = 0`. That equivalence is tested (PI10), so the old formula is a special case, not a different answer.

**Explaining the number.** Every `P'` can be expanded on the dashboard (item detail, and hovering a Chance cell) into its parts, for example: "market sells 72% · 3.1 days of supply · 6 units ahead of you (all expire in 5 h) · ~6 undercuts expected · queue 95% · tier history 80% (30 auctions) → 88% · your record → 85%".

**Expected net of posting** `q` units at unit price `p`:

`E_post = P·q·p·(1 − cut) + (1 − P)·(q·fallback − deposit)`

- The deposit is **refunded when the auction sells** and lost only when it expires.
- Unsold units come back to you, worth `fallback` = max(vendor sell price, DE value per unit if one of your characters can disenchant it). Relisting is not modelled, to avoid recursion.

**Anything you pay for now** (a buy or a craft): `expected profit = E_post − cost`. So an unsold flip shows as a loss, not just as a smaller profit.

Items that sell only a few units a week get a low `P`, so they rank low however big their margin.

#### 3.3.3 Your own sell record

The market model can be wrong about an item, and your own posts are the best evidence of that. For each item and market, over the last `own_record_days` (30) days, across all your characters:

- `s` = your posts that sold, `x` = your posts that expired. Cancelled posts don't count. A post is one auction, so its outcome is either sold or expired.
- Each expiry gets a **cause**, worked out from the observations during the post's lifetime (local books and AHF `cur`):
  - `undercut`: something cheaper than your price was listed at some point, so price was the problem.
  - `lowest`: you were the cheapest in every observation, so demand was the problem.
  - `unknown`: there were no observations; it is treated as `lowest`.
- `streak` = `lowest` or `unknown` expiries in a row since your last sale of that item. Undercut expiries don't add to it.
- If you chose **Keep** for the item (§3.4.2), only posts after that choice count. The record starts fresh.

It changes recommendations in four ways:

1. **Probability.** `P` is blended with your record, with the model counting as `k = own_record_weight` (4) posts of evidence:
   `P' = (k·P + s) / (k + s + x)`.
   With no record, `P' = P`. `P'` replaces `P` everywhere in §3.3.2 and §3.4.
2. **Quantity.** The quantity planned for posting is multiplied by `0.5^streak`, rounded down to whole stacks, with a minimum of 1 stack. A sale resets `streak` to 0.
3. **Price.**
   - After a `lowest` or `unknown` expiry, the planned price is multiplied by `(1 − reprice_step)^streak` (5% per step) and floored to whole copper.
   - It never goes below `ceil(fallback / (1 − cut))`. Below that price, vendoring or disenchanting pays more, so the plan picks one of those instead.
   - After an `undercut` expiry, the next post's price is capped at `curOthers − 1` when you post, even if the plan's peak-slot price is higher.
4. **Priority.** When `streak ≥ struggle_streak` (3), the item is flagged **not selling**. After a Keep, the threshold is `keep_retry_streak` (1) instead, so a single further expiry while cheapest brings the question back.
   - It is left out of buy-to-flip and craft-to-sell, so no new stock is bought or made.
   - Stock you already own stays in the sell plan, where vendor or disenchant often wins once `P'` is low.
   - The dashboard and the addon offer **Blacklist** or **Keep** (§3.4.2).

**Built** (`engine/sell_record`, `recommend.plan.sell_records`): the records are computed from your resolved posts (§3.4.1 post outcomes) and the item's hourly closes at every full refresh and re-plan, and the plan applies all four effects. The undercut cap uses the item's current cheapest listing (yours has expired). The missed-peak re-plan of the hourly status pass uses the record too (M8): each refresh and re-plan stores the records it computed in `sell_records`, so the status pass reads them without reading history. The dashboard shows the record line on every Today and Week row of the item (D42; P before the blend is worked back from P') and a Needs a decision section on Today with Blacklist and Keep (D43), which `POST /api/rule` stores (SEC8). `PortfolioPlan.lua` carries each record line and not-selling flag as `records`, outside the checksum (EX8). The addon shows each record line under the item's Today and Week rows and lists flagged items in a Needs a decision section below Today's rows (A52, UI6, UI10); a right-click offers "Don't buy or craft it", "Ignore it completely" and "Keep selling it (start fresh)", saved as rules in `AHProfitDB.rules` (A53).

It is always visible. Every action row for an item with a record shows (the tooltip only reflects it in Chance to sell), for example, "Your record (30 d): 2 of 5 sold · expired 2× while cheapest · P 80% → 58% · price −10% · qty ¼". So you can see when and why your own history changed a recommendation. Old failures stop counting after 30 days, so an item can recover.

#### 3.3.4 Weekly patterns and best selling windows

The week's 168 hours are treated as **repeating slots**: every Tuesday 19:00 is the same slot. Each week of history adds one more observation to each slot. So the patterns sharpen over time and become a forecast for the coming week.

- **Recency weighting.** `how` and `sales_how` use a weighted median per slot. Each week's observation has weight `0.5 ^ (age_in_weeks ÷ pattern_half_life_weeks)` (4), so a pattern from last month counts half as much as last week's. A shift in when people buy, say after a patch, shows up within a few weeks.
  - Weighted median, deterministic: sort the values, then take the first value where the cumulative weight reaches half the total.
- **Best selling windows** (`patterns.sell_windows`):
  1. Take the slots with data (at least 1 week, W16) whose `sell_score` is at least `window_threshold` (90%) of the best such slot. A slot whose value only comes from its weekday's fallback can't join a window, so every window has at least 1 week of data; buying windows (§3.3.6) follow the same rule.
  2. Join adjacent qualifying slots into windows; a window can cross midnight.
  3. Rank the windows by the sum of their scores, and keep the top `top_sell_windows` (config, 3).
  4. Each window carries its average price ratio (`how`) and the number of weeks of data behind it, and, since M11, its forecast (§3.3.6 "Every window carries the same fields", W15).
- A week whose every slot with data qualifies (a flat score over the hours observed) has no best window: posting time doesn't matter, so the plan posts now.
- **Thin data has no windows** (PF30): an item with fewer than `MIN_HOURLY_CLOSES` (24) hourly closes in the last 30 days is planned with posting now only (`plan.thin`; `plan.post_options` leaves its windows out), whatever its pattern: a forecast from a handful of closes earns no premium and no deferral, so its sell-stock posts, flips and crafts sell now. A Buy for later still sees the windows (it exists only for one) and is listed, excluded "Forecast too uncertain (<n> closes in 30 d, needs 24)" (§3.4D.6). An unknown count keeps the windows.
- **Pattern strength**, from the weeks of data in the window's slots (the fewest weeks of any slot in the window): under 3 weeks is "early pattern", 3–5 is "pattern", 6 or more is "established". It feeds the window's confidence. With no pattern, the item has no windows and the plan uses posting now.
- **Where windows are used.** The Week plan's peak slot is the best slot inside the top window, and the post is placed 8 h before it (§3.4C). Windows are also shown on the tooltip ("Best time to sell"), on the item detail (heat map plus windows) and in Inventory.
- **Forecast.** The expected price in a window next week = `level × how × (1 + trend7/100 × days ahead)` (§3.4C; the trend factor is clamped to `1 ± max_trend_total`), then clamped to `[p10_30, max(med7, p90_30)]` when those are known (TT22): a window can't promise a price the last 30 days never traded at, as the post-now price can't (PP2). With fewer than `MIN_HOURLY_CLOSES` (24) hourly closes in the last 30 days the ceiling is the level itself (TT23): thin data earns no premium over the level, because a p90 of three closes is the outlier, not the market. The count is the intelligence's `closes_30d` (schema 8; unknown in older documents, which keep the TT22 clamp) and the market file's `closes30`. Here the **price level** is `med7`, or `med30` when there is no `med7`. A month-old level misleads when a market has moved: old listings that sat unsold keep `med30` high while the item now sells far lower (R21). The chance to sell there is the simulation over that window (§3.3.2), using `sales_how` for demand. Both get more confident as the pattern strengthens.

#### 3.3.6 Market activity metrics

Numbers, not labels: the engine never classifies an item as "high" or "low" liquidity. For every priced item and market, the full refresh computes the fields below and stores them in `item_stats`. Each states its unit and window. A value the data can't support is **unavailable** (None, shown "—"), never a guess and never 0.

| Field | Meaning (unit, window) | Unavailable when |
|---|---|---|
| `estimated_sales_per_day` | `sold/day` (§3.3.1): units per day, sold units ÷ the day-equivalents observed over the days of the last 7 that have a sales source | no day has a sales source |
| `estimated_sales_per_hour_of_week` | 168 values: `estimated_sales_per_day ÷ 24 × sales_how[slot]` (units per hour) | sales per day unavailable |
| `current_listed_supply` | units on the AH in the newest valid observation (local scan or AHF) | never seen |
| `median_daily_supply` | median of each day's listed units over the last 30 days (a day's value: the median of its observations; days without one are left out, V23). The liquidity cap uses it when the current supply is unknown (`intel.contract.supply_level`) | no observation in 30 days |
| `supply_to_volume_ratio` | `current_listed_supply ÷ estimated_sales_per_day`, in days of supply (§3.3.2 market facts). Not a time-to-sell guarantee | either input unavailable, or sales per day is 0 |
| `volume_confidence` | 0–1: the confidence component for volume (§3.3.5 "Volume source") | — (0 without a source) |
| `volume_observation_coverage` | 0–1: share of the last 7 days that have a sales source | — |
| `observed_listing_turnover` | units per day that left the AH between consecutive quantity observations at most 6 h apart over the last 7 days (§3.3.1 item 4): the units that left ÷ the hours those pairs span × 24 (`engine.volume.observed_turnover`, V37). Informational only: never used as sales. The item detail shows it apart from newly listed units per day, a different number | fewer than two observations at most 6 h apart |
| `estimated_time_to_sell(q)` | optional: hours until the queue simulation (§3.3.2) has sold q units, posting now | sales per day unavailable, or q not sold within 48 h |

**Weekly distribution.** Each of the 168 hour-of-week slots (§3.3.4) carries, over the rolling 90 days and recency-weighted like `how`:

| Per slot | Meaning | Unavailable when |
|---|---|---|
| `how` | median normalized price (§3.3) | (flat 1.0, as §3.3) |
| `supply_how` | median listed units in that slot ÷ the overall median listed units | fewer than 3 weeks of observations (weekday fallback, as `how`) |
| `sales_how` | estimated sales relative to the average hour (§3.3) | (flat 1.0, as §3.3) |
| `price_spread` | (nearest-rank p90 − p10) ÷ median of the slot's closes | fewer than 3 closes |
| `weeks` | weeks of data behind the slot (its observation coverage) | — |

Missing observation periods never count as zero sales or zero supply; they are left out of medians and averages.

**Seasonality strength** (0–100): how *predictable* the weekly price pattern is, kept separate from how *profitable* it is.
- **Out-of-sample test.** Build `how` from all weeks but the most recent one, then predict that held-out week's hourly closes two ways: with the weekly profile (`med30 × how[slot]`) and with a flat price (`med30`).
- `strength = floor(100 × max(0, 1 − error_profile ÷ error_flat) × min(1, weeks ÷ 6) + 0.5)`, where error is the mean absolute error over the held-out hours with a close.
- **Insufficient evidence** (strength None, shown "—" with "insufficient evidence"): fewer than 3 weeks of data, fewer than 24 held-out closes, or a flat error of 0.
- Consistent patterns score high even when the premium is small; a big premium that doesn't repeat scores low.

**Buying windows.** The mirror of selling windows (§3.3.4): the slots whose price ratio is at most `min(how) ÷ window_threshold` (within about 11% of the cheapest slot), joined into windows that may cross midnight. Flips buy in them (§3.4C.2). With no pattern there are none, and a buy is planned for now.
- **Ranked by actionable value, not by price** (W14; built M11, `patterns.buy_windows` with `patterns.window_forecast`, filled by `analysis.market.window_fields`; until M11 they were ranked by lowest average `how`). The cheapest hour is worth little when almost nothing is listed in it. Each qualifying window gets:
  - an **expected price** `e`: `plan.slot_price` of the price level × the window's average `how` at the window's next best hour: the trend factor and the clamps of §3.3.4 (to `[p10_30, max(med7, p90_30)]`, the level itself on thin data), in whole copper;
  - an **executable quantity** `x` = `max(1 stack, floor(max_supply_share × others' listed units in the window))`. This is §3.4's liquidity rule applied to the window's supply (`capacity.window_supply`: current supply, else the typical daily supply, × the window's mean `supply_how` ÷ `supply_how` now), and it is 1 stack when the supply is unknown (gear: `max_supply_share_gear` and at least `min_gear_units`);
  - its **confidence** `c` = §3.3.5's percent with the window's weeks as the pattern component, ÷ 100;
  - a **value** = `max(0, s × (1 − cut) − e) × x × c` (float copper, `patterns.buy_value`), where `s` is the expected price at the item's top selling window (its forecast, computed the same way), or the price level when it has none (`patterns.sell_net_price`).

  Windows rank by value, highest first (ties: the lower `e`, then the earlier start slot). The top `top_buy_windows` (config, 3) are kept, and a window worth 0 is dropped. Example: level 100 c, top selling window 1.2 (`s` = 120 c, 114 c after the cut), a reference stack of 5, confidence 0.8. Mon 03–06 at 0.85 with 10 listed scores (114 − 85) × 5 × 0.8 = 116. Thu 04 at 0.93 with 100 listed scores (114 − 93) × 50 × 0.8 = 840, so Thu ranks first although Mon is cheaper. An item without a price level (no `med7` or `med30`) has no forecast, and its buying windows keep the cheapest-first ranking. Because the forecast is clamped at `p10_30` and the sale at `max(med7, p90_30)`, an item whose 30-day range is narrower than the AH cut has no buying window at all: a buy there can't pay (the WP1 world's buying windows went this way).
- **Every window carries the same fields** (W15; built M11, `IntelWindow`, `INTEL_SCHEMA_VERSION` 9): start slot and hours (so its weekday, start and end), best slot, average `how`, weeks (so its strength, §3.3.4), `expected_price` (copper), `exec_qty` (units) and `conf` (%). The forecast time is the document's `built_ms`, kept once per intelligence document rather than per window. Documents before schema 9 read the three forecast fields as None, and so does a window of an item without a price level. Selling windows keep their `sell_score` ranking (§3.3.4) and the top `top_sell_windows` (3); their executable quantity is the window's liquidity cap (W15). `MarketIntelligence.lua`'s window strings are unchanged (`start:hours:best:how1000:weeks`): the addon doesn't read the forecasts before M12.
- **Forecasts are reference data, not rules.** A live price below every buy window's expected price is a buy now (§4.0, A76), and the next historical peak is not always the actionable one: windows are turned into actual upcoming hours from now (§3.4C), and the wait to each is what the timing premium prices (§3.4D.2).

**Price volatility and long windows.** `volatility_30` = standard deviation ÷ mean of the daily medians over 30 days; 7-, 30- and 90-day medians and nearest-rank percentiles. A close outside 10 × p90 or below 10% of p10 is a suspect price (§5.2), excluded from all statistics until a second observation confirms it.

**Three selling answers, kept apart** (item detail and Inventory): the hour with the **highest expected price** (max `how`), the hour with the **highest estimated activity** (max `sales_how`), and the **best risk-adjusted** window (max `sell_score`, §3.3.4), which is the one the plan uses.

**Method version.** `VOLUME_METHOD_VERSION` (an integer in `engine/volume.py`) is stored with every `volume_d1` row and every `item_stats` row. When it changes, the next full refresh recomputes `books` volume and its cover from the stored order books (kept 30 days, §3.2) and marks older rows as from an earlier method on the Status page; AHF and mail rows are kept as they are. Version 2 (2026-10-19) pairs books of every source and stores covers. Version 3 (2026-10-19) never pairs books of different sources less than 15 min apart (§3.3.1, V39). Version 4 (2026-10-19) diffs units per price level instead of auctions per (price, stack), so a partly bought commodity auction counts only the units bought, not the whole auction sold plus the rest new (§3.3.1, V42, V43). **Built** (V24): `volume_d1.method` holds the version of an estimated row (NULL for confirmed sources and rows from before versions were stored, which count as version 1); `collect.local_scans.recompute_stale_volume` runs at the start of each market's full refresh.

#### 3.3.5 Confidence percentage

`conf` says how much the data behind a result can be trusted. It is the weighted mean of the components that apply to the result:

| Component | Score (0–1) | Weight |
|---|---|---|
| Price history | `min(1, n ÷ 60)`, hourly closes in the last 30 days | 0.25 |
| Volume source | the mean over the 7 days of each day's score by how its chosen row observed it (`engine.volume.day_score`): id diffs 1.0 · group diffs 0.3 + 0.5 × min(1, hours observed ÷ 24) (a whole day 0.8, a 2 h sample 0.34) · your mail only 0.4 · listing turnover only or none 0 | 0.30 |
| Order book freshness | ≤ 2 h old 1.0 · ≤ 24 h old 0.5 · older or none 0.3 | 0.15 |
| Weekly pattern | `min(1, weeks of data ÷ 6)` for the window used. **Only for planned posts**; posting now leaves it out | 0.15 |
| Tier history | `min(1, n_tier ÷ 30)` | 0.15 |

- `conf = Σ (score × weight) ÷ Σ (weights of the components that apply)`, shown as a whole percent (rounded half up).
- Calibration (§8.8) also reports predicted versus actual sell rate by confidence band, so a too-optimistic confidence shows up.

### 3.4 Recommendation engine

**Market capacity is shared and counted once** (§3.3.6). Every quantity a recommendation proposes stays within what the market can absorb, across all your characters together:
- **Flips:** units bought ≤ the liquidity cap of the windows they'll sell in, minus units you already hold or have planned for that item.
- **Crafts:** the craft count already stops when an extra craft stops paying (the product's chance falls as more is posted); the planned output also counts against the product's cap.
- **Disenchant:** the **mats** from all disenchant buys together stay within each mat's cap for its sell windows; buys are taken best profit first until a mat's cap is reached.
- **Portfolio (§3.4C.4, §3.4D):** the allocation spends one shared capacity per item and window across characters, so two characters never plan to fill the same demand, and no more than `max_item_capital_share` (config, 0.25) of total gold goes into items whose supply/volume ratio is above `slow_market_days` (config, 7). The portfolio optimizer (§3.4D, built M10) spends this same ledger: every candidate claims the capacity of the windows it sells in, and selection funds claims only up to each window's liquidity cap (PL4, PF25).
- Volume estimates are uncertain inputs: caps use the estimate as given, and a low `volume_confidence` lowers the result's confidence (§3.3.5), never inflates it.

All money is net of fees. Estimates come with a confidence level.

- **Fees** (`engine/fees`): the AH cut is 5% at the faction AH. The deposit is `floor(vendor_sell × q × rate(duration))`, and it is refunded on sale (§3.3.2). The durations are 2, 8 and 24 h, as in Classic Era (verified on Forever 2026-10-18). The deposit rates are seeded in `fees.py` with Classic Era values and must still be verified in game (§6). The neutral AH (15% cut) is not planned in v1.
- **Post price** `p` = `min(curOthers − 1, max(med7, p90_30))` in copper when a competitor exists (PP1, PP2): you undercut the cheapest competitor, up to the highest price the market has recently traded at, and never post above a price the last 30 days never saw (`p90_30`, the nearest-rank 90th percentile of hourly closes; the market file's `p90`). The cap uses whichever of `med7` and `p90_30` is known, and there is none when both are nil. With fewer than `MIN_HOURLY_CLOSES` (24) hourly closes in the last 30 days the cap is `med7` alone when it is known (PP4, the same thin-data rule as a window's ceiling, TT23): a p90 of five closes is the outlier, so cheapest 10 g, med7 3 g, p90 20 g on 5 closes posts at 3 g, and at 9 g 99 s 99 c with 30 closes. Every caller passes the count (`closes_30d`, the market file's `closes30`; unknown keeps the full cap). With no competitor, `p` = `med7`, else `p90_30`. If none of them is known, the item has no price: it is skipped and flagged "no price". Examples: cheapest 1 g, med7 50 s, p90 80 s → 80 s; cheapest 1 g, med7 50 s, p90 2 g → 99 s 99 c; nobody else listing → 50 s. `curOthers` leaves out your own auctions, so you never undercut yourself.
- **Sell value** per unit, for things you don't hold yet (DE results, a craft's mats you'd buy) = `E_post(q, p) / q`, using the posting-now window (the next 8 hours).
  - `q` is the item's **reference stack**: the most common stack size in the newest book, with ties going to the smaller one. It is 1 when there is no book.
  - It already includes `P`, so mats that don't sell are worth less.
- **Hold value** per unit, for things you already hold = the sell plan's expected value for all the units you hold (§3.4C.1, best option per lot) ÷ units held.
  - Posting now is one of the options the sell plan compares, so the hold value is never below the sell value.
  - It is higher when waiting for a peak pays more. That is the real opportunity cost of using the item.
  - The sell plan is computed first, treating all owned stock as for sale. Crafts then price owned mats at their hold value, and the allocation (§3.4C.4) decides which units are crafted and which are sold.
- **Reagent cost** for `q` units = the cheaper of vendor buy × `q` and buying from the AH. AH cost walks the newest local book from the cheapest listing up. Without a book, it is `cur × q`, flagged "depth unknown" when `q > curQty`. Mats you already own are priced at their **hold value**, because that's the opportunity cost. Otherwise a craft would look profitable even when selling the mats raw, now or at their peak, pays more.
- **Liquidity.** Your units planned for one sell window are capped at `max(1 stack, floor(max_supply_share × others' listed units in that window))`, with `max_supply_share` config (0.5): you are at most about a third of the window's listings, so you never set the price or invite undercuts. Others' listed units in the window = current supply × the mean `supply_how` over the window's hours ÷ `supply_how` at the current hour (§3.3.6), so a window where the AH is usually fuller allows more (R17). With no supply pattern it is the current supply; with supply unknown the cap is 1 stack. **Gear** (weapons and armor, item class 2 and 4) is more lenient, since each piece is its own listing and an empty market is exactly when to list: its cap is `max(min_gear_units, floor(max_supply_share_gear × others' listed units))`, with config `max_supply_share_gear` 1.0 (you may double the market) and `min_gear_units` 2 (one or two pieces always go up, R18). There is no separate sales limit: the queue simulation already lowers `P` as you post more than buyers take. A result that needs more units is capped, not hidden; its `P` already reflects what's left.
- **Money on every action** is shown as both **each** (per unit) and **total** (each × quantity):

  | Action | Each | Total |
  |---|---|---|
  | buy, DE (buy to disenchant) | AH price per unit | what you pay |
  | post | listing price per unit | listing value |
  | craft | **gold to buy the mats you're missing**, per craft, at the latest AH prices (or vendor, if cheaper) | × number of crafts |
  | vendor | vendor price per unit | what the vendor pays |

  A craft shows "—" only when you already own every mat it needs. When owned mats are used, the row adds a note, e.g. "uses 6 owned Copper Bar (hold value 57 s)". Profit still counts their hold value, as above.

  **On Today and Week** (`plan_chains.row_money`, D62, A71) a craft row that carries its chain shows the chain's planned buys: **Total** = the gold of every mat buy in the chain (vendor or AH, as the chain planned them, every character's), **Each** = Total ÷ crafts, rounded half up (76 crafts with 15 s 20 c of vials and 57 s of fish: Each 95 c, Total 72 s 20 c); both "—" when the chain buys nothing. A flip's buy keeps its price each and its cost; a mail, an intermediate craft and a disenchant show "—".
- **Expected net** on an action is what doing it is expected to gain. **A chain's profit is counted once**, so day, week, category and character totals never count the same gold twice (VW1). A chain is a buy or craft, its linked follow-ups (`parent_id`), and any mat buys, intermediate crafts and mail steps that feed it.

  | Action | Expected net |
  |---|---|
  | buy to flip | `E_post` of its planned sale − buy cost |
  | DE buy | DE EV − buy cost |
  | craft (to sell or to DE) | `E_post` of the product (or its DE EV) − sourced mat cost, with owned mats at hold value |
  | mat buy, intermediate craft, mail | none ("for Bolt of Silk Cloth 20:30"); its cost is part of the final craft; on Today and Week a step by the chain row's own character is a line under that row, not a row (D62) |
  | post, DE or vendor of stock you hold | `E_post` (or DE EV, or vendor price × qty) − what you paid (`cost_basis`; 0 for found items) |
  | hold | none; holds are no Today or Week row (D64) |

  - **Which action carries it:** the chain's earliest action that the view shows, whether done or pending. The Craft pages count a planned chain the same way (`planned_crafts.chain_net`, `ViewModel.ChainNet`, D59): the profit step's net, and the posts' own nets only when no step carries one, so a planned craft's profit there equals its Today row's net. The follow-up then shows "profit counted on the Fri 14:10 buy" and adds 0 to the totals.
  - **When the earliest action leaves the view** (the rolling week has moved past its day), the follow-up becomes a sale of stock you hold and carries its own net, as in the last table row.
- **Risk %** = gold the action spends ÷ the total gold across all your characters (sum of `characters.gold`) × 100, shown to one decimal place.
  - The gold spent is the total for a buy or DE buy, the mat gold for a craft, and the deposit for a post (the only gold a post can lose). It is 0 for a vendor sale and for mail. On Today and Week a chain's row risks the chain's gold: its mat buys for a craft (D62), its buy for a flip; a post row risks its deposit (`fees.deposit_copper` of the item's vendor sell price, units and the post's duration; 0 when the vendor price or duration is unknown, e.g. a kept history row) in `plan_chains.row_money` and `ViewModel.RowMoney` alike (D62, A71).
  - Rows above `high_risk_pct` (25%) show their risk in amber.
  - **Built (M10): the portfolio exposure budget caps all the gold tied up at once** (§3.4D.1). One limit, `risk_tolerance` × the capital base, caps the gold needed by every funded action across every character, and the selection decides how it is shared (§3.4D.4). There is no per-buy cap: a flip's or a buy's auctions are batches, funded cheapest first while they fit the remaining capacity and the buyer's gold (R19); the plan's crafts are funded one craft at a time from the gold of the crafter `craft.gather` picks (C24); the Craft and Disenchant pages leave out what is bigger than the remaining capacity (D45, D46). Posting stock you already own needs only its deposit. `max_item_capital_share` and `slow_market_days` stay (market capacity, above). **Risk %** stays as a display: the gold the row needs ÷ all your gold. Since M12 the addon's own crafts stay within the character's live cap, min(its live gold, the snapshot's remaining capacity − the other characters' reservations), its live gold alone without a snapshot (A64, §4.0).

**A. Disenchant.** For each disenchantable item on the AH (AHF snapshot or own scans), whether or not one of your characters has the Enchanting skill to do it (research; funding needs one, below):

`EV = Σ P(mat)·E[qty]·sellValue(mat)` and `profit = EV − buy`. Here `P(mat)` is the drop chance from the DE table.

**Built (M15): research is never gated by profession** (D71, PF2; `engine/disenchant.targets` takes no skill, `recommend/pages.disenchant_page` adds `views.disenchant_notice`). Every disenchantable item is evaluated whether or not a character can disenchant it, and the Disenchant page lists it with its calculations, sorting, filters and details unchanged, under the notice "Research only: no character has Enchanting yet. These rows enter the funded plan once one does." Only funding needs a character with Enchanting at the item's skill (§3.4D.4, PF9, `disenchant.can_disenchant`, unchanged). R2 changed with it: the item is listed, but not funded. The addon's Disenchant tab already works this way (A23).

**B. Craft now.** For each recipe in `known_recipes` for any of your characters:

`profit = E_post(product, q = avgYield) − Σ reagentCost`

**Mat sourcing.** Every known recipe is evaluated, not only the ones your bags already cover. Each reagent unit comes from its cheapest source:
- **owned** (bags, bank or mail on any character), valued at its hold value;
- **AH**, walking the newest book from the cheapest listing up;
- **vendor**, for vendor-sold reagents;
- **crafted by you**: when one of your characters knows a recipe that makes this reagent. Its cost is that recipe's own sourced mat cost, worked out with these same four sources.

Owned mats are used first when they cost the same. If the AH price is below an owned mat's hold value (for example, it is worth more at its Saturday peak than the AH asks today), the plan buys from the AH and leaves your stock to the sell plan.

The result is shown as the row's **Source**. The base tells where the raw mats come from, and **+ crafted** is added when any reagent is made by you along the way:

| Source | Meaning | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Owned | every mat comes from your stock; no gold needed | Copper Rod from 6 Copper Bar in your bags |
| AH | every mat is bought (AH or vendor); you craft entirely from the market | Bolt of Silk Cloth from Silk Cloth bought on the AH |
| Mixed | your stock plus a top-up from the AH or vendor | Linen Belt: your Linen + bought Coarse Thread |
| … + crafted | one or more reagents are made by your characters first | Runed Copper Rod: Brugan crafts the Copper Rod from AH Copper Bar, mails it to Amaya, Amaya adds bought Strange Dust |

**Crafting chains** (the "crafted by you" source):
- **Profit is counted once.** An intermediate is valued at the cost of its own mats, so its profit lands on the final product. Leftovers from a random yield go to the sell plan.
- **Limits.** Chains are followed up to `max_craft_depth` (3) steps, and a recipe never feeds itself, so transmute loops are ignored. Every step checks that character's skill and cooldowns.
- **Which character.** Each step is made by a character who knows it. When the next step's crafter is someone else, the plan adds a **mail** action ("mail 1 Copper Rod Brugan → Amaya"). Mail between your own characters must be verified on Forever (§6).
- **Owned mats on another character are mailed** (`craft_plan.chain_actions`, C22). The final crafter is the one `craft.gather` picks, as on the Craft page (C19, D44): of the characters who know the recipe, the one who already holds the most of the owned mats the crafts use (ties to the first by id). Every owned mat another character holds becomes a `mail` action from that character (`char_id`, the sender) to the crafter (`to_char`), biggest holder first, a minute before the craft, in the craft's chain, with no net. The craft count is checked against the chosen crafter's budget and the chain's mat gold is charged to that same character (C24): the option is first worked out with the first crafter's budget to learn which owned mats it uses, and again with the chosen crafter's when `gather` picks someone else. The per-character stock the plan has not yet used (`CraftWorld.held`, from your lots) goes down by what each chain takes, the crafter's own units first, so two chains never mail the same bars. Owned raw mats of an intermediate are not mailed (only the final recipe's reagents are tracked per character).
- **Mail on its way counts as the recipient's stock** (`engine/transit`, `state.reads.transit`, T22, C23). A confirmed `mail_sent` event is on its way while it is newer than the recipient's newest mail and bags snapshots, the sender's bags snapshot is at or after it (before that the item is still counted in the sender's bags) and it is at most 30 days old (mail comes back; "now" is the newest send or snapshot of the characters involved, so the result depends on the data only). Its units are the recipient's owned stock (`owned_lots`, place `transit`), so a re-plan neither mails nor buys the mats again; Inventory lists them as a row in place "transit" whose plan cell starts "On its way from Brugan, mailed Mon 10:40 · " (several senders joined by " and "), and a craft row of the recipient whose product's recipes use the item gets the line "6 Copper Bar from Brugan · mailed Mon 10:40 · on its way", which survives a re-plan with new chain ids. It stops counting as soon as the recipient's mail or bags snapshot is newer than the send.
- **The row note shows the chain**, e.g. "Copper Rod: crafted by Brugan from 2 Copper Bar (AH) → mailed to Amaya".

- **How many crafts.** Crafts are added one at a time. Each extra craft must still make a profit: AH mats get pricier as the book is walked, and the product's chance to sell drops as more is posted. Crafts stop at the first one that would lose money, or at the budget, the AH depth, or the cooldown.
- **Missing mats.** A recipe with a mat that has no AH listing, no vendor and no stock is not craftable now. It is listed only if it is one craft short, as "missing: 2 Iron Bar".
- This includes craft → disenchant.
- It shows how many crafts the mats you already own can cover, and which character crafts each one (only characters who know the recipe).
- Recipes with a cooldown (transmutes, Mooncloth) are limited to one craft per cooldown per character.

**Today list.** The actions to do now are listed **at the plan's level** (`engine/plan_chains`, `plan_views.today_view`; `UI/PlanView.lua`; D62, D64, D66, A71, A73, A74):
- **One row per chain**: the action that carries the chain's profit (a flip's buy, a craft chain's final craft; a chain whose crafts carry no net is carried by its last craft). Its mat buys (AH and vendor) and intermediate crafts **by the same character** are lines under the row, in the Craft page's wording ("buy 76 Empty Vial from a vendor · 15s 20c", "buy 76 Raw Brilliant Smallfish on the AH · 57s", "craft 21 Tin Bar first"); then one line per incoming mail ("6 Copper Bar from Brugan · not mailed yet", "… · mailed Mon 10:40" once its mail_sent event matched, "… · partly mailed Mon 10:40", "… · skipped"); then when it sells ("then: post Fri 11:00, for the 19:00 peak"; "then: relist now" for a flip posting now; "then: post now" for a craft posting now; "then: disenchant it"). That last line replaces the note's former "Flip: …" / "Then: …" text. A step another character does stays a row of its own (they are the one who acts), with "for crafting …"; only the row's lines fold, the underlying actions are unchanged: `PortfolioPlan.lua`, the tracker, the AH panel's Shopping list and the Craft pages still read every buy.
- **Mail is the sender's row**: "MAIL 6 Copper Bar", note "to Amaya · for crafting Copper Rod", no net, no chance, risk 0. It is done when a mail_sent event matches it (§3.4.1), and a done row leaves Today (Week shows it ticked). The crafter's craft row carries the incoming line; once the send is confirmed (A75) and the sender's bags are read, the re-plan counts the mats as hers on their way (§3.4B, T22), plans no mail, and her row says "6 Copper Bar from Brugan · mailed Mon 10:40 · on its way" (C23) until her mail or bags snapshot holds them.
- **Holds and vendor sales are no rows** (D64): they add nothing to any day, week, category or character total. They stay in the plan, in `PortfolioPlan.lua` and on Inventory (its plan cell still says "Hold for a later week" / "Vendor"), and vendoring stays the floor unsold units are valued at. A vendor *buy* for a craft stays, as a line under its craft ("from a vendor").
- Posts stay rows (they are what you do at the AH, and the AH panel's Post list reads them).

The rows are sorted by **expected net, highest first**. Ties go to the higher `P'`, then the item name A–Z, then the character name A–Z, so the order is always the same (§8.6). Rows with `P'` below `min_sell_prob` (config 0.5) are hidden, and the footer says how many. **A row whose item the intelligence marks "volume unknown" is never hidden by that filter**, at any threshold (D58, A68): its chance is a default, not a measurement, so it keeps its place in the expected-net order and its chance cell carries the "volume unknown" marker (the dashboard's note under the chance; the addon's Volume cell "—", as on every tab). The footer's count is of rows the filter hid. Both sides take the flag from the intelligence (`ItemIntel.volume_unknown`; the market file's `volumeUnknown`), so the plan file's format is unchanged. You can change the threshold from a dropdown on the dashboard and in the addon. Items flagged "not selling" go in a separate **Needs a decision** section under the list.

**C. Weekly plan** (a rolling 7 days, hour resolution). It always starts at the current hour today and covers the next 7 × 24 hours, so the first column is always today. Earlier hours of today show what was done or missed.

1. **Sell plan for what you own** (all characters: bags, bank and mail; bound items are left out, and items already on the AH are shown as "listed" rather than planned again). For each item, it compares `E_post` for:
   - post now at `p`
   - post for the best slot in the next 7 days, at price `level × how[slot] × factor`, clamped to `[p10_30, max(med7, p90_30)]` when known (TT22: a forecast never above what the last 30 days traded at), the ceiling being the level itself with fewer than 24 hourly closes in 30 days (TT23: a p90 of a few closes is an outlier), `factor = clamp(1 + trend7/100 × days ahead, 1 − max_trend_total, 1 + max_trend_total)`, the level being `med7`, else `med30` (R21). `trend7` is clamped to ±5% per day, and the whole factor to ±`max_trend_total` (config, 0.15), so a week of thin data can't move a 7-day forecast 35% (`plan.slot_price`, TT6, TT7). The auction duration is chosen so the listing covers the peak.
   - **Every post action is scheduled `post_lead_hours` (8) before the best hour of its target selling window**, not at it. That is the same 8 hours used for posting now. Its chance to sell is computed from the post through the end of the window (§3.3.2), and a window that ends with no post is a missed peak (§3.4.0). The row says which peak it is for, e.g. "Sat 10:00 · post · for the 18:00 peak". If that time has already passed, the post is planned for now.
   - disenchant (if a character can)
   - vendor
   - hold beyond 7 days (only flagged, never planned; shown on Inventory, not on Today or Week, D64)

   It picks the option with the highest expected value, except that **a window must beat posting now by a margin that shrinks as its pattern earns trust** (R26, PL5). `required_uplift(weeks) = window_margin × (1 − min(1, weeks ÷ pattern_full_weeks))`, with config `window_margin` 0.15 and `pattern_full_weeks` 6 (the "established" strength, §3.3.4), `weeks` being the window's weeks of data. A window is eligible only when `E_window ≥ E_now × (1 + required_uplift)` (`E_now + required_uplift × |E_now|` when `E_now` is negative), `E_now` being posting now's expected value for the same units; without a posting-now option with room (no post price, or its cap is full) the windows compete among themselves. Among eligible options the highest expected value wins, ties to the earlier post time. So with 1 week of data a window needs +12.5% (E_now 100: 110 posts now, 120 takes the window), and from 6 weeks any gain will do (101). The same rule (`plan.choose_option`) decides between posting now and a window in flips (§3.4C.2) and crafts (§3.4C.3). An item with fewer than 24 hourly closes in 30 days has no window to choose (§3.3.4, PF30): it posts now. Large stacks are split across slots so that what you post for each window stays within the liquidity cap (§3.4).
   - **How it allocates** (`engine/plan.py`): each character's lot, in character order, is split into reference stacks; a character's last remainder may be smaller (PL2). Each stack goes to the post option with the highest expected value that still has room in its window, a window counting only when it beats posting now by its required uplift (R26). When no post this week pays more per unit than disenchanting or vendoring (no window has room left, or one more stack would mostly not sell), it is a **hold** while the AH price after the cut still pays more than either, so stock waits for later weeks instead of being dumped; otherwise it is disenchanted or vendored.
   - **A post's chance** is the queue simulation over its hours (§3.3.2), with `sales_how` as demand, today's book as the competition, and your own units already planned for the same window ahead of you. Your sell record is added once it is tracked (M6b, §3.3.3).
   - **The duration** is the shortest one that keeps the listing up through the window's best hour.
2. **Buy to flip**: items whose expected profit (`E_post` of posting now or at an upcoming peak slot − buy cost) is at least `min_flip_margin` (20%) × buy cost. Typical sources are a weekday or hour pattern, a dip below `p10_30`, or one stack dumped below the next price level (relisted now, R24). They are ranked by expected profit × `conf` (as a fraction).
   - **How a flip is built** (`engine/flips.py`): it buys other sellers' auctions now, cheapest first, and posts each one where it is worth most, with the auctions it bought taken out of the competition: **posting now** or a selling window. Posting now is priced for the book left after the buy (`flips.relisted`: `relist_price` of the cheapest listing left, `med7`, `p90_30` and the close count, after your record. It is `post_price` (PP4) when a listing is left; with none, the level is a price only with at least 24 hourly closes in 30 days, and otherwise posting now has no room, so the flip ends before that auction, R28. When not even the first auction is grounded, the flip is listed excluded "No listing left to price against" (§3.4D.6); the addon's live flip-now rows use the same `Prices.RelistPrice`), with the posting-now window's chance and duration. An auction is added only while it fits the spend limit, an option with room, and its own expected profit is at least `min_flip_margin` × its cost; prices only rise along the book, so the first one that fails ends the flip. A window still wins when it pays more (R24) by at least its required uplift over relisting now (§3.4C.1, R26), and an item without a selling window can still flip now. A flip whose item has fewer than 24 hourly closes in 30 days has no window to sell at (§3.3.4, PF30): it relists now at the thin-data post price (PP4), so thin data never funds a flip on a forecast premium; a window forecast that does run on thin data (a Buy for later's) is at most the level (TT23). A flip's post must beat disenchanting or vendoring the stock: an auction joins only when its chosen option's expected value per unit exceeds that per-unit value (as in the sell plan), else it is a disenchant buy, not a flip (R27). Example (R24): Linen 1 × 20 at 10 c below 5 × 20 at 50 c, med7 48 c, p90 55 c, P 0.9: buy the 10 c stack and post it now at 49 c, expected 0.9 × 20 × 49 × 0.95 + 0.1 × (20 × 10 − 10) = 856.9 c against 200 c; with the next level at 50 c and the cheapest at 45 c it is no flip (856.9 < 1.2 × 900, R25).
   - **The actions**: a `buy` now and its `post`s, chain `flip:<key>`; a post for now has no window (`window_start_ms` and `peak_ms` empty), is due now and lasts the shortest duration covering the posting-now window. Like any post planned for now, the status pass marks it missed 24 h after its post time (§3.4.0). The Flip page's row says "relist now, under the listings left" for it.
   - **Who buys**: each item gets at most one flip, bought by the character with the most spendable gold (ties: character id), so two characters never fill the same demand (R16). Each auction is a batch of the flip's candidate (§3.4D.3); selection funds them in order while they fit that character's gold and the remaining capacity (R9, R19). The flips' posts share the windows' capacity with your own stock (PL4).
   - The buy carries the chain's expected profit; each post also records its own net against what its units cost, for when the buy has left the view (§3.4 expected net).
3. **Craft to sell later**: buy mats on cheap slots, craft, and post at the product's peak slot. It uses all three sourcing paths (B).
   - **Mats to buy appear as their own buy actions, scheduled before the craft** and linked to it by `parent_id`, e.g. "20:15 buy 8 Silk Cloth → for Bolt of Silk Cloth". A mat buy can be on an earlier, cheaper slot.
   - **A crafting chain is a linked sequence**: buy raw mats → craft the intermediate → mail it (if the next crafter is another character) → craft the product → post.
   - If any step is missed or only partly done, the steps after it are re-planned for what you actually hold. An intermediate you already made counts as owned stock.
   - **Every planned craft has a follow-up for its product within 7 days after the craft**, linked by `parent_id`. It is a post, or a disenchant for craft → DE.
   - The follow-up also has to fall inside the plan window. A craft whose product has no worthwhile slot in time is not planned.
   - This applies to crafts listed for right now (B) as well. When the craft is done, the follow-up stays planned (§3.4.1).
   - **How chains are built** (`engine/craft_plan.py`): each recipe's output is valued at the product's best selling window in the 7 days (or posting now) that still has room in the shared capacity, a window beating posting now only by its required uplift (§3.4C.1, R26); output no window has room for is worth nothing, so the craft count stops where capacity ends. Chains are generated best expected profit × confidence first, each from the owned mats and AH units the better ones left, so two chains never plan the same bars; flips buy from the auctions the crafts left. Each craft is a batch funded from its crafter's gold within the remaining capacity (§3.4D.4, C24).
   - **Steps:** each intermediate's crafter buys that intermediate's mats (none when it is made from your own stock), the final crafter (`craft.gather`'s choice, C22) buys the rest, then the intermediate crafts and their mail, then the mail of owned mats other characters hold (§3.4B, C22), then the craft, a minute apart from now; the post follows at the product's window. The craft carries the chain's expected profit; mat buys, intermediate crafts and mail carry none (D22). On Today and Week the chain is one row (§3.4B Today list).
4. **Capital and time limits**: a budget per character (default: the character's gold, overridable in config `[budgets]`) and an optional maximum number of actions per day (`max_actions_per_day`, 0 = no limit). The allocation also spends each owned unit only once, either on a craft or in the sell plan (§3.4 hold value).

Output is a calendar of **actions**: `when · character · action (buy/craft/post/DE/vendor/mail/hold) · item · qty · each · total · expected net · P' · risk % · confidence`. The dashboard's Week view shows it like Today (`plan_views.week_view`; D63, D64, D65): one card per chain on its carrier's day with its steps as lines, posts as cards of their own on their day (a chain's follow-up post says "profit counted on the Mon 10:38 craft" while that craft is in view), mail as the sender's card, and no holds or vendor sales. Each dashboard card names its character (short form, D65). The addon's Week tab was replaced by the Portfolio tab's Next 7 days at M13 (§4.3). Totals are shown per day, per category and per character, over the cards shown.

**Building and storing the plan** (`engine/week.py`, `engine/candidates.py`, `engine/craft_candidates.py`, `engine/allocation.py`, `recommend/portfolio.py`, `recommend/plan.py`; built M10). Each full refresh and each re-plan plans each market's characters together: a first sell plan of everything you own gives each item's hold value (`week.prepare`); the candidates are generated in the old order, so each sizes itself behind the posts generated before it (crafts best first, then your stock, then flips from the auctions the crafts left, then buys for later), and the portfolio optimizer funds the best affordable set (§3.4D). Every unit you hold that no funded batch uses is a hold, a disenchant or a vendor sale, as before. Facts are built only for items the plan can use: your stock, your recipes' products and mats, and **flip candidates** (`recommend.plan.flip_candidate`): items whose best peak hour (the price level × its price ratio × the most a week of trend can add, after the cut) could beat the cheapest auction by `min_flip_margin` (`window_candidate`), or whose book's second price level `p2` could: `(p2 − 1) × (1 − cut) × chance ≥ (1 + min_flip_margin) × cur`, `chance` being the item's posting-now chance for one reference stack (`now_candidate`, R24, R25). Neither admits a suspect `cur` (FM5). The market file's flip offers (EX11) still come from window candidates only, so its format is unchanged. Rule `ignore` also takes the item out of every craft as a mat. The plan is staged in `plan_new` and published with the intelligence and pages in one transaction; it replaces the market's characters' `pending` and `late` actions, while `done`, `partial`, `missed` and `skipped` ones stay as history (§3.4.1). A chain's later steps name its first action as their parent.

#### 3.4D The portfolio optimizer

**Built: the data contracts** (M9, 2026-10-19: the candidate record, the accounting, the new config keys and the `alternatives` table, `engine/portfolio.py`, PF1, PF3, PF8, PF22, PF23) **and the optimizer** (M10, 2026-10-19: candidates as marginal batches `engine/candidates.py` and `engine/craft_candidates.py`, the timing premium `engine/timing.py`, selection with one swap pass, validation and exclusion reasons `engine/selection.py`, the pipeline `engine/allocation.py` and `recommend/portfolio.py`; PF2, PF4–PF7, PF9–PF21, PF24–PF27). The funded selection is the plan: it replaced §3.4C.4's allocation. **Built: the exports** (M11, 2026-10-19: the candidate universe and the dependency graph in `MarketIntelligence.lua`, the portfolio snapshot and positions in `PortfolioPlan.lua`, `engine/graph.py`, `export/portfolio_lua.py`, D.10, §3.5; with buying windows ranked by actionable value and every window's forecast, §3.3.6; W14, W15, EX17, EX18, FM40). **Built: the addon's live adaptation** (M12, 2026-10-19: graph repricing `Engine/Graph.lua` with its twin in `engine/graph.py`, the live reselection `Recommend/Reselect.lua` and `Recommend/Adapt.lua`, the Portfolio tab's view-model `UI/PortfolioView.lua`, §4.0; DG1–DG7, A76–A78, A80, A83, FM39). **Built: the dashboard's Portfolio page and risk dropdown** (M13's dashboard half, 2026-10-19: `engine/portfolio_view.py`, `recommend/portfolio_page.py`, `POST /api/risk_tolerance`, §3.5; D72, D73, D77–D79, SEC11) and the addon's Portfolio tab (M13). **Built: positions and reconciliation** (M14, 2026-10-19: `engine/positions.py`, `engine/position_views.py`, `state/positions.py`, `recommend/positions_pass.py`, the addon's local positions in `Core/Plan.lua`; D.9; PO1–PO13, A81, FM38). The rest comes with M15 (§7). It turns §3.4A–C into one decision: **which combination of actions, across all your characters, Today and the next 7 days together, makes the most timing-adjusted expected net profit without tying up more gold than your risk tolerance allows.** It doesn't sort by return on investment, and it doesn't show each list's best trades on their own. A profitable trade that isn't funded is shown as an alternative, with the reason it was left out.

**The funded selection is the plan** (built M10). Its actions replace §3.4C.4's allocation in `actions` and `PortfolioPlan.lua` (pending and late, as before, in the same action kinds, chain ids and file format), so Today, Week, the Craft and Flip pages and the addon all read one plan. Unfunded candidates are stored in `alternatives` with their reason and list, never as actions; the pages that show them come with M13.

It extends what is built. Where it needs something that already exists, it reuses it:

| It needs | Already built as |
|---|---|
| owned materials valued once, at what they could fetch, never free | hold value = opportunity cost (§3.4 hold value, reagent cost) |
| one market capacity per item and window, shared by all characters | §3.4 market capacity and the §3.4C.4 ledger (PL4) |
| a multistage strategy funded once, as one plan | chain profit counted once (§3.4 expected net, VW1, CI3); crafting chains (§3.4B) |
| mail between characters as a task, and mail on its way as the recipient's | mail is the sender's row (§3.4B Today list); matching (§3.4.1, T21); transit (§3.4B, T22) |
| live prices that create opportunities outside buy windows | flip now (§3.4C.2, R24, A69) and the addon's live repricing (§4.0) |
| sunk costs are not avoidable costs | "below what you paid" is still planned (§3.4.1 follow-up, T7) |
| never infer a sale from missing inventory | untracked changes (§2.2.1, FM21, D51) |
| idempotent, deduplicated events | event ids (§5.2 rule 2) |
| no history rebuild when only eligibility or risk change | the incremental re-plan from the published intelligence (§3.1.1, IU13) |
| the same numbers on the desktop and in the addon | shared scenarios (§8.1) and LP1 |
| Disenchant visible without an enchanter, in the addon | A23 |

The examples below use the mockups' world: Monday 10:38, Amaya 21 g and Brugan 10 g 40 s in hand (31 g 40 s), 4 g 18 s committed in positions, risk tolerance 30%.

**D.1 Accounting and the risk budget** (PF8, PF22, PF23).

| Term | Definition | Mockup |
|---|---|---|
| Cash gold | `characters.gold` summed over your characters, each at its last snapshot | 31 g 40 s |
| Spendable gold of a character | its `[budgets]` value when set (§3.6), else its gold | Brugan 10 g 40 s |
| Committed capital | the remaining cost basis of open positions (D.9): what you paid for the units still held, listed, in the mail, or sold with their gold still waiting in the mailbox | 4 g 18 s |
| Capital base | cash gold + committed capital | 35 g 58 s |
| Risk budget (exposure limit) | capital base × `risk_tolerance` (config, 0.30) | 10 g 67 s 40 c |
| Remaining capacity | max(0, risk budget − committed capital) | 6 g 49 s 40 c |
| Available new investment | min(remaining capacity, spendable gold − the gold already reserved by funded actions not yet executed, deposits included) | 6 g 49 s 40 c |

- The pasted spec's example: gold 2,000 g, committed 250 g, 30% → base 2,250 g, budget 675 g, available 425 g (PF8).
- **Exposure is cost, never the expected loss.** Gold in stock or on the AH is locked until the item sells (at least the 2 h shortest auction, usually a day) and then until the payout is taken from the mailbox. A funded row's exposure is its **gold needed**: its buys, the mats it buys and its deposits, all from the acting character. A post of stock you own needs only its deposit. The gold moves into committed capital when it is spent (a position opens, D.9). A unit's cost leaves it only when its payout is **collected** (T17), at the moment the gold reaches cash gold, so the capital base never dips while gold waits in the mailbox. Mageweave Cloth, 25 bought for 30 s with 15 sold and their 25 s 65 c not yet collected, still commits all 30 s: 12 s for the 10 held and 18 s for the 15 sold.
- **Cost basis, not mark-to-market.** A position counts what you paid, not today's price.
- **Found items** (cost 0: looted, farmed, held before tracking) add nothing to the capital base, the risk budget or spendable gold. Their worth shows only as expected profit and in Inventory's Worth. Their opportunity cost as a craft mat is unchanged: the hold value (PF22).
- **Gold stays on its character.** A row is funded from the gold of the character who acts, and the plan never moves gold between characters (items are still mailed, §3.4B). A row the account could afford but its character can't is "Insufficient gold on <character>" (D.6).
- **A shown suggestion reserves nothing durably.** Gold for funded but unexecuted rows is reserved only inside one allocation run; the next run starts again from what you hold. Only a confirmed purchase or craft commits gold (a position, D.9).
- **No reinvestment of projected proceeds.** v1 funds only from gold in hand: a sale planned for Thursday doesn't fund a Friday buy.
- `max_item_capital_share` and `slow_market_days` (§3.4 market capacity) still cap slow markets inside the budget.
- **Built (M14): committed capital is the open positions' cost left** (D.9). Each plan run replays the positions from their lots and the journal (`recommend.positions_pass.current`, the positions table being its only source of what was committed), keeps those of the market's characters, and passes `engine.positions.committed` (one `OpenPosition` per position not Completed, its `cost_left`) to the accounting. A sold unit's cost leaves only when its payout is collected (PO4, PO11); found stock and purchases that matched no funded row commit nothing (PF22, PO12). It replaced M10's interim rule (every held unit at the account-wide cost basis).
- **Built (M9)** as `engine.portfolio.accounting(chars, positions, risk_tolerance, reserved)` → `Accounting` (cash gold, committed capital, capital base, risk budget, remaining capacity, reserved, capacity left, spendable and reserved per character, available new investment), with `available_for(char)` for one character's next row (PF23) and `committed_share_pct` (PF8's 12%). The risk budget is integer: the tolerance is taken in whole thousandths, `floor(x × 1000 + 0.5)`, and the budget is `floor(base × permille ÷ 1000)`, so float error never loses a copper and Lua gets the same number. Capacity left is `max(0, remaining capacity − Σ reserved)`, and available new investment is `max(0, min(capacity left, spendable − reserved))`. Positions are passed in as `OpenPosition` (just their cost left; `engine.positions.committed` since M14).
- **Risk tolerance** is `risk_tolerance` in config.toml. The Portfolio page's dropdown (10–50%, §3.5) writes it through `POST /api/risk_tolerance` and re-runs the allocation without reading history (SEC11, D79): only selection changes, never the candidates (PF1). **Built (M13)**: `config.with_risk_tolerance` (the pure rewrite of that one line) and `config.write_risk_tolerance` (temp file, read-back check, rename); the running app takes the new value (`__main__.LiveConfig`), so the scheduler's next refresh and re-plan use it too.

**D.2 The timing premium** (PF17–PF20).

Profit later is worth less than profit now: it ties gold up longer, and the forecast may miss. So every candidate is scored by its **adjusted profit** = expected net ÷ (1 + premium):

- **wait** = the days from now to the expected sale hour, which is the start of the selling window the sale is planned in ("now" for a post now). It is fractional, rounded half up to 0.01 d: `floor(minutes × 100 ÷ 1440 + 0.5) ÷ 100`.
- **premium** = 0 when wait × 24 ≤ `immediate_hours` (config 8, the posting-now window; 24 is allowed), else `premium_base` (0.15) + `premium_per_day` (0.05) × wait: 20% at 1 day, 30% at 3 days, 50% at 7 days.
- Adjusted profit is a float (§3.3), rounded half up only when shown.

Examples:
- 40 s now against 55 s in exactly 3 days: 55 s ÷ 1.30 = 42 s 31 c, so waiting wins (PF17).
- At Mon 10:38, Thick Leather sells in the Thu 19:00–22:00 window. The wait is 4,822 min ÷ 1,440 = 3.349 → 3.35 d, so the premium is 15% + 5% × 3.35 = 31.75%, and 55 s ÷ 1.3175 = 41 s 75 c. That still beats the 40 s Silver Rod Brugan can make today (PF18).
- A post for tonight's 18:00 peak waits 0.31 d (7.37 h; 7.44 h after rounding), inside the 8 h window, so its premium is 0 (PF19).

Two rules come with it:
- **The premium is the only timing rule.** No extra rule says weekly rows must beat the best Today row, and neither list has a fixed count: Today and the week compete in one pool.
- **Built (M10)** as `engine/timing.py` (`wait_hundredths`, `immediate`, `premium_micro`, `adjusted`, `adjusted_profit`) with its Lua twin `Engine/Timing.lua` (shared scenarios `tests/scenarios/timing.json`, PF17–PF19; in the addon's .toc since M12, where the live reselection scores with it). The wait is kept in whole hundredths of a day, `floor((minutes × 200 + 1440) ÷ 2880)` with minutes = whole minutes from now to the sale, never below 0; the premium in whole millionths, `floor(base × 1e6 + 0.5) + floor(per_day × 1e4 + 0.5) × hundredths`; adjusted = net × 1e6 ÷ (1e6 + premium), exactly the net when the premium is 0 (PF26). Integer steps keep Python and Lua equal. Where a batch could sell now or at a window, generation picks the eligible option with the best adjusted value (`plan.choose_option`'s value hook), after the trust gate.
- **Trust and time are different axes, and both apply.** The premium prices *time*. Whether a window's forecast can be *trusted* is still §3.4C.1's gate (R26: `window_margin`, `pattern_full_weeks`): a window that doesn't beat posting now by its required uplift is not eligible, whatever its adjusted profit. The pasted spec's §7.4 drops fixed counts and the "beat Today" rule; it does not drop the trust gate. A **Buy for later** candidate (D.3) has no posting-now alternative to beat, because it exists only for its window, so it needs that window to be at least a "pattern" (3 weeks of data, §3.3.4). Both cases are excluded as "Forecast too uncertain (N weeks of data)" (D.6), and a window sale of an item with fewer than 24 hourly closes in 30 days as "Forecast too uncertain (N closes in 30 d, needs 24)" (PF30). Greater Magic Essence's Sunday peak, seen in 1 week, is excluded that way (PF20).

**D.3 Candidates (stage A: generation)** (PF1–PF4).

Candidates are generated broadly, **independently of your risk tolerance and of which professions your characters have**:

| Strategy (the Strategy column) | Built from |
|---|---|
| Flip | §3.4C.2, flip now included (R24) |
