| Buy for later | new: buy in a top buy window, or now when the price is below `p10_30`, hold, and sell at a top selling window (Silk Cloth "below its 10th percentile now"; Greater Magic Essence in its Wednesday buy window) |
| Craft | §3.4B and §3.4C.3: craft, then post now or at a window |
| Multistage craft | a crafting chain (§3.4B), mail steps included, as one candidate |
| Disenchant | §3.4A, for every disenchantable item, enchanter or not |
| Sell stock | the sell plan's post options for what you own (§3.4C.1) |

Buying mats for a later craft is part of the Craft and Multistage candidates (mat buys on earlier, cheaper slots, C7).

- **Marginal batches.** Each opportunity is split into batches of one reference stack, or one craft, each worth less than the one before. Batch 2 of a flip buys the next auctions up the book and sells into what is left of the window, exactly as the flip, sell-plan and craft loops already add one stack or craft at a time (§3.4C.1 allocation, §3.4C.2, §3.4B "How many crafts"). The first batch that would lose money ends the opportunity.
- **Alternative routes are separate candidates for the same output**: buy the reagent or craft it, post now or at each window. The capacity, material and gold constraints let only one of them use a resource (PF4).
- **What generation may leave out**: recipes no character knows (crafts only: disenchants are never left out for that), vendor-sold items as posts (R23), suspect prices (FM5), unknown-suffix keys (§2.2.1), items whose rule is `ignore` or `no_buy` (§3.4.2), and "not selling" items as new stock (§3.3.3). Thin data lowers confidence, which the selection uses; it doesn't hide a candidate. **Built (M10, PF28)**, to keep the re-plan inside its budget, generation also leaves out: (a) a Buy for later whose item's best selling window has fewer than `pattern_full_weeks` ÷ 2 weeks of data (the trust gate would exclude it; "Forecast too uncertain" remains for a younger window chosen after the buy); (b) any flip, buy for later or disenchant of an item whose newest price is more than 24 h old; (c) every flip and buy for later past the best `candidate_pool` (config, 400, ≥ `candidate_export`): each passing item gets its first batch, the first batches are ranked by adjusted profit per copper as the fill orders them (ties: candidate id), and only the best `candidate_pool` get their later batches. What is left out here never enters the universe and is no alternative; the universe is still bigger than what is exported (PF3) since `candidate_pool` ≥ `candidate_export`.
- **A candidate's fields**:
  - a stable id, `<strategy>:<key>:<charId>:<route>:<batch>`, unchanged between runs while those parts don't change;
  - strategy, input and output keys and quantities, and the route (each reagent's source, §3.4B);
  - gold needed and its parts (buys, mats, deposits), and the cost basis of bought stock it uses;
  - expected net (§3.4 expected net: fees, deposits, the chance it sells, unsold units at their fallback), the chance `P'`, the executable quantity (the liquidity room it fits in) and confidence;
  - the acting character and what it needs (profession, skill, recipe, cooldown);
  - its buy time and expected sale hour;
  - the resources it claims: owned units by character and place, and capacity per item and window;
  - the intelligence's `built_ms` and the method versions it was computed with.
- **The universe is bigger than what is shown.** Every candidate is evaluated; the export keeps the best `candidate_export` (150): the funded ones first, then the best alternatives by adjusted profit (PF3, D.10).
- **Built (M10)** as `engine/candidates.py` (Sell stock, Flip, Buy for later) and `engine/craft_candidates.py` (Craft, Multistage craft, Disenchant), the universe assembled by `engine.allocation.generate`, each strategy from the engine that already sizes it one step at a time: **Sell stock** = each reference stack of `plan.stack_choices` (the sell plan's per-stack loop) that posts; **Flip** = each auction of `flips.flip_steps` (build_flip's loop), flip now included; **Buy for later** = a flip bought below `p10_30` whose first sale is at a window, and, new, a reference stack bought at an upcoming buy window's slot price (`plan.slot_price`) and sold at the selling window after it with the best adjusted value, while each stack pays `min_flip_margin` and fits the window's cap; **Craft** and **Multistage craft** = each craft of `craft.craft_option` (its `CraftStep`s: gold, owned and AH mats, intermediates), crafted by `craft.gather`'s choice, Multistage when it crafts an intermediate or mails mats, its mail steps part of it (PF12); **Disenchant** = each auction of every disenchantable item on the AH, enchanter or not (`recommend.portfolio.de_infos`, PF2). A recipe with crafted reagents gets two routes when they differ (intermediates crafted, every mat bought), which claim one shared unit so only one is funded (PF4). Candidates are generated in the plan's old order with one generation ledger, so a later candidate queues behind the posts before it and crafts never plan the same owned mats; selection still decides, through the claims (owned units `own:<char>:<key>`, a window's capacity `cap:<key>:<window>`, AH units `ah:<key>`). A batch's gold, deposits and capacity claims telescope, so the funded prefix of an opportunity always sums to what its realized actions use. Not generated in M10: crafts missing a mat nobody sells (the Craft page lists them, D46). Owned units are claimed per character with place `stock` (bags, bank and mail together, as a lot).
- **Built (M9)**: the record is `engine.portfolio.Candidate`, a frozen dataclass with `strategy` (a code: `flip`, `buy_later`, `craft`, `multistage`, `disenchant`, `sell_stock`; `label` gives the Strategy column's text), `key`, `char_id`, `route`, `batch`, `out_qty`, `inputs` ((key, units) by key), `gold_buys`, `gold_mats`, `gold_deposits` (`gold_needed` is their sum), `cost_basis`, `expected_net` (float), `chance`, `exec_qty`, `conf`, `needs` (`Requirements`: profession, skill, spell_id, cooldown_s), `buy_ms`, `sale_ms`, `owned_claims` (`OwnedClaim`: char, place, key, units), `capacity_claims` (`CapacityClaim`: key, window start ms or None for now, as the §3.4C.4 ledger keys them, units), `built_ms` and `method_versions`. It has no risk field (PF1). `cand_id` is built by `candidate_id` from its parts, so the two can't disagree. The **route** is `<reagents>/<buy>/<sale>`: reagents as `<key><letters>` joined by `+`, by key, the letters being the reagent's sources in the order `o` owned, `v` vendor, `a` AH, `c` crafted by you, or `-` with no reagents; buy and sale as `-` (none), `now` (at or before the run's now, so a "now" route doesn't change every run) or `h<hour>` (`floor(ms ÷ 3,600,000)`). For example `craft:6217:Brugan-Grommash:2840c/-/now:1`. Id parts may not hold `:`, `;`, `,` or `|`. `candidate_to_json` writes it with sorted keys, its id and a schema version (`CANDIDATE_SCHEMA_VERSION` 1), and `candidate_from_json` refuses a newer version or an id that doesn't match its parts. `export_subset(candidates, funded_ids, adjusted, limit)` keeps the funded in funding order, then the rest by adjusted profit (`timing.adjusted` since M10), ties by id (PF3). Since M11 (`CANDIDATE_SCHEMA_VERSION` 2) a candidate also carries `window_end_ms`, when the selling window it sells in ends (None selling now; a version-1 record reads it as None), for the export's `windowEndMs`; `input_sources` names each input's source for the export, from the route's reagent letters (own, vendor, ah, craft, joined by `+` when a reagent has several), sell stock's own units and a disenchant's AH buy; `config_version` is §3.6's.

**D.4 Selection (stage B)** (PF5–PF16).

1. **Eligibility.** Each candidate is checked against its acting character: profession and skill, recipe known, cooldown ready, owned materials (a unit claimed by a better candidate is gone), room in the capacity ledger, and the trust gate (D.2). A failure is an exclusion reason (D.6). The candidate stays in the universe and is checked again at the next run, or live in game after a learned recipe or a skill-up (PF10).
2. **Greedy fill.** Eligible batches are taken in order of **adjusted profit per copper of exposure** (gold needed). Batches that need no gold come first, by adjusted profit. Ties go to the candidate id. A batch is funded when:
   - its gold needed fits the remaining capacity and its character's spendable gold left;
   - its list has room (`today_limit` 20, `week_limit` 20);
   - its resources are still free;
   - and, for batch n + 1 of an opportunity, batch n is funded.

   Otherwise it is excluded with the first reason that fails, in D.6's order.
3. **One swap pass.** It takes the funded batches in funding order, as they stood when the pass began. For each one, it tries each batch excluded for capacity, gold or a list limit, in step 2's order: remove the funded batch, add the excluded one, then refill greedily (step 2) from the batches still excluded. The swap is kept when the total adjusted profit rises; ties go to the earlier candidate id. A swap never leaves batch n + 1 of an opportunity funded without batch n. There is one pass, so the run is bounded: at most (funded × excluded) refills.
4. **Today and Next 7 days.** A funded row whose wait is within the immediate window is **Today**; every other row is **Next 7 days** (horizon `horizon_days`, 7). A flip bought now and sold on a later day is Next 7 days (Mithril Spurs: buy now, sell Saturday). The list limits are selection constraints, so the funded allocation always fits what is shown and no gold is reserved for a hidden row (PF13).
5. **Validation** (D.5), then publish.

**Built (M10)** as `engine/selection.py` (`select`, `fill_order`, `check`, `validate`) over language-neutral `Pick`s (gold needed, adjusted profit, list, character, claims, the static reason) with its Lua twin `Engine/Portfolio.lua` (shared scenarios `tests/scenarios/portfolio.json`, PF5, PF11, PF15, PF24, PF27, PF29, PF30; in the .toc since M12, run by the addon's live reselection, §4.0). The fill order is `(0, −adjusted)` for a batch that needs no gold, else `(1, −adjusted ÷ gold)`, then the candidate id; within an opportunity a batch's key is the worse of its own and its predecessor's, and the places an opportunity's batches take are filled in batch order, so batch n + 1 never comes first. A row (an opportunity) takes a list slot with its first funded batch and keeps its first batch's list. The swap pass tries batches excluded for a claimed resource (owned units or a window's capacity, the ledger "capacity"), gold on the character, a list limit or the remaining capacity; Python skips a swap only when an upper bound proves it can't win (exact pruning, checked by a test), Lua tries them all. **Bounds (PF29, both languages):** the swap pass tries only the best `swap_candidates` (config, 50) of those excluded batches by adjusted profit (ties: candidate id), in fill order; and the fill stops once both lists are full and the capacity left is below the least gold any remaining batch needs, after which the remaining batches get their status without the claims check (a new row "Recommendation limit reached", a later batch "Exceeds risk tolerance"). Each excluded batch's status is the first failing check against the final allocation; a later batch whose predecessor isn't funded takes its predecessor's status.

**Selection order and display order differ.** Rows are *selected* by adjusted profit per copper of exposure, then the swap pass; the Portfolio page's funding card *shows* the funded rows by adjusted profit, best first, with the capacity left after each (§3.5). In the mockup both give the same 12 funded rows.

This is a heuristic, not an exhaustive search. The ratio order prefers trades that earn the most per copper tied up, and the swap pass catches the bigger trades that a run of small ones crowded out: with 1 g of capacity, A (60 s for 30 s adjusted), B (50 s for 20 s) and C (50 s for 19 s), the fill takes A alone (30 s), and the swap pass replaces it with B and C (39 s) (PF5). A higher return on a smaller trade is never preferred just for its ratio (PF11). Selection is deterministic and standard library only. It runs inside the full refresh's 120 s, and it is benchmarked against the incremental re-plan's 1 s (§3.1.2, §6 item 16).

The mockup's pool (PF6): capacity 6 g 49 s 40 c. The fill funds 12 rows, 5 for Today (51 s 60 c) and 7 for the week (5 g 73 s 40 c), 6 g 25 s in all, which leaves 24 s 40 c. Lesser Mana Potion, a Today craft that needs 45 s, is excluded "Exceeds risk tolerance (needs 45s, 24s 40c left)" even though Today's list has room: the week's rows used the shared capacity first (Mithril Spurs alone takes 3 g).

**D.5 Validation before publishing.** A failure here is a bug: the run is not published, the previous plan stays live, and Status shows an error (§5.2 rules 3 and 4).
- **Gold:** Σ gold needed of the funded rows ≤ available new investment, and each character's share ≤ its spendable gold.
- **Exposure:** committed capital + Σ gold needed ≤ the risk budget.
- **Materials:** each owned unit is claimed at most once, and every chain's steps are funded together.
- **Capabilities:** every funded row's character can do it.
- **Quantities:** every item and window stays within its liquidity cap (PL4).
- **Counts:** Today ≤ `today_limit` and the week ≤ `week_limit`.
- **Totals:** profit and gold totals sum funded rows only; an excluded row counts in no total.
- **Zero funded rows is a valid result** (D.7, PF14).
- **Built (M10)** as `selection.validate`, run by `allocation.portfolio_plan` before anything is staged; it raises `SelectionError`, the refresh or re-plan publishes nothing and the last plan stays live (§5.2). Chains are atomic by construction (a craft batch is the whole chain of one craft), so "every chain's steps are funded together" is checked as "batch n + 1 only with batch n".

**D.6 Exclusion reasons.** The Status cell says "Funded" (a badge) or why the row was left out, in the same words on the dashboard and in the addon. The checks run in this order, and the first that fails is shown (PF24, PF30):

| Status | When | Example (mockup) |
|---|---|---|
| Required profession unavailable: <profession> <skill> | no character has the profession, or none has the skill. The detail names the best one ("Amaya has Enchanting 142") or says "no character has Enchanting" | Grunt's Bracers of Defense: Enchanting 150 |
| Forecast too uncertain (<n> closes in 30 d, needs 24), or (<n> week(s) of data) | the batch sells at a window and its item has fewer than `MIN_HOURLY_CLOSES` (24) hourly closes in the last 30 days (checked first, PF30: a forecast from a handful of closes is noise; any strategy), or the trust gate fails (D.2). The detail says closes or weeks, whichever failed. A batch that sells now is never checked for closes: its price comes from the live book, capped at med7 on thin data (PP4) | Greater Magic Essence: "its Sunday peak has been seen once; a forecast needs 3 weeks" |
| Forecast too stale (prices <age> old) | the item's newest price is more than 24 h old | — |
| Sales volume unknown, or Sales volume unknown (<mat>) | the item's sales rate is unknown (`sold_per_day` None), so neither the chance to sell nor liquidity can be measured; any batch that sells, now or at a window (PF30). A Disenchant buy is checked on its **main mat** instead (the outcome with the largest expected value; its sell value is what the EV rests on), named in brackets: the green is never resold, so its own closes and rate don't count | a potion with 30 closes and no sales estimate |
| No listing left to price against | the batch sells now an item you don't already own (a flip's relist, a craft posted now) into a book with no other listing, and the item has fewer than 24 hourly closes in 30 days: its price would be the level alone, which nothing grounds (R28). A flip's later auctions simply end the flip there; the row is listed only when not even its first auction is grounded. Stock you own may still post now at the capped level | Fortified Spaulders: the only listing at 35 s, med7 150 g from 4 closes |
| Insufficient liquidity: <s>/day, <q> planned | the batch would take longer than the horizon to sell at the item's sales rate (`q ÷ s > horizon_days`), or its windows' liquidity caps have no room left | Elixir of Fortitude: 3/day, 40 planned, "the market sells about 3 a day; 40 would take about 13 days" |
| Missing materials: <n> <item> | a mat with no stock, no AH listing and no vendor | — |
| Resources already claimed: <item> (<funded row>) | an owned unit, or the capacity it needs, went to a better funded row | — |
| Insufficient gold (needs <x>, all characters hold <y>) | its gold needed is more than all your characters' spendable gold | — |
| Insufficient gold on <character> (needs <x>, has <y>) | its character's spendable gold left is too low, though the account's would cover it | — |
| Recommendation limit reached (20 of 20 today), or (… this week) | its list is full | — |
| Exceeds risk tolerance (needs <x>, <y> left) | its gold needed is more than the capacity left at its turn | Lesser Mana Potion: needs 45 s, 24 s 40 c left |

**Built (M10)**: the static checks (the first seven rows: profession, forecast too uncertain, forecast too stale, sales volume unknown, no listing left, liquidity, missing materials) are `selection.static_reason` from an `Eligibility` (and `profession_detail` for the detail line), the dynamic ones `selection.check`; both are ported (`Portfolio.StaticReason`, `Portfolio.Select`) with the same texts. Forecast too stale is only ever the status of Sell stock, Craft and Multistage rows: generation leaves out flips, buys for later and disenchants of a stale item (D.3, PF28). It reads the item's price age now (`recommend.portfolio.price_ages`: the age at the build plus the time since; a scan after the build is fresh). Liquidity reads the units the opportunity plans up to the batch and the item's sales rate (no check when it is unknown). The other detail lines are built with the Portfolio page (M13, `portfolio_view.reason_detail`): the liquidity one from the item's rate and the planned units ("the market sells about 3 a day; 40 would take about 13 days", else "its windows' liquidity caps have no room left"), the forecast one from the failed closes or the weeks the window was seen ("its Sunday peak has been seen once; a forecast needs 3 weeks"), the risk one as below, and a fixed line for each other reason (`portfolio_view.DETAILS`). An alternative keeps the `Eligibility` its static checks read (`allocation.Alternative.eligibility`) for these lines. The evidence checks read `Eligibility.window_closes` (the item's hourly closes in 30 days when the batch sells at a window, None when it sells now; an unknown count counts as none) and `Eligibility.volume_unknown` (None when the rate is known, "" for the item's own, a mat's name for a disenchant), set by `candidates.eligibility_of` from the batch's post option for every strategy, and for disenchants from `DeInfo.volume_unknown` (`recommend.portfolio.main_mat_unknown`). Thin data has posting now only (§3.3.4), so on thin data only a Buy for later, which exists for its window, carries the closes reason; it is an exclusion, not a generation filter, so the universe still shows it (PF30).

The pasted spec's "exceeds risk tolerance" and "affordable but blocked by risk" are this one status. Its detail line says which applies: better rows took the capacity ("its turn came after every funded row, so 24s 40c is also what stays unallocated"), or the batch alone is bigger than the whole remaining capacity ("it alone needs more than the whole remaining capacity (6g 49s 40c)").

**D.7 Empty states** (PF14, D77, UI19). When nothing new is funded, sections B and C are replaced by one block that gives the reason; positions (A) stay. The first matching reason wins:

1. **No gold in hand**: no character had gold at the last snapshot.
2. **No characters yet**: log in once with the addon so AHSync knows your gold and professions.
3. **Risk budget exhausted**: committed capital is at or over the limit. For example, after Amaya spends 6 g 70 s on new positions: "No new opportunities: risk budget exhausted (10g 67s 40c limit, 10g 88s committed)".
4. **Recommendation limit reached**: 20 of 20 funded in that list.
5. **Market data too old**: the newest prices are more than 24 h old; run AHSync.
6. **Nothing pays**: no opportunity nets above 0 after fees, deposits and the chance it sells.
7. **Every candidate excluded**: each one has a reason, and they are listed as alternatives.

A recommendation is never made up to fill the list. **Built (M10)** as `selection.empty_state` (the reason's text); the block that shows it is built with the Portfolio page (M13, `portfolio_view.empty_block`: the title and one line saying what it means, e.g. "New suggestions come back as positions sell and free capital").

**D.8 Profit % (return on cash)** (PF21). Profit % = expected net ÷ **cash**, where cash = the gold needed + the cost basis of bought stock the row uses. Found stock counts 0. It is a whole percent, rounded half up. A row whose cash is 0 (it uses only found stock) shows "—" and is left out of the % totals; its net still counts in the expected totals. The % is shown per row in the funding card and in both opportunity footers. It is never a ranking key (D.4).
- Copper Rod (post 5 rods bought at 10 s each, 1 s deposit): cash 51 s, net 63 s 6 c → 124%.
- Today's funded rows: 1 g 15 s 14 c ÷ 1 g 1 s 60 c (51 s 60 c gold needed + 50 s of bought rods) → 113%.
- The week's funded rows: (1 g 73 s 85 c − Heavy Grinding Stone's 1 s 34 c, which uses only found Heavy Stone) ÷ 5 g 73 s 40 c → 30%.
- **Built (M10)** as `portfolio.cash_of`, `portfolio.profit_pct` and `portfolio.pool_profit_pct`; shown on the Portfolio page since M13.
- **Positions** show "+X% on committed capital": Σ est. net of the positions that cost something and have one ÷ committed capital, whole percent half up. Found stock (Paid 0) commits nothing, so, as with a funded row whose cash is 0, it is left out of the %, and the footer names it: "% leaves out Wool Cloth (found stock only)"; "estimated profit on positions" still counts it, since it is what the positions are expected to make. (96 s 79 c − Wool's 4 s 72 c) ÷ 4 g 18 s = 92 s 7 c ÷ 4 g 18 s → +22%; Medium Leather's 40 s and Mageweave's 18 s of sold, uncollected units count in the base. In the addon a local position (A81) counts as costing something; found stock there is a row with cost 0 that isn't local.

**D.9 Positions** (PO1–PO13). A **position** is a durable record of gold you actually committed. It is not an action, so a re-plan never replaces it.

- **Opens** when a confirmed purchase or craft matches a funded opportunity (§3.4.1 matching). An unconfirmed attempt opens nothing.
- **Holds:** the character, the candidate id and strategy, the item, its **lots** (event id, units, unit cost, time), the units and cost left, the expected net at open and now, the original target window and the latest recommended one, its state and the proceeds realized so far.
- **Remaining cost** leaves at the position's average lot cost when a sold unit's payout is collected, or when a unit is vendored, disenchanted or used. A craft turns the mat lots of a Processing position into its product.
- **An extra purchase is a new decision.** Matched to a new funded opportunity, it opens a new position with its own lots; earlier positions are unchanged (PO6). A purchase that matches no funded row opens none and only feeds the average cost basis (§3.2 `cost_basis`), which stays the record for items that never matched a plan (PO12).
- **Closes** (Completed) when no units are left and every sale is paid out (PO11).
- **Found stock the plan holds for a later window** shows in section A as a Holding row with Paid 0 and cost basis left 0. It is not a durable position, since no gold was committed: the sell plan rebuilds it at each refresh (Wool Cloth in the mockup).

| State | When | Mockup |
|---|---|---|
| Acquiring | its buys are partly done and their window is still open | — |
| Processing | a craft or disenchant step is still to do on its units | Iron Ore: "smelt today · post Thu 11:00 for the 19:00 peak" |
| Holding | units held, waiting for their window | Copper Bar: "post Sat 11:00 for the 19:00 peak" |
| Ready to sell | now is inside its window, or selling now beats waiting: `E_now ≥ E_target ÷ (1 + premium)`, with the premium for the wait to its target window (D.2) and what you paid left out (sunk) | Elixir of Agility: "sell now: price spike +38%" |
| Listed | its units are on the AH | Mithril Coif: "undercut: relist at 4g 49s 99c" |
| Partially sold | some units sold, the rest held or listed | Mageweave Cloth: "collect mail: 15 sold, 25s 65c waiting" |
| Completed | nothing left and paid out (closed) | — |
| Needs reconciliation | the journal says more units than the newest snapshots show, and no event explains the gap | Medium Leather: "check bags and mail: 14 of 20 found" |

- **Adaptive exit.** Every refresh, and the addon live, re-evaluates each position's sell-now against hold with live prices (Ready to sell, above). A position past its window keeps being tracked, and its next action is re-planned.
- **Never infer a sale from missing inventory.** A Needs reconciliation position keeps its cost committed and its est. net unknown. It leaves the state when events explain the gap (auction mail, a payout, mail sent) or the units reappear. If nothing explains it 31 days later (auction mail is kept 30 days), the missing units close as an untracked loss at their cost, listed in History (PO10).
- **Mail tasks persist once executed.** A matched `mail_sent` stays the position's next action ("collect mail on Amaya") across re-plans until the recipient's mail or bags snapshot holds the items (the transit rule, §3.4B, T22). A suggested transfer that wasn't sent commits nothing (PO7).
- **History compares at position level** once positions exist (§3.4.1, PO9).

**Built (M14, 2026-10-19)**, pure in `engine/positions.py` (the facts) and `engine/position_views.py` (the plan and the market), kept by `recommend/positions_pass.py` over `state/positions.py`:
- **Opening and the position id.** The tracking pass (after matching and the cost basis) reads every confirmed purchase and craft matched to a buy or craft action that carries a funded candidate (`actions.cand_id`, written by `plan_rows` from `PortfolioPlan.action_cands`) and is no lot yet (`position_openings`). `positions.place` puts each, oldest first, into the open position of the same character, chain (`COALESCE(parent_id, action_id)`) and item (several purchases filling one buy), or a new one whose **id is the opening event's id**, the id the addon also knows (A81). A craft whose chain holds an open Processing position opens none: the craft converts it. Lots are upserted by (position, event) (PO8); a lot adds its share of the expected net at open: the event's copied net (T23), or, for a mat buy that carries none, the chain carrier's net × its units ÷ the matched action's. The target window is the chain's first post's window start. A craft lot's unit cost is its reagents' average cost basis at that moment per unit made.
- **The replay** (`positions.track`, every pass, from the stored positions' lots and every event since the oldest opened). Events of a character and item are split over that character's positions of that item oldest first (FIFO); units no position holds are your other stock. A post lists units; its sold mail moves them to sold-not-paid-out (their cost still committed); its payout realizes `money − deposit − units × average cost` (the payout's own deposit, else its post's share) and takes their cost out of committed capital (T17); an expired or cancelled mail brings them back, an expiry losing its deposit; an unlinked sold mail or payout sells from what is held; a disenchant or a craft that isn't the position's own conversion uses units (their cost leaves, realized falls by it); a craft whose reagents a Processing position holds turns it into the product (key, units = units made × used ÷ needed, at least 1; its cost carried, other Processing positions of the same craft merging their cost into it and closing); a sent mail moves units to the recipient, in transit until a mail or bags snapshot of hers is at or after the send (or she acts on them), the position then held by her (PO7). Cost left = `cost_total × units still yours ÷ units`, half up in integer arithmetic.
- **States** (the first that holds): Completed (nothing held, in transit, listed or unpaid; `closed_at` its last event); Needs reconciliation (below); Acquiring (its opening buy is partial and still open for matching, until 24 h after its slot, PO13); Processing (a craft or disenchant strategy whose units are not its product yet; a disenchant until its disenchant); Partially sold (some units sold); Listed (some units on the AH); Holding. Ready to sell replaces Holding when `position_views.ready_to_sell` holds: now is inside the plan's first selling window for the units, or `E_now ≥ E_target ÷ (1 + premium)` with E_now the market's posting-now value of the units held and E_target the plan's expected value at that window, the premium `timing.premium_micro` for the wait to its start, what was paid left out (PO5); a plan that only posts the units now is Ready to sell too.
- **Next action** (`position_views.evaluate`), the first that applies: "collect mail on Amaya" (in transit); "check bags and mail: 14 of 20 found"; "collect mail: 15 sold, 25s 65c waiting" (Σ price × units × 0.95); "buy 20 more by Tue 23:00" (Acquiring); "undercut: relist at 4g 49s 99c" for a listed position someone else lists at or below (one copper under the lowest other price, `relist_price`), else "listed · cheapest"; while Processing "<smelt | craft | disenchant> <today | Tue> · <the product's first post>" (smelt for a Mining recipe); "sell now: price spike +38%" (the posting-now price over its median, whole percent half up) or "sell now"; else the plan's first action for the units ("post Sat 11:00 for the 19:00 peak", "post now", "hold", "disenchant", "sell to a vendor"), with " · below what you paid" when the plan's expected value for them is under the cost left (PO3, T7). Est. net now is the plan's actions' net for the units (their share of the character's holding), else the expected net at open for the units still yours, unknown ("—") while it needs reconciliation. A chain's later steps (posts of units still to buy or make) are not the units' actions; a stored follow-up post is, once its buy or craft is done (§3.4.1).
- **Reconciliation** (PO10): after the replay, per character and item, when every snapshot of the holder (bags, bank, mail) is at or after the positions' last events and shows fewer units than they hold, the positions, oldest first, miss the difference: Needs reconciliation, cost committed, est. net unknown. An auction mail, a payout or a sent mail after the snapshot explains it (the snapshot is then older than an event), as does a newer snapshot with the units. A position whose last event is more than 31 days old closes its missing units as an untracked loss at their cost (realized falls by it; History notes "6 lost (untracked)"). Never a sale on missing inventory alone.
- **Found stock** (`position_views.found_rows`): a character's held item with no cost basis that the plan holds or posts at a window after now, and no position shows, is a Holding row `found:<charId>:<key>` with Paid and cost left 0, its est. net the plan's; never stored.
- **When.** The tracking pass replays and stores the facts after every SavedVariables read and hourly (`tracking_pass(db, now_ms)`). Every plan run (full refresh and re-plan) replays again without writing, takes committed capital from it, evaluates the market's positions against the run's actions and the (scan-repriced) intelligence for section A and `PortfolioPlan.lua`, and stores each position's evaluation. The hourly status pass re-evaluates from the stored actions (an action's expected value is its net plus its units' cost basis) and the published intelligence, no history read, and swaps the published snapshot's positions so the next plan file carries them (`positions_pass.reevaluate`).
- **Simplifications**, documented: events are split FIFO, not by lot; a partial mail of a position's units leaves it with the sender; a conversion's leftover mats are dropped from the position (their cost stays in the product's); a disenchant strategy's position closes at −cost, its History row "mats sold separately" (not tracked, as D68's disenchant).

**D.10 Export and the addon.** The desktop exports the candidate universe, the dependency graph and a portfolio snapshot (§3.5 formats, §5.0 contracts; built M11: each run stores its exported candidates and snapshot, `recommend.portfolio.portfolio_export`, and the export writes them with the graph of the characters' known recipes, `engine.graph.build`; the addon decodes them from M11 and uses them since M12, §4.0). The addon reselects the logged-in character's part live (§4.0). Desktop and addon share the money rules, risk semantics, profit and timing formulas, constraints, capability rules, candidate ids, quantities, tie-breaks and validation, so equivalent inputs give the same selection (PF15, PF27). Differences come only from different observed data.

#### 3.4E Gathering: what to farm

**Built: M16, the day-one page** (2026-10-21: `data/gathering_catalog.json`, `engine/gathering.py`, `engine/gathering_view.py`, `recommend/gathering_page.py`; E.1, E.2, E.6 and E.8, the rate priority of E.5 over whatever rates exist, GA1–GA12, D80, D81, D88). **Built: M17, loot capture and rates** (2026-10-22: the addon's `Capture/Gather.lua`, `engine/sessions.py`, `engine.gathering.observed_rate` and `observed_rates`, `state/gathering.py`, migration 23, the rate card's sessions and override, `POST /api/manual_rate`; E.3, E.4, E.5 and E.7's rate card, GA13–GA27, D82, D85, D87, A85–A91, SEC12, FM41, FM42). **Built: M18, the price history and the detail** (2026-10-22: `engine/gathering_history.py` for the historical columns and the detail's heatmap, reading line, windows and seasonality, `engine.gathering.observed_categories`, `unplaced_loot` and `unplaced_text`, `state.gathering.looted_sources`, the history fields of `recommend/gathering_page.price_facts`, `timing_edge_min_strength`; E.1's observed categories, E.7, E.8's per-build day-one rule, Status's unplaced loot and the Characters page's gathering professions; GA28–GA34, D83, D84, D86). Merged 2026-10-21 from the user's Gathering tab spec v1.0 and the decisions taken with the user while aligning the mockup, which override the pasted text where they differ; GA1–GA34, D80–D88, A85–A91, SEC12, FM41, FM42). A research page on the dashboard, not a funding decision: **Gathering** answers what to farm, whether your character can farm it, what it earns per hour at **your own** pace, and when to sell it. The page is desktop only (§3.5); the addon only records loot (E.3). The look is the mockup's Gathering page.

It reuses what is built:

| It needs | Already built as |
|---|---|
| the current AH unit price, its quantity and age | `current` and the intelligence's `cur` (§3.3, §3.3.6 `current_listed_supply`) |
| the AH cut and the deposit | `engine/fees` (§3.4 Fees) |
| a chance to sell with no sales data | the position-aware default (§3.3.2, PD1) |
| med30, selling windows, seasonality strength, the 168-slot `how` | §3.3.4, §3.3.6 (the intelligence; no history read) |
| a percent difference with its colour | `views.delta_percent` (the tooltip's delta rule, TT1–TT5) |
| the item detail's heatmap and windows | the Inventory item detail (§3.5, `item_detail`) |
| professions and skills per character | the professions capture (§2.2; professions follow the file, §2.2.1) |
| idempotent events and the pruning handshake | event ids and `ingestedUntil` (§2.2, §5.2) |
| a dashboard write | the Security rules (§3.5) |

The examples use the mockup's world: Monday 10:38; Brugan has Mining 68 and Skinning 40, Amaya Herbalism 45 and Fishing 20; AH prices from an AHF scan 26 h old (Sun 08:38); 7 confirmed farming sessions, 3 h 40 min observed.

**E.1 The catalog** (GA1, GA2, GA33; **built M16**: `engine.gathering.catalog`, `skipped_text`; the observed categories **built M18**: `engine.gathering.observed_categories` over `state.gathering.looted_sources`; the file is version 1, 150 items: 30 herbs, 18 ores and node stones, 23 leathers, hides and scales, 24 fish, 59 monster drops, Light Leather, Medium Leather, Ruined Leather Scraps and Wildvine counted under both of their categories; mining gems, grey vendor trash and raid-only materials are left out; a row's optional `note` marks an uncertain skill or a secondary source, and the file's `_comment` carries its version and the Forever caveat. The dashboard mockup's world has its own 16-item catalog, without Rough Stone, which the shipped file lists as Copper Vein loot at Mining 1). What is farmable comes from a **static data file**, `ahsync/data/gathering_catalog.json` (CLAUDE.md exempts `data/*` from mirroring), never from AH data: a price never makes an item farmable.
- One entry per item: `key` (the AHF item key, §2.1), `name`, `categories` (one or more of Fishing, Herbalism, Mining, Skinning, Monster), `sources` (each `{type, profession, skill}`: `node` for a Mining or Herbalism node, `skinning` for a skinnable corpse, `fishing` for fishing, `monster` for direct corpse loot, the last with no profession and no skill), and an optional external item URL. Direct monster loot and skinning loot are separate sources: Light Leather is `Skinning · Monster` (a Skinning 1 source and a monster source).
- **V1 scope**: stackable, directly farmable trade goods: ores, herbs, leathers, fish, cloth, elemental and cooking drops (the pasted spec's §4.1 examples). No equipment, recipes, patterns or other world drops; no drop rates, spawn maps or locations (§9).
- **Loaded at the edge**: `recommend/gathering_page.py` reads the file and `engine.gathering.catalog(rows)` (pure) validates it. A row with an unknown profession or category, a skill outside 1–300, or a category that none of its sources backs is skipped with a Status line ("gathering catalog: 1 row skipped (Mithril Ore: unknown profession 'Minning')"); the other rows still show (GA2). So is a row without a key, name or source, a profession that can't gather its source type ("profession 'Skinning' can't gather a node source"), or a key already listed ("duplicate key 2770"). A file that can't be read leaves the page empty with the Status line "gathering catalog not read (…): the Gathering page is empty"; the Status topic is `gathering_catalog` (kind `gathering_catalog`), cleared once every row is good.
- **Unknown sources are never farmable.** An item the catalog doesn't know is never a row, even if you looted it; its units still count toward a session (E.4), and Status lists them as gathered but not placed (**built M18**, GA34: `engine.gathering.unplaced_loot` and `unplaced_text`, set by `recommend/gathering_page` as the info line `gathering_unplaced:<market>`, cleared when nothing is left; units over the confirmed sessions and the open candidates, so a candidate dropped under the confirm units, which is not farming, is left out: the GA15 event's 2 Rough Stone are not in the 22). The page says so in a note under the table, always present.
- **Observed categories** (**built M18**, GA33: `engine.gathering.observed_categories`, the sources from `state.gathering.looted_sources` over every stored gather event, a dropped candidate's too): a gather event's source (E.3) may teach the catalog that a **known** item also comes from another category (`mining`, `herbalism`, `skinning`, `fishing`, `monster` → that category). The Category cell then adds it with "(observed)" ("Skinning · Monster (observed)"), and the category filter matches it (the row's `cats`). It never changes eligibility, the Skill column or the item's note: those stay the catalog's. An unknown item is never taught.
- **Forever caveat**: Forever customises items and professions, so the catalog may be incomplete or wrong for its content. It is a game-version-appropriate Classic-era list, corrected as Forever's differences are found (§6).

**E.2 Eligibility** (GA3–GA5; **built M16**: `engine.gathering.eligible`, `skill_source`). A character can gather an item when it knows one of the item's source professions with current skill ≥ that source's required skill (`engine.gathering.eligible`). On a Classic-era client progression collapses into the skill check. Gear and enchant bonuses are ignored in V1.
- **Monster drops are always eligible**, for every character, with or without professions.
- **Several sources**: eligible when any one is (Light Leather: Skinning 1, or monster loot).
- The **Skill** column shows the lowest required skill among the item's gathering sources, "—" for a monster-only item, amber when no character has it (Silver Ore: Mining 75, Brugan has 68).
- **A lost profession**: professions follow the file (§2.2.1), so a character who unlearns Mining stops being eligible at the next read. Her sessions and manual rates are kept (her history), but she is no longer a rate source under All characters nor a fallback for others (E.5). In her own view her rate still shows.

**E.3 Acquisition events** (A85–A91, GA13, FM41, FM42; **built M17**: `Capture/Gather.lua`, ingest in `state/importer.py` and `state/gathering.py`; the source rule, the windows a cast owns and the login flag are in §2.2.1 `gather`). The addon records **only what passes through the loot window**, tagged by what opened it (`Capture/Gather.lua`, the Disenchant pattern of A55):
- a `LOOT_OPENED` after the player's successful **Mining**, **Herb Gathering**, **Skinning** or **Fishing** cast is tagged `mining`, `herbalism`, `skinning` or `fishing`;
- a `LOOT_OPENED` on a creature's corpse with no such cast pending is tagged `monster`;
- every other loot window records nothing: a Disenchant (it is a `disenchant` event, A55), a chest or other object, an item opened from the bags, Pick Pocket, and any loot after another cast.
- **Excluded by construction**: AH purchases, mail, trades, the bank, crafts and vendor buys never pass through the loot window, so they can't become gather events, without any correlation with bag changes.
- **One event per loot window**: all its item slots, summed per item and ordered by key, coin left out. What the window shows is recorded, whether or not every slot is then taken (full bags): a documented simplification. A second `LOOT_OPENED` for the same window (some autoloot paths fire it twice) adds nothing.
- **Fields** (§2.2.1, event type `gather`): the usual `id` (`charId:timeMs:seq`, the deduplication key), `char` and `at`, plus `source`, `login` (the character's initial-login time, kept across `/reload`, E.4) and `loot` (`"key,qty;…"`). Ingest upserts on the id, so reading the same file twice adds nothing (GA13, FM41). Events stay in SavedVariables until AHSync has stored them (the A4 pruning handshake, which covers them like any event).
- A loot window the adapter can't read (no slots, or an error) records no event and raises no Lua error; the diagnostics trace it (FM42).
- The addon does no analytics on loot: no sessions, no rates (§4.1, §5.1).

**E.4 Sessions** (GA14–GA18, GA24; **built M17**: `engine.sessions.sessions`). AHSync groups a character's gather events into **farming sessions** (`engine/sessions.py`, pure; run by `state/gathering.py` after each SavedVariables read). No manual start or stop. Events are taken in time order, those of the same second in capture order (their id's seq), so equal timestamps stay one candidate (a gap of 0). A `/reload` keeps the login, so the candidate goes on; a gap of exactly `session_gap_minutes` ends it (GA15: the 15:14 event starts a new candidate), one minute less continues it. When the next event both comes after the gap and carries another login, the session "ended at logout".
- A **candidate** collects one character's events in time order, whatever their source: mining, skinning and corpses can share a session (Brugan's Sat 21:10–21:33: 8 Copper Ore, 7 Light Leather, 5 Ruined Leather Scraps).
- It **ends** when the next event comes `session_gap_minutes` (15) or more after the last one, or carries a different `login` (a logout ends it; so does a game restart), or, for the newest candidate, when now is 15 minutes past its last event. A session **never spans characters**: each character's events are grouped alone (GA18).
- It is **confirmed** when its units reach `session_confirm_units` (20), counting every unit looted in it, catalog or not (Amaya's Sat 17:00–17:30: 7 Linen Cloth + 7 Broken Fang + 6 Chipped Claw = 20). Confirmation is retroactive: the session starts at its first event. A candidate that ends under 20 units is dropped; its events stay stored.
- **Duration** = last event − first event. The 15 quiet minutes that ended it are not counted (Brugan's Sun 14:02–14:59 is 57 min).
- **No duration, no rate**: a confirmed session whose events share one time (a single loot of 20+ units) is stored but gives no rate (GA17).
- **The open candidate** (the newest, not ended) is shown only as a line on the rate card (E.7), never used for a rate: "open: Mon 10:21– · 9 Copper Ore so far · not yet confirmed (12 of the 20 units needed)" (9 Copper Ore + 3 Rough Stone). One that already holds 20 units or more reads "… so far · 25 units: a session once it ends": it is confirmed only when it ends, since its duration can still grow. Its hover: "still collecting: it ends 15 min after the last loot (10:30) or at logout; no rate uses it".
- Sessions are stored (`farming_sessions`, `farming_session_items`, §3.2) with their id = the first event's id, rebuilt from the stored events of each character whose events changed, in one transaction. Only ended sessions are stored; the open candidate, and one that ended since the last read because time passed, are derived at each page build from the events after the last stored session (§3.2). The same events always give the same sessions, so a re-import changes nothing (FM41).

**E.5 Rates** (GA19–GA23; **built M17**: `engine.gathering.observed_rate`, `observed_rates`, fed to `effective_rate` by `engine/gathering_view` at each page build). Per character and material (`engine.gathering.observed_rate`):
- **Observed rate** = units of that material ÷ the total duration of the confirmed sessions that contained it, × 60, rounded half up to **whole items per hour** (§3.3: `floor(x + 0.5)`; in integer form `floor((units × 7,200,000 + Σ ms) ÷ (2 × Σ ms))`). Every material of a session uses the whole session's duration, so a secondary drop doesn't double-count time. Copper Ore: (45 + 17 + 8) units ÷ (30 + 57 + 23) min = 38.18 → **38/hr**.
- Each session's own items per hour, for the rate card, is its units × 60 ÷ its minutes, half up (Mon 08:05–08:35: 45 in 30 min → 90/hr).
- **Priority** (`engine.gathering.effective_rate`, with `own_rate` and `best_rate`; **built M16**, fed with the observed rates of the stored sessions since M17): the character's **manual override** → her **own observed** rate → a **fallback**: the highest effective rate (manual or observed) among the *other* characters who are eligible now, ties by character id → **blank** ("—"). Nothing is ever inferred from prices or drop rates.
- **Under All characters** a row shows the highest effective rate among the eligible characters, and that character (Copper Ore: 38, Brugan).
- **The fallback is display only**: muted grey, tagged "Fallback", the source character in the Character column, its tooltip ("Amaya's observed rate; Brugan has none yet. Replaced by Brugan's own rate once Brugan has one, even if lower."). It is never written to the character's data, and her own rate supersedes it once it exists, even if lower (Linen Cloth for Brugan: 14/hr from Amaya until his own 8/hr, GA22). It still sorts like any rate.
- **Manual overrides** are per character and material, a whole number 1–10,000 items per hour, stored in the database (`manual_rates`, §3.2), never in config.toml, set and cleared from the rate card (`POST /api/manual_rate`, §3.5, SEC12). An override replaces the observed rate in the table for that character until cleared; the cell is marked "manual". Amaya's Earthroot: 12/hr manual over an observed 10/hr (8 Earthroot over 2 sessions, 48 min).
- **Rates are computed, not stored.** Each page build derives them from the stored sessions and overrides: a few hundred sessions are cheap, and eligibility (which picks fallbacks) changes with every professions read, so a stored rate would be a cache to invalidate.

**E.6 Gold per hour** (GA6–GA8; **built M16**: `engine.gathering.deposit_per_unit` = `fees.deposit_copper(vendor, 20, 24) ÷ 20`, `chance_to_sell` (the intelligence's posting-now chance when `S` is known, else `sellprob.default_chance` at the AH price against `sellprob.cheapest_other`), `net_per_unit` = `fees.sale_net(price, 1) − (1 − chance) × deposit per unit`, `net_per_hour`, `gross_per_hour`; an unknown vendor price counts as no deposit). With an effective rate and a current AH price:
- **Gross gold/hr** = items/hr × the current AH unit price (integer copper). It is shown only in the detail (Copper Ore: 38 × 2s 20c = 83s 60c).
- **Net per unit** = post price − 5% AH cut − (1 − chance) × deposit (float copper, an expected value, §3.3). The **post price** is the current AH unit price (a listed price, not a sale). The **deposit** is the 24 h deposit of one 20-unit post ÷ 20 (`engine/fees`; 15% of the vendor price per unit, exact for 20 units), refunded on a sale, so only the chance it expires costs it. The **chance** is the item's posting-now chance (`P'`, §3.3.2), the Market activity Chance.
- **Net gold/hr** = items/hr (the whole number) × net per unit, shown rounded half up. Copper Ore: 38 × (220 − 11 − 0.15 × 0.75) = 38 × 208.8875 = 7,937.7 → **79s 38c**.
- **Day-one chance**: with no sales data (`S` unknown) the chance is §3.3.2's default: `default_sell_prob_cheapest` (50%) when you'd be the cheapest seller, else `default_sell_prob` (25%). Posting at the current AH price ties the cheapest, so it is 50%. The cell carries the low-confidence dot, and its tooltip says "chance is the default: no sales data yet". Tin Ore: 4/hr × (275 − 13.75 − 0.5 × 3.75) = 4 × 259.375 = **10s 38c** (GA7). A mat's deposit is pennies, so the default barely moves the figure.
- No rate, or no listing, leaves Items/hr, Character and both gold/hr "—" (GA8).
- **Stale prices** (over 24 h) are used as they are, with their age amber: this page is research, not funding.

**E.7 Historical columns and the detail** (GA28–GA32; **built M18**: `engine/gathering_history.py`, `columns` for the cells and their sort keys, `detail` for the heatmap, reading line, windows and seasonality, `median_lines` for the price card, called by `engine/gathering_view._row` and `_detail`). Each needs price history; on day one they are "—" (E.8). Everything comes from the published intelligence (`recommend/gathering_page.price_facts`: `med30`, `closes_30d`, `seasonality`, the packed `how` with its weeks and the `sell_windows`, each with its `how_avg` and `weeks`), never from a history read (§3.1.2).
- **Median 30d** = `med30` (§3.3.6). It is shown once the item has at least `MIN_HOURLY_CLOSES` (24) hourly closes in the last 30 days, the thin-data threshold of §3.3.4: a median of one scan is that scan (`gathering_history.has_median`; an unknown count, from a document before schema 8, keeps it, as it keeps the windows). Else "—", its hover "thin data: 23 hourly closes in 30 days; the median needs 24" (or "no price history yet" without a med30). **Deviation** = `views.delta_percent(cur, med30)`, with its colours (Copper Ore: 2s 20c against 2s → +10%); "—" without a listing.
- **Sell window** = the start (day and hour, local time) of the item's top selling window (§3.3.4): Copper Ore's Sat 17:00–21:00 reads "Sat 17:00", its tooltip naming the window and its best hour ("Start of the top selling window Sat 17:00–21:00 (best hour Sat 19:00) · seasonality strength 61"). Under `timing_edge_min_strength` (40, inclusive) of seasonality strength (§3.3.6) it reads "no timing edge" (muted; Peacebloom, 22); with thin data (no median shown), insufficient evidence or no window, "—" with "too little data" (Silver Ore), its hover saying which. It sorts by the wait from now to the window's start (`gathering_history.wait_minutes`; 0 while now is inside it).
- **Sell window price** = med30 × the window's average price ratio (the intelligence window's `how_avg`), `floor(x + 0.5)`, with `views.delta_percent` against the AH price now under it: Copper Ore 2s × 1.12 = **2s 24c**, "+2% vs now"; Tin Ore 3s × 1.05 = 3s 15c, "+15% vs now"; its hover "2s median × 1.12 (Sat 17:00–21:00)". "—" wherever the Sell window is not a window.
- **The detail** (a row click; `engine/gathering_view._detail` with `engine/gathering_history.detail`), in this order:
  1. **Price card**: current AH price with its age (amber over 24 h), 30-day median, deviation (coloured), listed quantity (D83: "2s 20c · 26 h old", "2s", "+10%", "64 units").
  2. **The rate card** ("Your farming rate"; **built M17**: `engine/gathering_view._card_block` and `_session_lines`, each block `{"char", "kind", "head", "sub", "foot", "lines", "clear", "override"}`, lines `{"text", "title", "open"}`, `override` `{"placeholder", "note", "clear"}` or null; an inactivity hover adds "; those 15 min are not counted"; a character who lost the profession keeps her rate and lines but gets no input), first after the price card, one block per character, those with a rate first (highest first), then by name. With a rate: "Brugan · 38/hr observed · 70 Copper Ore over 3 sessions, 1 h 50 min"; the open candidate's line when it is hers and contains the material; the **last 3 confirmed sessions that contained the material**, newest first, each "Mon 08:05–08:35 · 30 min · 45 Copper Ore · 90/hr" (the material named on the first only; hover: how it ended, "ended at logout" or "ended by inactivity: no loot 14:59–15:14"); then "Manual override: [38] items/hr [Save] [Clear]". An active override reads "Amaya · 12/hr manual (observed 10/hr from 8 Earthroot over 2 sessions, 48 min) [Clear]". An eligible character without a rate: "Amaya — no sessions with Light Leather yet" and an empty override input; not eligible: "Brugan — no sessions with Earthroot yet · not eligible (no Herbalism)", no input. With a character picked, only hers. **There is no session list anywhere else on the page.**
  3. **Market activity**: Chance · Est. Sales/Day · Current Supply · Supply/Volume (§3.5's columns, in that order).
  4. **Gross gold/hr, net per unit and net gold/hr**, each as its formula with the row's numbers ("38 × 208.89c = 79s 38c"), and the deposit line.
  5. **The 7 × 24 price heatmap** (`how`, local time, the item detail's cells, `item_detail.heatmap`) marking **now** (thick accent outline: Mon 10:00), the **top window** (dashed: Sat 17:00–21:00) and **windows 2–3** (dotted), with a legend and the **reading line**: "now 2s 20c (Mon 10:00 slot ratio 0.97) · best Sat 17:00–21:00 at 2s 24c · +2% for a 5 d 6 h 22 min wait" (the wait runs from now to the window's start; "in the window now" inside it; the now price is the current AH price, the slot ratio `how` at now's slot, or "(Wed 12:00 slot: no data)" when that slot has no week of data, as its cell says). The windows are marked only where the Sell window column shows one; without a timing edge the reading line ends "· no timing edge: seasonality strength 22, under 40" or "· too little data for a selling window", and without a published profile there is no heatmap. The cells are sent compactly (`gathering_history.heat`: per cell its level, an index into the item's distinct hover texts and its mark; the page writes the hover as the day, the payload's `heat_hours` label and that text), about 2 KB per item.
  6. **Top 3 selling windows**: Rank · Window · Median price (med30 × the window's ratio) · Weeks of data (the window's thinnest slot, the intelligence window's `weeks`): 1 Sat 17:00–21:00 2s 24c 7; 2 Sun 19:00–22:00 2s 18c 7; 3 Wed 20:00–22:00 2s 16c 4. Favourable listing windows, not guaranteed sale times. Shown only with a timing edge.
  7. **Seasonality strength** with its sentence: "61 · the weekly profile beat a flat price on the held-out week", or under 40 "weak pattern: no strong timing advantage", or "—" with "insufficient evidence"; then "Sell window in the table" with what the column says ("Sat 17:00 · start of Sat 17:00–21:00 (best hour Sat 19:00)").

**E.8 The day-one rule** (the user's requirement; GA7, D84; **built M16**; **built M18**: decided at each page build, day one while no catalog item has a med30 that `gathering_history.has_median` shows; only then are the historical columns "—" with their "needs price history" header notes and the detail the compact form, its header "Copper Ore: day one · selected row · compact"; else "Copper Ore: price, timing and your rate · selected row · Mining 1", the header notes without "needs price history", and the full detail for every item, "—" where an item has no history). The page is useful right after a first in-game AH scan. Only the historical columns may be blank without price history:

| Shows | Needs |
|---|---|
| Item, Category, Skill, eligibility | the catalog and the professions capture; no AH data |
| Items/hr, Character, the rate card | the addon's loot events and the manual overrides; no AH data |
| AH Price, AH Qty, Net gold/hr | the newest scan or AHF snapshot: one scan is enough, its age shows; the default chance (E.6) until sales data exist |
| Median 30d, Deviation, Sell window, Sell window price, the heatmap, the top windows, seasonality strength | price history (weeks of data); "—" until then, their header tooltips adding "needs price history" |

AHF being offline changes nothing except that the price age is your own scan's. The day-one detail is compact: the price and rate lines, "Chance to sell 50% · default: no sales data yet", and one line in place of the history ("no price history yet: the heatmap, the top selling windows and seasonality strength appear as weeks of data arrive"). Day one, Copper Ore nets 38 × (209 − 0.5 × 0.75) = **79s 28c**.

**E.9 What Gathering does not do.**
- **No Portfolio interaction in V1.** Farmed items reach your bags as found stock (cost 0, §3.2 `cost_basis`), and the optimizer sells them like any stock (§3.4D.1 found items). Gathering is time, not gold: no exposure, no funding, no candidates, no actions, nothing in `PortfolioPlan.lua`.
- **No in-game Gathering tab.** The addon records loot only; heatmaps are dashboard-only anyway (§3.5).
- No multi-material session gold/hr (the pasted spec's §9.1) and no separate session history: sessions show only on the rate card.
- The rest of the pasted spec's §13 is out of scope (§9).

#### 3.4.0 Missed peaks: move, or hold for next week

An item usually has several peaks a week, for example raid nights on Tue, Wed and Thu. These are its best selling windows (§3.3.4). A planned post targets one of them, its **original peak**. Its expected net there, `E_orig`, is the **anchor**.

- **Late but not missed.** The post time (8 h before) has passed, but the window hasn't ended. The plan says "post now (late)", and the chance is computed over the rest of the window.
- **Missed.** The window ended with no matching post. The action becomes `missed` and is labelled with what was lost, e.g. "Missed Tue 20:00–23:00 (+12%)". Then the plan looks at the item's next windows **in the same plan week**, in time order, and takes the first one that passes all three checks:
  1. **Close to the original:** `E_candidate ≥ (1 − peak_tolerance) × E_orig` (`peak_tolerance` = 10%). It is always compared with the **original** anchor, never with an earlier replacement, so it can't slide down one window at a time.
  2. **Likely to sell:** `P'_candidate ≥ min_sell_prob`.
  3. **Still worth it:** `E_candidate` is above the item's best alternative (vendor, disenchant, or holding).
- **The result is shown either way:**
  - If a window passes, the post moves there and is labelled "Missed Tue peak → Wed 20:00–23:00 instead (−5% vs original, 88% chance)".
  - If none passes, the action becomes **hold**: "Missed Tue and Wed; Thu is 18% below → hold until next week's Tue 20:00 peak". No post is planned for the rest of this week.
- **A new week is a new slate.** The plan week runs from `week_start` (Mon 00:00 local). When a new week starts, held and missed items are planned from scratch: the anchor becomes that week's best window and earlier misses are forgotten. History keeps them.
- **Mechanics** (`engine/missed.py`, `recommend/status_pass.py`): the hourly status pass reads only stored actions, the published intelligence and character state, never history (IU10). A missed post gets one replacement action, a pending post or a hold, with `replaces_id` set to it and the same `anchor_net`, so a later miss is still compared with the first peak (M3). A post planned for "now" has no stored window: it is missed 24 h after its post time and not re-planned; the next full refresh plans its stock again.

#### 3.4.1 Tracking what you did

Captured events (§2.2) are matched to plan actions automatically, by the tracking pass (`engine/tracking`, `recommend/tracking_pass`), which runs after every SavedVariables read and before the hourly status pass. Only confirmed purchases and posts match; an attempt waits for its confirmation. A craft event matches by its recipe's product, and each auction mail resolves at most one post (`auction_mail.post_id`):

- **Matching.** An event matches the earliest `pending`, `late` or `partial` action with the same character, kind and item that is still open for matching:
  - a buy, craft or mail: its slot is within 24 h of the event;
  - a post: the event falls between 24 h before its post time and the end of its selling window.

  Further rules per kind:
  - A buy matches only at or below the planned price.
  - A post matches at any price, and records the price you used.
  - A craft matches by recipe.
  - A mail matches only a confirmed mail_sent event (A75) to its planned recipient (`to_char`), from the same sender, of the same item (T21); 6 of 6 is `done`, 4 of 6 `partial`.
- **Status.** Matching the full quantity sets `done`, and part of it sets `partial`. You can also mark an action `skipped`. Events that match nothing are still recorded, and they still update inventory and cost. **What "late" and "missed" mean depends on the kind**, and the hourly status pass (§3.1.1) applies them:

  | Kind | `late` | `missed` |
  |---|---|---|
  | buy, craft, mail, DE, vendor | — | still unmatched 24 h after its slot |
  | post | its post time has passed but its selling window hasn't ended ("post now (late)", §3.4.0) | its selling window ended with no matching post; the missed-peak re-plan runs (§3.4.0) |
- **Follow-up.** A done buy or craft for a flip or craft-to-sell keeps its sell action (via `parent_id`). Each engine run re-plans that sell action from fresh prices, and it appears in Today and Week as a reminder ("You bought 20 Linen Cloth on Tue for 40 c each; sell Sat 18:00"). The sell plan shows "below what you paid" when the best option no longer covers the cost. It still recommends that option, because what you paid is already spent.
- **Post outcomes.**
  - A sold invoice resolves the oldest pending post with the same character, item, stack and price.
  - An expired or cancelled mail has no price, so it resolves the oldest pending post with the same character, item and stack.
  - Each outcome updates your sell record (§3.3.3).
- **Expected and realized** (T23, D68–D70). When an event matches an action, the tracking pass copies the action's `expected_net` into the event (`posts`, `purchases`, `crafts`, `disenchants`.`expected_net`, float copper), the same way a post keeps its `p_at_post` (K5): its share, `expected_net × matched units ÷ the action's units`, so five purchases filling one buy never count it five times. It is copied once, at match time; a later re-plan doesn't change it. An event that matched no action stores NULL. Which net an action carries follows §3.4 "Expected net": a chain's carrier (a flip's buy, a craft chain's craft) carries the chain's profit, and a chain's follow-up post carries its own net against its cost, so that is what its post event copies. What the event **realized** (`engine/tracking.judge`, pure):
  - **A post**: its payout's `realized` (T17) once paid; "not sold yet" while it is up and "sold · not paid out yet" before the gold is taken (both open); `−deposit` when it expired ("expired"; 0 when the deposit wasn't captured); 0 when cancelled ("cancelled").
  - **A chain carrier** (a buy or craft whose action is in a chain and carries a net; mat buys and intermediate crafts carry none): the sum of what the chain's posts realized so far, as above, times the event's share; "N of M posts paid"; "nothing sold yet" while no post has resolved. The chain's posts are the confirmed posts matched to post actions with the carrier's chain id (`COALESCE(parent_id, action_id)`). It is open until every one of those posts has resolved and none of the chain's post actions is still pending or late. A chain that plans a disenchant and no post shows "mats sold separately".
  - **A disenchant**: not tracked, "mats sold separately": its mats are sold later as posts of their own.
  - Other rows (sold, expired and cancelled mail, payouts, vendor buys, mat buys, mail, missed and skipped actions, untracked changes) are not judged. A craft that carries no chain shows "—" for both.
- **Positions** (built, M14, §3.4D.9). A confirmed purchase or craft that matches a funded opportunity also opens a position, which outlives every re-plan. History compares expected and realized **per position**: one row per position with its expected net at open (Expected) and its proceeds less its cost and lost deposits (Realized), open until it is Completed. Its buy, craft and post events then count on the position, never again on their own, so a profit is still counted once (PO9). Events of items that never matched a plan keep the event-level rows above, and their cost stays the average cost basis. **Built** as `position_views.history_judged` and `history_view.judged_rows` (the event rows less those whose event id a position counted, `positions.rolled_events`, then the positions open now or closed in the window); the journal carries each row's event id since M14.
- **Stale state.** Every recommendation records the age of the character snapshot it used (§2.2.1 per-location times). One built on a snapshot older than 24 h says so, e.g. "based on Amaya's bags from 3 d ago" (D24): on the Craft page, for every place (bags, bank, mail) of a character that holds an owned mat the row uses. The addon uses its live state instead.
- **Re-planning.** Each engine run replaces `pending` and `late` actions. `done`, `partial`, `missed` and `skipped` actions are kept as history.

**Vendor-sold items** (R22, A33). An item a merchant has been seen selling in unlimited supply (`vendor_reagents`, §3.2)
is bought from the vendor only: the AH is not a source for it, even when a listing is cheaper (`craft.vendor_first`). An
owned unit of it is worth no more than buying another from the vendor, so its hold value is capped at the vendor price,
and it is never planned as a flip. **It is never posted either** (R23, A35): anyone can buy it from the vendor, so the sell plan only sells it to a merchant (when one pays) and plans no hold for it, and a recipe whose product a vendor sells is not crafted. The addon builds its craft market and its own sell rows the same way.

#### 3.4.2 Item rules (blacklist)

- `no_buy`: never recommend buying it (flip, disenchant buys) or crafting it. Stock you already own still gets a sell plan.
- `ignore`: leave it out of every recommendation, including the sell plan. Tooltip prices are still shown.
- `keep` (the **Keep** button on a "not selling" prompt): clears the flag and starts the item's sell record fresh from that moment, so `P'`, the quantity cut and the price cut all reset (§3.3.3). If it then expires again while cheapest (`keep_retry_streak`, default 1), the prompt comes back. Choosing Keep again resets it again.

You set or clear a rule from any dashboard row, from the **Rules** page, or by right-clicking a row in the addon. Rules set in the addon reach AHSync through SavedVariables, and rules set on the dashboard reach the addon through `PortfolioPlan.lua`. When both sides changed the same item, the newer `set_at` wins. **Clearing is a change too:** it is recorded as rule `none` with its own `set_at` (§3.2), so a clear on one side beats an older rule on the other instead of being undone by it.

#### 3.4.3 Reserves (keep for yourself)

Some items you hold are for your own use: food, potions, mats for your own gear. A **reserve** keeps them out of every suggestion without hiding the item. It is set per character, because what you keep belongs to the character that uses it, by right-clicking a row in the addon (window or AH panel): **Keep all**, **Keep at least…** (a number) or **Sell normally** (clears it).

- Kept units are never sold, disenchanted, vendored or used as craft mats, in the addon (A25) or the plan (R20). Only what the character holds above the reserve is planned; "keep all" plans none of it.
- The addon applies a reserve at once, trimming any AHSync action for the item to the free units and scaling its net. AHSync reads `AHProfitDB.reserves` and **follows the file** like professions: when the character's `updated` is at least the stored one, its reserves are replaced with the file's (S14). The next refresh plans with them.
- Inventory still lists kept items and counts their worth at hold value; the plan cell says "Kept for yourself" or "Keeping 10 for yourself · …" (D40).
- The addon's **Reservations** tab lists the character's reserves, held or not: item, how many it keeps ("All" or a number), how many it holds and how many are free to sell. Clicking a row (either button) opens the same menu, to change the number or pick Sell normally (A26).
- **Both sides edit reserves; the newest change wins** (S15, EX12, SEC9, D52, A60). The addon records when it set or cleared each reserve in `AHProfitDB.reserveTimes[charId] = "key,ms;…"`. AHSync stores every reserve with `set_at` and `source`, a clear as quantity 0, and takes a file's reserve only when its time is newer than the stored one; a file without times (an older addon) replaces the reserves that have no time, never a dashboard one. The dashboard's Inventory sets them per character and item through `POST /api/reserve {char, key, qty}` (qty −1 keeps all, 0 sells normally), re-plans and writes `PortfolioPlan.lua`, which carries them as `reserves = { [charId] = "key,qty,ms;…" }` outside the checksum; at login the addon applies each one newer than its own time for that item.

### 3.5 Outputs

1. **Dashboard** (out of game), at `http://localhost:8765`, served by Python's standard library and bound to `127.0.0.1` only, and exclusively: on Windows `SO_EXCLUSIVEADDRUSE` in place of `SO_REUSEADDR`, which would let a second server share the port and take its requests (FM57; M24). The packaged app shows it in its own window (§3.7.1); from source, any browser. The look of every page is defined by the mockup [docs/mockups/dashboard.html](mockups/dashboard.html). It uses an always-dark WoW theme: stone panels, gold trim and serif gold headings, Blizzard-style red buttons and item-quality colours. Data tables stay in a plain sans-serif font for readability. Open it in a browser and click through the pages, or use a direct link such as `dashboard.html#Week`.
   - **Portfolio** (**built**, M13's dashboard half, 2026-10-19: `engine/portfolio_view`, `recommend/portfolio_page`; §3.4D; the first page in the bar and the page it opens on; D72, D73, D77–D79). One plan for all your gold, laid out as in the mockup:
     - **Summary tiles**: Gold in hand (31g 40s, "Amaya 21g · Brugan 10g 40s"); Committed capital (4g 18s, "12% of capital base"); Capital base (35g 58s, "gold in hand + committed"); Risk tolerance, a dropdown of 10, 20, 30, 40 and 50% ("exposure limit 10g 67s 40c · changing it re-runs the allocation"); Remaining capacity (6g 49s 40c, "10g 67s 40c limit − 4g 18s committed"); Allocated to new opportunities ("6g 25s of 6g 49s 40c", "Today 51s 60c · next 7 days 5g 73s 40c · 24s 40c left"). Under them, one muted line: "portfolio snapshot Mon 10:38 · tiles show the values at 30% · gold as each character last logged in · positions from the journal".
     - **A. Active positions** (§3.4D.9): Item (with a note line) · Qty · Character · Status (the stage badge) · Paid · Cost basis left · Est. net · Sell window · Next action. The footer says "Capital committed 4g 18s · 12% of capital base (4g 18s ÷ 35g 58s) · estimated profit on positions +96s 79c · +22% on committed capital · % leaves out Wool Cloth (found stock only)" (D.8: found stock is left out of the %), and a muted line lists the stages. Positions take no recommendation slots. **Built (M14)**: the rows are the market's open positions, oldest first, then its found stock (`recommend.positions_pass.views`, §3.4D.9): Paid what the position cost, Cost basis left what it still commits, Est. net the evaluation's, Sell window the latest recommended window's start, Next action the evaluation's text ("post Sat 11:00 for the 19:00 peak", "sell now: price spike +38%", "collect mail: 15 sold, 25s 65c waiting").
     - **B. Top opportunities today** (realized within the immediate window, no premium): Strategy · Action · Item (its chain lines under it) · Qty · Each · Total · Expected net · Chance · Est. Sales/Day · Current Supply · Supply/Volume · Risk · Character · Status · Skip. Funded rows come first, best expected net first; then one red line, "capacity reached · 24s 40c left" ("funded rows end here · … left" when no row was left out for the risk tolerance); then every excluded row, greyed, with its reason in Status (§3.4D.6). A row is an opportunity: its funded batches together, or, when none is funded, its first batch with that batch's reason; the excluded rows come in two groups, those left out for gold, capacity or a list limit first, then the rest, each in §3.4D.6's order and best adjusted profit first. Each is the buy price, a craft's mat gold per craft or a sell-stock post's price; Item's lines are a craft's mats with their sources. A Min chance dropdown (default 0%) hides rows from view only: a hidden funded row stays funded and is counted in the footer, so no gold goes to a row you can't see. Footer: "5 of 20 funded · expected +1g 15s 14c · gold needed 51s 60c · profit 113% on cash · 3 alternatives excluded (in no total)".
     - **C. Top opportunities, next 7 days**: Strategy · Item · Qty · Buy when · Process · Sell when (with "expected sale 19:00") · Gold needed · Materials · Expected net · Adjusted (with "Wait 3.35 d · premium 31.75%") · the four market columns · Character · Status · Skip. Funded rows by adjusted profit, the red line, then the excluded ones. Footer: "7 of 20 funded · expected +1g 73s 85c · adjusted +1g 26s 24c · gold needed 5g 73s 40c · profit 30% on cash · 1 alternative excluded (in no total)", and a note with the wait and premium of every sale hour the plan uses.
     - **How the pool was funded**: # · Item · Sells · Adjusted · Expected net · Gold needed · Cash · Profit % · Left. The funded rows of both lists, shown best adjusted profit first (they were selected by adjusted profit per copper of exposure, then the swap pass, §3.4D.4), with the capacity left after each; a Total funded row (12 rows, adjusted 2g 41s 38c, expected +2g 88s 99c, gold 6g 25s, cash 6g 75s, 43%, 24s 40c left, noting which rows the % leaves out); the red line; then the best row the risk tolerance left out ("needs 45s: not funded"), if any. Totals of expected and adjusted profit are the sums of the rows' shown (half-up) values, so they always reconcile with the rows.
     - **Empty state** (§3.4D.7): sections B and C give way to one alert with the first matching reason; A stays.
     - Every number is built by `engine/portfolio_view` (§8.5); the page computes nothing. The dropdown posts the payload's own option value (`risk.values`).
   - **Today**: summary tiles (expected today and this week, budget left, inventory worth), alerts (low coverage, stale data), the actions for right now sorted by expected net with the minimum-chance filter, and a Needs a decision section. Each row has a Skip button. One row per chain with its steps as grey lines under the item (§3.4B Today list), mail as the sender's row, no holds or vendor sales; the footer counts its rows
   - **Week**: the rolling calendar from §3.4C (today first), one column per day with a day total, plus totals per category and per character. Each card names its character, right of the action badge (D65), and a chain's card lists its lines. Posts show at their post time (8 h before the peak) with "for the HH:00 peak"
   - **Today and Week stay during the rollout** (built, M15, D76). They show the funded plan's actions (§3.4D): the same items as the Portfolio page's funded rows, one row per action or chain. Removing them from the dashboard is decided only after the Portfolio page has been validated in real use (M15). The addon replaces its Today and Week tabs with a Portfolio tab (§4.3).
   - **On the AH**: every auction you have up right now, on all characters. Each row shows:
     - quantity, listed price each and total;
     - **what it cost you** each and total (what you paid, or what the mats cost you for a craft; 0 for items you found);
     - deposit, profit if it sells (total × (1 − cut) − cost);
     - time left, your position (cheapest, or how many units are listed below you and at what prices), and the chance it sells.
     
     Tiles at the top total the listed value, the cost, the expected profit and the deposits at stake.

     **Built** (`engine/on_ah_view`, `recommend/on_ah_page`, D18) from the own-auctions snapshots the addon captures: one row per group of identical auctions; the deposit comes from the post event that made it ("—" when that post wasn't captured); position compares with the item's current cheapest listing; the chance is the item's posting-now chance. Each group also carries its soonest end, from the snapshot's `ends` (S16): the row shows the time left ("ends in 5 h"), and its chance is the queue model's for the units still up, at their price, over the time actually left (the posting-now window cut to it). An auction the addon saved without an end (its `timeLeftSeconds` was not a number, A70) shows its band's bounds ("2 – 8 h"), as for a snapshot without `ends`, and its chance stays the posting-now chance; before A70 such an auction was written as ending now and showed "ending" with 0%. Position says how many units are listed below you now ("12 units listed below you at 38c–40c", "1 unit …" for one, D61), from the item's newest book; who listed them is not in the scan data. (D53)
   - **Disenchant** / **Craft** / **Flip** tables, sortable and filterable, with each, total and risk %.
