  5. **Install updates automatically**, a checkbox (`auto_update`);
  6. **Get beta updates**, a checkbox (`update_channel`);
  7. a separator, then **Quit AHSync**.
- **Hover text** (Windows; macOS shows the icon only): "AHSync 0.3.1 · synced 14:06"; a dev build reads "AHSync dev build".
- **Launch modes.** A manual launch shows the window on the dashboard (or the setup page); a launch with `--autostart` (the login entry's argument, §3.7.5) leaves it hidden.
- **From source**, `python -m ahsync_app` runs the shell against the per-user folders (§3.7.3), window and tray included, so it can be tried without a build (the app's packages from `requirements-app.txt` installed). Its login entry starts `pythonw.exe` beside the running Python (no console window) with the path of `ahsync_app/__main__.py`, which puts the repository on `sys.path` when run as a script, then `--autostart`. `python -m ahsync` is unchanged: `./data`, `./config.toml`, no tray, "AHSync is already running" on a second start (FM15).

#### 3.7.2 The platform layer (`ahsync_app/platform/`)

**Built (M22)**: `base.py`, `windows.py`, `macos.py` and `__init__.py` with `app_dirs`, `wow_candidates`, `restrict_file`, `run_location_problem` and `current()`; the update members raise `NotImplementedError` naming M27. **Built (M24)**: the start-at-login members, `autostart_enable(exe, args=())`, `autostart_disable()` and `autostart_enabled()`: Windows over an injected `RunKey` (`WinRegRunKey` by default), macOS over the home folder (DR9, DR10). The guardrail is built (`tests/test_conventions.py`, DR16); `tools/build.py` joins its allowlist in M26, when the file exists (an entry for a file without a token is stale).

Everything that decides what AHSync does is written and tested once. An OS answers only a few narrow questions, as data or as a thin writer:

- `base.py` defines the `Platform` protocol, **ten members**: `app_dirs()` (config and data folders, §3.7.3), `wow_candidates()` (likely WoW folders, as a list), `autostart_enable(exe)`, `autostart_disable()`, `autostart_enabled()` (§3.7.5), `restrict_file(path)` (the key file's permissions, §3.7.4), `run_location_problem(exe)` (macOS App Translocation; Windows always None, §3.7.5), and for updates `update_asset_name(version)`, `verify_update(path)` and `apply_update(path, exe)` (§3.7.9).
- `windows.py`: `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, `winreg` for the Run key, the Windows WoW candidates. `macos.py`: `~/Library/Application Support`, `plistlib` and `launchctl` for the LaunchAgent, the macOS candidates, translocation detection, `codesign` for `verify_update`. `__init__.py` holds `current()`, the **one place** that reads `sys.platform`.
- Each module is a few dozen lines with no business logic, and takes injected fakes (a registry-like object, a plist folder, a `run` callable for `launchctl`, `codesign` and the installer), so **both are tested on every OS** (DR9–DR11, DR22, DR23).
- **Guardrail (DR16).** `tests/test_conventions.py` scans `ahsync/`, `ahsync_app/` and `tools/` and fails, naming the file and line, when `sys.platform`, `os.name`, `winreg`, `plistlib`, `launchctl` or `platform.system` appears outside `ahsync_app/platform/` and outside an explicit allowlist of places that predate the layer or cannot import it: `ahsync/__main__.py`'s `InstanceLock` (`msvcrt` on Windows, `fcntl` elsewhere; `ahsync/` must not import `ahsync_app/`), `tools/deploy.py`'s detached start of AHSync, `tools/bench.py`'s memory units and `tools/build.py`'s choice of packaging step. The allowlist names each file; an entry whose file no longer holds a token fails too, so it cannot go stale.

#### 3.7.3 Paths

**Built (M22)**: the per-user folders (`app_dirs`, DR1) and the candidate lists (`wow_candidates`; `_classic_beta_`, `_classic_`, `_classic_era_`, `_anniversary_`). The probe `ahsync/app/wow_paths.py` is built (M23): `detect` over the candidates the shell passes in (`ahsync/` can't import the platform layer) and `check`, which only reads.

- **Packaged**: Windows `%LOCALAPPDATA%\AHSync\`, macOS `~/Library/Application Support/AHSync/`; each holds `config.toml` and `data/` (database, backups, log, lock, stop file, `updates/`). Not Roaming: the database is large and machine-local. `platform.current().app_dirs()` decides them and the shell passes them to `run()` as `--data` and `--config` (DR1).
- **From source**: `python -m ahsync` keeps `./data` and `./config.toml`, so the developer loop, `tools/deploy.py --restart` and the snapshot tools are untouched (DR1). A developer's `./data` is never migrated to the per-user folder.
- **The WoW folder** (`ahsync/app/wow_paths.py`, shared) probes `platform.current().wow_candidates()`: Windows `C:\Program Files (x86)\World of Warcraft\<name>`, macOS `/Applications/World of Warcraft/<name>`, `<name>` from a list with `_classic_beta_` first (the Nov 4 launch may rename it, §1). A folder is valid when `Interface/AddOns` exists; when `Interface` exists but `AddOns` does not, as in a fresh client, `AddOns` is created when setup saves; a Check only reads (DR6). The macOS path is **UNVERIFIED** (§6 item 20).

#### 3.7.4 First-run setup

**Built (M23)**: `ahsync/app/wow_paths.py`, `ahsync/app/setup.py` (the checks and the writes), `ahsync/web/setup_app.py` (setup mode's requests, sharing `web/server.py`'s Host, Origin and token checks and its server class), `web/static/setup.html`, `engine/app_view.wow_check` and `app_state`, `ahsync_app/app_info.py` (the payload's facts) and the shell's setup mode and hand-over. Until M24 the setup's start at login is asked of the platform, which answers "not built" (logged); `autostart.enabled` reads false. Until M25 `update` is "not checked yet" ("not checked for a dev build" from source). `python -m ahsync` has no app shell, so its dashboard answers `GET /api/app` with `404`.

- **Setup mode.** When `config.toml` is missing, and only then (an existing config whose `wow_path` has no `Interface/AddOns` runs normally with FM49's amber Addon line; setup never rewrites an existing config, whose other keys it would lose), the shell starts `web/server.py` in setup mode on `dashboard_port`: `GET /` redirects (302) to `GET /setup`, which serves `web/static/setup.html` with the per-run token embedded as on the main page; `GET /api/app` (below) answers; every `GET /api/views/*` is `404 {"error": "no_refresh_yet"}` until setup is done (DR5). The window opens on it (the browser only in the FM56 fallback).
- **The page** (`docs/mockups/dashboard.html`, "Setup"; it shows alone at `/setup`, with no page bar): "Set up AHSync" and the lede; **1. Your WoW: Forever folder**, a text field pre-filled with the detected folder (the page cannot pick a folder; a native folder dialog through the window is a v1 non-goal, §3.7.14), the check line and a **Check** button; **2. Your market**, Ruleset (Normal, PvP, RP) and Faction (Alliance, Horde) dropdowns with the market id they make ("→ forever.pvp.horde.us"); **3. auctionhouse.fyi key**, a password field, optional ("Stored only on this computer, in your user folder. It never appears on this page or in the log."); the **Start AHSync when I log in** checkbox, on by default; **Finish and start**.
- **Detection and the check text** (`app/wow_paths.py`, texts by `engine/app_view.py`; DR6): a found folder gives the pill "found" and "found Interface/AddOns · 2 accounts" (the count of `WTF/Account/*` folders; "no accounts yet" with none); nothing found gives an empty field, the pill "not found" (amber) and "not found: enter the folder that holds Interface and WTF", with Finish disabled until a Check passes; a typed folder without the subfolder gives "no Interface/AddOns under C:\Games\WoW". **Check** sends `POST /api/setup {"wow_path": …, "check_only": true}` and gets `200 {"ok": true|false, "text": …}`.
- **Saving.** `POST /api/setup {"wow_path", "ruleset", "faction", "api_key", "autostart"}` follows the §3.5 Security rules (SEC13). Validation: the folder has `Interface/AddOns`; the ruleset is `normal`, `pvp` or `rp` (`config.RULESETS`); the faction is `alliance` or `horde`; `api_key` is optional and stripped of whitespace; `autostart` a bool. A bad field is `400 {"error": "bad_request", "message": "ruleset must be normal, pvp or rp"}` with nothing written (DR8). Then, in order: `config.toml` is written (temp file + rename) holding `markets = ["forever.pvp.horde.us"]`, `wow_path` and a comment pointing at `docs/config.toml`; the key file `~/.ahsync/api_key` is written when a key was given, then `platform.current().restrict_file` (0600 on POSIX; a Windows profile folder is already the user's), so the existing lookup (env var, key file, config; §3.6) finds it; start at login is set when asked (§3.7.5); the addon is installed (§3.7.7). The answer is `200 {"set": true, "restart_wow": true|false}` (`restart_wow` true when the install added or removed files). The key never appears in a response or a log line (DR7).
- **Hand-over.** The shell stops the setup server and starts the normal run. The button reads "Starting AHSync…" with the line "first sync and refresh, about a minute"; the page polls `GET /api/markets` and reloads into the dashboard when it answers.
- **Failure.** When `config.toml` was written but the key file could not be, the answer is `500 {"error": "key_not_saved", "message": "the key could not be saved: <reason>"}`, the config is kept, the Status line `api_key_not_saved` is set, and no message carries the key (FM50). The page shows the server's `message` in a red alert and keeps the fields.
- **The key** must be one line of printable ASCII without spaces, at most 256 characters (it travels in an HTTP header); otherwise `400` "api_key must be one line of letters, digits and punctuation, at most 256 characters", never quoting it. **The key file** is written as a new temp file readable by the user only (mode 0600 where the OS has modes), renamed over `api_key` (a failed write keeps an earlier key file) and then passed to `restrict_file`. **The folder** is read as the user meant it: one pair of surrounding quotes removed (Windows' "Copy as path"), a leading `~` expanded; a relative folder never passes. A second save after a successful one answers `409 {"error": "already_set"}`; a `config.toml` that can't be written answers `500 {"error": "config_not_written", "message": "the settings could not be saved: check the folder can be written"}`, naming no path. The Status line `api_key_not_saved` (topic `api_key_setup`, apart from FM25's `api_key`; warn: "API key not saved", "Enter it again, or set AUCTIONHOUSE_APIKEY.") is cleared by a later successful setup and by any start that finds a key (M23). A step after the save that fails (start at login) is logged and the hand-over goes ahead. Setup mode logs to `data/ahsync.log` with the key added to the redactor before any write, prints "Setup: http://localhost:8765/setup", and ends, like `run()`, on Ctrl-C or the stop file (`tools/deploy.py --restart`, the installer).
- **Re-running setup** later is a non-goal: edit `config.toml`, or delete it to start over. Setup writes only a missing `config.toml`, never over an existing one (DR5).
- **`GET /api/app`** (D97) is served on every page and in setup mode; every text is made by `engine/app_view.py`:

  ```
  {"version": "0.3.1", "version_text": "0.3.1 · built 2 Nov", "dev": false, "channel": "stable",
   "setup": false,                                           -- true while the setup server answers
   "wow_path": "C:\\Program Files (x86)\\World of Warcraft\\_classic_beta_",
   "wow_check": {"ok": true, "pill": "found", "text": "found Interface/AddOns · 2 accounts"},
   "markets": {"rulesets": [["normal", "Normal"], ["pvp", "PvP"], ["rp", "RP"]],
               "factions": [["alliance", "Alliance"], ["horde", "Horde"]]},
   "api_key_set": true,
   "autostart": {"enabled": true, "available": true, "note": ""},   -- note: the reason when not available
   "auto_update": true,
   "update": {"text": "AHSync 0.4.0 downloaded · installs at the next start · you run 0.3.1",   -- always present; "not checked yet" before the first check
              "url": "https://github.com/ctng1213/WowAuction/releases/tag/v0.4.0" | null,
              "pill": "update ready · 0.4.0" | "update available" | null, "restart": true, "class": "amber" | "pos" | "muted" | ""},
   "location_problem": null | "AHSync is running from where it was downloaded. Drag AHSync to Applications and open it from there, then Start at login can be turned on.",
   "addon": {"text": "AHProfit installed · up to date", "class": ""},
   "data_folder": "%LOCALAPPDATA%\\AHSync"}                   -- unexpanded: "~/Library/Application Support/AHSync" on macOS (M25)
  ```

  `wow_path` is the configured (or detected) folder: the one path outside `data/` a response may carry, because the user typed it (§3.5 Security). `pill` is the top bar's update pill, hidden when null; `restart` says whether **Restart to update** is offered.

#### 3.7.5 Start at login

**Built (M24)**: the platform members (§3.7.2), `ahsync_app/autostart.py` (`set_enabled`, `request`, `self_heal`, `facts`), `POST /api/autostart` through the shell's `AppHooks.set_autostart`, which keeps the Status line `autostart_not_set` (topic `autostart`), the tray's checkbox, setup's checkbox, and the Status page's "AHSync app" card with its Start at login line (`engine/app_view.autostart_line` makes `autostart` and its note).

- **Windows**: `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run`, value `AHSync` = `"<exe>" --autostart`, through `winreg` (DR9).
- **macOS**: `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.wowauction.ahsync.plist` with `Label`, `ProgramArguments` [`/Applications/AHSync.app/Contents/MacOS/AHSync`, `--autostart`], `RunAtLoad` true and no `KeepAlive` (Quit stays quit), written with `plistlib` (temp file and rename; unchanged bytes are not rewritten) and deleted to turn it off (DR10). **`launchctl` is never run** (the user, 2026-11-02, at M24): launchd loads the folder at each login, while `launchctl bootout` would stop an AHSync that login started (turning the toggle off would quit the app) and `bootstrap` would start a second instance at once. `SMAppService` and Login Items are not used.
- **Self-heal.** While it is on, each start of a build rewrites the entry with the current executable path, so a moved app keeps starting; a source run never does, so it can't take over the installed app's entry (M24). Enabling twice leaves one entry.
- **App Translocation (macOS).** An executable under `/private/var/folders/…/AppTranslocation/` is refused: `autostart_enable` is not called, nothing is written, and `GET /api/app` carries `location_problem` with the text above; the Status card's Start at login reads "not set: Move AHSync to Applications, then turn it on again" (amber), and the setup page shows the alert (DR11). The dmg shows an Applications shortcut for this reason (§3.7.13).
- **Controls.** The setup checkbox, the tray checkbox and the Status card's toggle all call `ahsync_app/autostart.py`; the card's toggle sends `POST /api/autostart {"enabled": true|false}` → `200 {"set": true, "enabled": true}`; a non-bool is `400`; a translocated location `400 {"error": "bad_location", "message": "Move AHSync to Applications, then turn it on again"}`; a registry or plist write that raises is `500 {"error": "autostart_not_set"}` with the Status line "Start at login not set" (`autostart_not_set`, cleared by the next success) and the toggle showing the real state (FM51). `GET /api/autostart` is `405` (SEC14). The installer never sets it: the app is the one source of truth.

#### 3.7.6 Single instance

**Built (M22)** in `ahsync_app/shell.py`; the shell keeps the lock it took and hands it to `run(…, held_lock=lock)`, which uses it and leaves its release to the shell (a second `InstanceLock` in the same process would be refused). `InstanceLock` on `<data>/ahsync.lock` stays (§5.2). When the packaged shell finds it held, it writes `<data>/ahsync.show` (the stop file's pattern, FM36) and exits 0; the running shell's loop sees the file within a second, removes it and shows its window on the dashboard (§3.7.1; built M24; the browser in the FM56 fallback), so a second click on the icon brings the dashboard up (FM48). A launch with `--autostart` that finds it held exits 0 and writes nothing: a login start never pops the window up over an AHSync that is already running (§3.7.5; FM48; the first reason, `launchctl bootstrap` starting the job at once, went with `launchctl` at M24). `python -m ahsync` still prints "AHSync is already running" (FM15). Only the lock owner installs the addon, so two processes on one data folder never write the same files; two data folders can (a developer's `./data` and the per-user folder): see the risks in §3.7.14.

#### 3.7.7 Addon install at every start

**Built (M22)**: `ahsync/export/addon_install.py` (`install_addon`, `AddonResult`, the Addon line from `engine/app_view.addon_text`); `tools/deploy.py` imports it. The card and `GET /api/app` that show the line are planned (M23, M25).

- `tools/deploy.py`'s `Plan`, `plan_mirror`, `plan_data_addon` and `apply` move to `ahsync/export/addon_install.py` (stdlib); `deploy.py` imports them and its CLI is unchanged. The addon is bundled with the app (`addon/` as PyInstaller data), so the app installs the addon it was built with.
- The shell calls `install_addon(bundle_addon_dir, wow_path)` at each start before `run()`. The rules stay: `AHProfit/` is mirrored and stale files inside it removed; `AHProfit_Data/` gets the `.toc` and the placeholders only when none exists; the generated `MarketIntelligence.lua` and `PortfolioPlan.lua` are never overwritten (§3.5; DR3, DR4).
- **The Addon line** on the Status card (D98) and `GET /api/app`'s `addon`: files added or removed → "AHProfit updated · restart WoW to load it" (WoW loads only files present at client start, §3.5); existing files changed only → "AHProfit updated · /reload in game"; nothing to do → "AHProfit installed · up to date"; no `Interface/AddOns` under `wow_path` → "not installed: no Interface/AddOns under C:\Games\WoW" (amber); not writable → "not installed: Interface/AddOns not writable" (amber). The folder and a write probe (a temp file in `AddOns/`, removed) are checked before any copy, and each file is written to a temp name and renamed, so in the last two cases nothing is copied, no file is ever half-written, and the app runs on (FM49).

#### 3.7.8 Update check

**Built (M25)**: `ahsync/app/update_check.py` (`check`, `due`, `read_state`, `read_meta`/`write_meta` over `meta` `update.*`), `ahsync/engine/updates.py` (`parse_tag`, `newer`, `pick_release`, `UpdateState`, `update_text`), run by the scheduler's `update` job every 10 minutes (the first at start) only when the app shell is around (`run()` gets `AppHooks`). The request is one plain GET through the `Transport`, not `ApiClient`: the client would attach the AHF key and retry with a log line each time, and GitHub must never see the key (DR15) and a failure is one line and a day's wait (FM52). Switching the channel asks again at the next job. The tray's update items, the Status card's Update line and the top bar's pill read the same state.

- `ahsync/app/update_check.py` asks GitHub at start and then daily, with one plain GET through the `Transport` (never `ApiClient`, so never the AHF key: the key goes only to auctionhouse.fyi, DR15), with `Accept: application/vnd.github+json` and `If-None-Match` (the stored ETag; a `304` changes nothing). The unauthenticated limit of 60 an hour is ample. Stable channel: `GET https://api.github.com/repos/ctng1213/WowAuction/releases/latest`, which never returns a pre-release or a draft, so a beta is invisible. Beta channel: `GET …/releases?per_page=20`, taking the highest `vX.Y.Z` among them, pre-releases included (DR25). The result goes to `meta` (`update.etag`, `update.tag`, `update.url`, `update.checked_ms`); a second check within 24 h sends nothing (DR15).
- **Comparison and texts** are pure (`engine/updates.py`): `vX.Y.Z` is parsed and compared with the running version by numeric parts; a tag with any suffix (`v0.4.0-rc1`) is ignored; equal or older is "up to date" (DR13, DR14, DR24).
- **Shown** in three places: the Status card's Update line (D98), the top bar's pill on every page (from `GET /api/app`) and the tray (§3.7.1). Notify-only (`auto_update` false): "AHSync 0.4.0 is available · you run 0.3.1" (amber, linking the release page), pill "update available".
- **Switches.** A dev build (`0.0.0`) never asks; `update_check = false` turns the check off (§3.6).
- **Failures** (FM52): offline, `404` (no release yet), `403` (rate limit) or a body without `tag_name` show no alert and no pill, write one log line and wait until tomorrow; the card's Update line (muted) reads "not checked: offline since Fri 06:00" when the transport failed, "not checked: no release yet" on `404`, and "not checked: GitHub unavailable since Fri 06:00" on `403` or a bad body (the time is the first failed check since the last success).

#### 3.7.9 Automatic updates

**Built (M25), the download half (steps 1, 2, 4 and the failures before an install)**: `ahsync/app/updater.py` (`download`, `fetch_verified`, `parse_sums`) after each check; the files come from `https://github.com/ctng1213/WowAuction/releases/download/<tag>/<name>`; a body shorter than its Content-Length, a network error (including a read cut off mid-body, now a `TransportError` in `UrllibTransport`) or any status but 200 is "download cut off"; a failed download is tried again a day later; an older file in `updates/` is removed when a newer one is verified. `platform.update_asset_name` is built for both OSes; `verify_update` is M27's, and until then the shell accepts what the hash accepted. The toggles `POST /api/update/auto` and the tray's item go through the same `DashboardApp.set_auto_update`. Steps 3 (signatures), 5 and 6 are M27's. **Only what the channel still offers waits** (DR30): a download counts as waiting (`engine/updates.ready`) only while the check's tag is that version, `auto_update` is on and it is not a pre-release on the stable channel; otherwise the next update job removes the file, and M27's install asks the same. A channel switch forgets the offered release at once. A failed try, or a signature check that raises ("could not be verified"), leaves `updates/` empty, and the next try is a day after the last one whatever the reason. The release page is built from the tag, never taken from GitHub's answer. Known limit: the asset is read whole on the scheduler's thread (a few tens of MB once a day).

With `auto_update` true (the default) a newer release found by §3.7.8 is downloaded, verified and installed. Everything but the final swap is shared code (`ahsync/app/updater.py`; texts in `engine/updates.py`):

1. **Download** the asset for this OS, `platform.current().update_asset_name(version)`: `AHSync-<ver>-windows-x64-setup.exe` on Windows, `AHSync-<ver>-macos-arm64.zip` on macOS, into `<data>/updates/`, temp file + rename. A failed or cut-off download deletes the partial file; the next try is tomorrow.
2. **Verify the hash**: the release's `SHA256SUMS` (one `<sha256>  <asset>` line per asset) is downloaded and compared; a mismatch, or an asset missing from it, deletes the file (FM53).
3. **Verify the signature** where there is one: `platform.current().verify_update(path)`. macOS unpacks the zip with `ditto -xk` into `updates/` (never Python's `zipfile`, which drops the bundle's symlinks and executable bits and so breaks both the signature and the launch), runs `codesign --verify --deep --strict` and checks the Team ID equals the running app's; a zip validly signed by anyone else is refused (DR23). Windows verifies nothing beyond the hash (the installer is unsigned).
4. **Record** `meta` `update.downloaded` (version and path): the Status line reads "AHSync 0.4.0 downloaded · installs at the next start · you run 0.3.1" (amber, linked) with the **Restart to update** button, the pill "update ready · 0.4.0", and the tray offers **Restart to update (0.4.0)** (DR20). On the beta channel a pre-release reads "AHSync 0.4.1 (beta) downloaded · installs at the next start · you run 0.4.0".
5. **Install** at the next start, or now on **Restart to update** (the tray item, or `POST /api/update/restart` → `200 {"restarting": true}`; `409 {"error": "no_update"}` when nothing is downloaded; SEC15), which sets the stop event. Start at login restarts the app at each login, so a machine that is logged off daily takes the update within a day without a click; a machine left on waits for the next start or the click, and INSTALL.md says so. The shell calls `platform.current().apply_update(path, exe)` only after `run()` has returned and the lock is released, so no refresh or database write is running; it writes `meta` `update.pending = <version>` first.
   - **Windows**: the installer is started detached with `/VERYSILENT /SUPPRESSMSGBOXES /NORESTART /CLOSEAPPLICATIONS`, through a `cmd /c` wrapper that relaunches the current `<exe>` only when the installer exits non-zero, and the shell exits; Inno Setup upgrades the per-user install in place and its `[Run]` entry restarts AHSync after a successful silent install (DR22). A file the app downloaded itself has no mark-of-the-web, so SmartScreen does not interrupt.
   - **macOS**: the shell writes a small detached `/bin/sh` helper and exits; the helper waits for the process to end, moves the running bundle (derived from the executable, `…/AHSync.app/Contents/MacOS/AHSync`, three folders up; never a constant path) to `AHSync.app.previous`, moves the new app into place and opens it; when the move or the launch fails it puts the previous app back and opens that (FM55). When the bundle's parent folder is not writable by the user nothing is attempted and the Update line reads "Update downloaded · AHSync can't replace itself here; install it from the release page" (amber, linked; FM54).
6. **Record the result** at the next start, from `update.pending`: running the pending version → "Updated to AHSync 0.4.0 · today 06:02" (`update.result`, shown until the next check finds nothing newer); still on the old one → "Update to 0.4.0 failed · still on 0.3.1 · tries again tomorrow" (amber). Either way the installer or unpacked app in `updates/` is removed; when Windows still holds the installer, because its process is finishing while the relaunched AHSync starts, the removal is skipped without failing the start and retried at the next start (DR22). A failure before the install (hash, signature, download) reads "Update to 0.4.0 failed: <reason> · still on 0.3.1 · tries again tomorrow", the reason being "download cut off", "checksum mismatch", "not in SHA256SUMS" or "not signed by the AHSync developer" (FM53, DR23). An installer that exits non-zero leaves the old install running with the same line (FM55).

**Switches**: `auto_update` (config, §3.6) and the **Install updates automatically** toggle (`POST /api/update/auto {"enabled": false}` → `200 {"set": true, "enabled": false}`, rewriting only that config line like `risk_tolerance`, §3.5; SEC15); `update_channel` and **Get beta updates** (§3.7.10). A dev build never downloads or installs.

**Safety rules**: never an equal or older version, never a suffixed tag, never a pre-release on the stable channel (DR24, DR25); never an install while `run()` is alive; the new version migrates the database at its first start, covered by the migration backups (§5.2), and there is no downgrade; the addon follows through the install at start (§3.7.7), so an app update also brings the matching addon and the "restart WoW" line.

#### 3.7.10 Release stages: beta, then GA

**Built (M25), the channel half**: `update_channel`, `POST /api/update/channel` and the tray's Get beta updates (`DashboardApp.set_update_channel`), the beta channel's list check and the version pill. Leaving the beta is remembered in `meta` `update.left_beta`, which gives the "stays on 0.4.1 until a newer stable release" line (DR26) only to an install that switched channel; an older stable tag otherwise reads "up to date" (DR14). **Built (M26), the release half**: `tools/build.py release` (the `release` job, `SHA256SUMS`, the pre-release with a first notes line naming `INSTALL.md`), `tools/promote.py` and `promote.yml`; promotion also refuses when the latest release can't be read.

- **Beta.** Pushing a tag `vX.Y.Z` builds, signs and notarizes (§3.7.12), and the `release` job publishes a GitHub **pre-release** with `gh release create vX.Y.Z --prerelease --generate-notes <assets>` (DR28). Stable installs don't see it (`releases/latest` skips pre-releases). The user's own installs, on the beta channel, pick it up at their next start through the normal download, verify and install, on the Windows PC and the Mac.
- **GA.** The user runs the **Promote release** workflow (`promote.yml`, `workflow_dispatch` with the tag) or `python tools/promote.py v0.4.0`, which runs `gh release edit v0.4.0 --prerelease=false --latest`. The same Release, with the same signed files, becomes the latest release; stable installs update from it within a day. Nothing is rebuilt, so friends get exactly the files the user tested.
- **`tools/promote.py`** refuses, with the reason and no edit, unless the Release exists, is still a pre-release and lists every asset (`AHSync-<ver>-windows-x64-setup.exe`, `AHSync-<ver>-macos-arm64.dmg`, `AHSync-<ver>-macos-arm64.zip`, `SHA256SUMS`); it also refuses a tag lower than the current latest release (DR27).
- **A bad beta**: delete the Release and its tag, or leave it a pre-release; fix; tag the next patch version. A skipped version number costs nothing.
- **The channel**: `update_channel = "stable"` or `"beta"` (§3.6); the **Get beta updates** toggle (`POST /api/update/channel {"channel": "beta"}` → `200 {"set": true, "channel": "beta"}`, `400` for any other value; SEC15). On the beta channel the Status card's version pill reads "0.4.0 · beta · built 2 Nov". Switching from beta to stable never downgrades: the install stays on its version until a newer stable release exists, and the Update line reads "up to date · stays on 0.4.1 until a newer stable release" (DR26).

#### 3.7.11 Versioning

- Git tags `vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`, the first `v0.1.0`. No `-beta` suffix: a beta is the same version as the GA it becomes. A suffixed tag is ignored by the update check and never released.
- `ahsync/version.py` (**built** M22) holds `VERSION = "0.0.0"` and `BUILT = ""` in source; the workflow writes the tag and the build date (`"2026-11-02"`) into it before PyInstaller runs, so a release needs no version-bump commit. The Status card's pill reads "0.3.1 · built 2 Nov"; a source run reads "dev build" and checks for no update (DR2). `EXPORT_VERSION` (the addon file format, §3.5) stays separate.

#### 3.7.12 Build, signing and release

**Built (M26)**: `packaging/ahsync.spec` (one recipe; the code reads its data files next to its modules, so `ahsync/data`, `db/schema.sql`, `web/static` and `ahsync_app/icon.png` are bundled beside `addon/`), `packaging/windows/ahsync.iss`, `packaging/macos/make_dmg.sh` and `entitlements.plist` (`allow-unsigned-executable-memory` only, until the first notarization says otherwise, §6 item 21), `tools/build.py` (`policy`, `build`, `release`) and the `build` and `release` jobs. What the build showed: **the dmg is signed and notarized itself** before it is stapled, since `stapler` looks a ticket up by the file's own signature and the app's ticket does not cover the dmg (DR18); **Apple's Developer ID intermediates are imported with the certificate** (`packaging/macos/certs/`, public files from apple.com/certificateauthority): a runner may lack them, and `find-identity -v` then hides the identity as untrusted; **the `.p12` password never reaches a command line**: `/usr/bin/openssl` reads it from the environment and re-encrypts the certificate with a throwaway password, which is all `security import` sees; **the `build` job also runs on pull requests** (unsigned, decision 10), so a packaging change is built before it merges; the installer, after the lock frees, also waits up to 10 s for `AHSync.exe` to end (the app releases its lock about a second before its process exits); `ahsync_app/__main__.py` keeps a frozen build's `sys.path` (the from-source fix would drop PyInstaller's folder). A real onedir build on Windows ran through setup to the dashboard and installed the addon (2026-11-02). The signed path, the Inno Setup compile and the dmg are **UNVERIFIED** until CI and the user's v0.1.0 run.

- **PyInstaller 6.x, onedir, windowed**: one `packaging/ahsync.spec` with at most a few lines that differ by OS (the icon; macOS's `BUNDLE` with `LSUIElement` true, so there is no Dock icon). `addon/` is bundled as data; hidden imports for pystray's and pywebview's backends (and `pythonnet`'s runtime files on Windows). `.gitignore` gets `!packaging/*.spec`.
- **`tools/build.py`** runs PyInstaller, then the current OS's one packaging step: Windows, Inno Setup over `packaging/windows/ahsync.iss` → `AHSync-<ver>-windows-x64-setup.exe`; macOS, `packaging/macos/make_dmg.sh` (`hdiutil create` of a folder with `AHSync.app` and an `Applications` symlink) → `AHSync-<ver>-macos-arm64.dmg`, plus `ditto -c -k --keepParent` → `AHSync-<ver>-macos-arm64.zip`, the asset the updater installs. Its command lines are tested with a fake runner; no real build runs in tests (DR17). It also decides the signing policy and writes `SHA256SUMS`, so the workflow stays thin and the decisions are tested (DR19, DR28).
- **The Windows installer** (Inno Setup 6, `packaging/windows/ahsync.iss`): per user, no admin (`PrivilegesRequired=lowest`), into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\AHSync`; a Start menu shortcut and an optional desktop one; "Launch AHSync" on the last page; an upgrade over an existing version keeps config and data, which live outside the install folder (§3.7.3); a fixed `AppId`, so an upgrade is recognised, and `[InstallDelete]` of `{app}\_internal`, so no stale PyInstaller file survives one; before copying, the installer and the uninstaller quit a running AHSync the way `tools/deploy.py --restart` does (write `<data>/ahsync.stop`, wait for the lock): Inno Setup's `CloseApplications` asks the app's windows to close, and the window cancels its close (§3.7.1), so it would hang; two `[Run]` entries, "Launch AHSync" for an interactive install (`postinstall skipifsilent`) and the relaunch after a silent upgrade (`nowait`, `Check: WizardSilent`), so no install launches it twice (§3.7.9); uninstall removes the Run value in `[Code]` (a `[Registry]` entry would set it on install, which the installer never does) and leaves `%LOCALAPPDATA%\AHSync` and the key file unless the user answers Yes to "Also remove my AHSync data (settings, database, log and your auctionhouse.fyi key)?" (default No); Yes deletes `%LOCALAPPDATA%\AHSync`, `~/.ahsync/api_key` and the `.ahsync` folder when it is then empty (the user, 2026-11-02: a plain-text credential left behind is not what "remove my data" means; a source run that shares the key file loses it, a developer-only case). It stays unsigned: SmartScreen warns once on the downloaded installer (§3.7.13).
- **macOS signing and notarization**: `AHSync.app` is signed with the user's Developer ID Application certificate and the hardened runtime (PyInstaller's `codesign_identity` and `entitlements_file`, so every bundled binary is signed; Python needs `com.apple.security.cs.allow-unsigned-executable-memory`, possibly `com.apple.security.cs.disable-library-validation`, §6 item 21), the app is submitted, zipped, with `xcrun notarytool submit --wait` and stapled with `xcrun stapler staple`; the dmg and the release zip are then made from the stapled app, and the dmg is signed, notarized and stapled itself (a dmg has no ticket of its own until it is submitted), so an app downloaded either way carries its ticket (DR18). The secrets reach only the macOS job's policy and build steps (never a pull request's), and `tools/build.py` removes the three credentials from every command's environment but openssl's (M26). Secrets, as secrets of the GitHub environment **`signing`** and nowhere else (the user, 2026-11-02; they were repository secrets in the approved proposal): the environment may deploy only from `main` and `v*` tags, and only the macOS leg of a `main`, tag or manual run names it, so a branch or pull-request run never receives them and a manual signed run works only from `main`: `MACOS_CERT_P12` (base64) and `MACOS_CERT_PASSWORD`, imported into a temporary keychain the job deletes at its end; `APPLE_TEAM_ID`; the App Store Connect API key `NOTARY_KEY_ID`, `NOTARY_ISSUER_ID`, `NOTARY_KEY_P8`. Their values never appear in the repo, logs or artifacts.
- **The signing policy** (`tools/build.py policy`, DR19), one of three modes:
  - **sign**: a tag `vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` (a suffixed tag builds unsigned and is never released; M26), or a manual run with `sign: true`. Secrets missing → the job fails before PyInstaller with "macOS signing secrets are required for a release (SPEC §3.7): missing <name>". A signing or notarization failure fails the job, so no Release is created. Fix, delete the tag, push it again.
  - **preflight**: a push to `main` once any `v*` tag exists. The app is ad-hoc signed and not notarized; the preflight checks that all six secrets are set, imports the certificate into a temporary keychain, confirms `security find-identity -v -p codesigning` lists a valid Developer ID Application identity for `APPLE_TEAM_ID`, warns when it expires within 30 days ("signing certificate expires in 10 days"), and deletes the keychain. A failure fails the job naming the missing secret or "certificate invalid". Before the first tag `main` skips it.
  - **unsigned**: development branches, pull requests, manual runs without `sign`: ad-hoc signature, no notarize, no preflight, no secrets needed. Releases come only from tags in this repo. The Windows steps are no-ops in every mode.
- **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`**: job `test` on push and pull request, on `windows-latest` and `macos-15` (arm64; `macos-latest` moved to macOS 26 and macOS 14 retires 2 Nov 2026): checkout, Python 3.12, `requirements-dev.txt`, Lua 5.1 (`leafo/gh-actions-lua`, with an MSVC dev-cmd step on Windows; fallback `brew install lua@5.1`), `pwsh -File tools/test.ps1` (PowerShell 7 is on every image; `test.ps1` stays 5.1/7-compatible). Job `build` needs `test`; runs on pushes to `main`, tags `v*`, pull requests (unsigned) and `workflow_dispatch` (input `sign`, default false); `fetch-depth: 0` so a `main` build sees the tags; steps: `requirements-app.txt` and `pyinstaller==6.22.*`, write the version, PyInstaller, sign (macOS, mode `sign` only), package, notarize (same), upload the artifacts. Job `release` needs `build`, `permissions: contents: write`, runs on tags only: `SHA256SUMS`, then the pre-release (§3.7.10). **`promote.yml`**: `workflow_dispatch` with `tag`, `contents: write`, runs `tools/promote.py`.
- **Cost**: standard runners are free on public repositories; a macOS test-plus-build run takes about 10–15 minutes.

#### 3.7.13 Installing (`INSTALL.md`, linked from every Release)

**Built (M26)**: `INSTALL.md` at the repository root; every Release's notes start with a link to it at the release's tag.

- **Windows**: SmartScreen shows "Windows protected your PC" on the downloaded installer: **More info → Run anyway**, once. Then Launch AHSync → the setup page → the dashboard, in the AHSync window. The window needs the Edge WebView2 runtime, which Windows 11 and an up-to-date Windows 10 have; without it the dashboard opens in the browser instead (FM56), and INSTALL.md links Microsoft's Evergreen runtime installer.
- **macOS**: open the dmg, **drag AHSync to Applications**, open it from there; it is signed and notarized, so there is no warning. An app opened from the dmg or Downloads runs translocated under a random path, which would break Start at login (§3.7.5), hence the Applications shortcut in the dmg and the alert. The unsigned-build steps (a fork or a local build; macOS 15+: open once, then System Settings → Privacy & Security → Open Anyway, or `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine /Applications/AHSync.app`) stay documented for those cases.
- The key sits in a plain file (`~/.ahsync/api_key`, §3.6); accepted for a friends-only tool, and said so.

#### 3.7.14 Non-goals (v1)

More than one beta tester group or staged rollouts; Windows code signing; rolling back to an older version (the previous release stays downloadable); installing in the middle of a session; Linux; an Intel macOS build; a Settings page beyond setup; a tray "Setup…" item; a native folder picker in setup (the text field stays); remembering the window's size and position; migrating a developer's `./data`; an MSI, winget or any store.

**Risks** (from the proposal §4): macOS is untested until the user's Mac runs the dmg (pystray, pywebview and pyobjc in a bundle, the two GUI libraries sharing one `NSApplication`, `LSUIElement`, entitlements, notarization, the LaunchAgent); Windows updates are verified by hash only, so the GitHub account's two-factor authentication is the guard (an Ed25519 signature over `SHA256SUMS` can be added inside `verify_update` later); runner images drift (`macos-15` pinned, revisit yearly); pywebview's Windows backend needs `pythonnet` inside PyInstaller and the WebView2 runtime on the machine (the browser fallback covers a missing runtime, FM56); `tools/test.ps1` under pwsh 7 on macOS may need small fixes at the first CI run. **Two AHSyncs on one machine** (recorded, not mitigated in v1; the user's decision of 2026-11-01): the lock is per data folder, so on the developer's own PC the source instance on `./data` and the installed app on `%LOCALAPPDATA%\AHSync` can run at once and both export into the same WoW AddOns folder, and `python -m ahsync_app` from source shares the installed app's data folder and would migrate its database forward with no way back. Friends run one install and never meet it; a `--data` override for the shell is the cheap fix if it bites.

## 4. AHProfit (addon)

### 4.0 Split of work: app and addon (hybrid)

| Lives in AHSync (heavy, rarely) | Lives in the addon (light, as state changes) |
|---|---|
| all history reads, statistics, weekly patterns, the queue simulation, the plan (§3.1.1) | current listings from in-game scans and searches, your bags and gold right now |
| precomputed **options** per item: sell and hold values, DE EV, crafting options, flip candidates, sell-probability curves | **choosing between precomputed options** when state changes (a price moves, you buy, you post) |
| the dashboard | the AH window panel, tooltip, one-click searches |

**The addon never reads history and never simulates.** It tracks state, reads the market intelligence, and builds its own recommendations (§5.0 invariant 4):
- AHSync exports **market intelligence** in `MarketIntelligence.lua` (per-item baselines, curves, windows, volume and supply, the disenchant table) and, optionally, its cross-character plan in `PortfolioPlan.lua` (§3.5).
- **The addon's own recommendations** (`Recommend/Options.lua`): Disenchant, Craft, Flip and Sell options for the **shortlist** in the intelligence (tier 2 items plus what you hold and your recipes), from the intelligence plus live state (bags, gold, recipes, prices it just saw), using the ported formulas. They follow the same economic rules as the desktop's: shared scenarios check that identical inputs give identical profit numbers (LP1). With no `PortfolioPlan.lua`, or an old one, the addon still recommends; the plan only adds cross-character advice (transfers, which character acts).
- **Built so far** (A15–A18): sell options for what the logged-in character carries. Each unbound item in the bags with market data gets its best option, posting now (the intelligence's sell value and posting-now chance, `chance1000`) or a merchant (`GetItemInfo`'s sell price), shown on Today with the note "post now · AHSync has no plan for it" (since M13: the Portfolio tab without a portfolio snapshot, and the AH panel's Post list). Items AHSync's plan already has an action for keep the plan's row.
  - **Live prices** (A2, A19): the scanner keeps the last complete full scan's order book in memory (`Scan/Replicate.lua`), and `Recommend/Live.lua` reads an item's listings from it when it is newer than the market file's newest observation. Your own sell rows are re-priced from it the way analysis prices them: post at `min(cheapest − 1, max(med7, p90))`, capped at `med7` alone under 24 closes (`Engine/Prices.lua`, shared scenarios PP1, PP2, PP4), `P` from the curve row for this hour at the units ahead (a tooltip-only item has one row, used at any hour, EX19; an item with volume unknown has no curve: `SellProb.DefaultChance` for the post's position in the live book, A67), `E_post` with the merchant price as the fallback and the deposit for the posting-now window; the note says "post now at the live price". A planned buy with no units left at or below its price is dropped from Today, and the footer counts it ("2 gone"). A finished scan redraws the open window.
  - **Crafts** (A9, A20): for the logged-in character's known recipes that AHSync's plan doesn't already craft, `Engine/Craft.lua`'s `Option` (a port of `craft_option` without crafted intermediates, shared scenarios C1–C3) sources mats from the bags at their hold value and from the AH (the live book, else `cur × curQty`), and counts crafts while each still pays. The best-paying craft goes first and each uses what the ones before it left; the mats they use get no sell row. The product's output is valued with its posting-now chance for any quantity (the desktop simulates per quantity, so the two agree when the chance doesn't depend on it). The note says where the mats come from ("from your own mats", "mats from the AH: 60s", "your mats topped up from the AH: 20s"). Vendor-sold mats are bought at the vendor price from the market file's `vendor` table (EX6) when that is cheaper than the AH, as on the desktop (R5). The note says where the mats come from ("mats from a vendor: 2s 66c"). Crafts stop at the product's liquidity cap for the posting-now window, as the plan's do: `max(refStack, floor(max_supply_share × supply))` units, less the product you already hold, divided by the average yield, at least 1 while any units are left (`capacity.liquidity_cap`; A30, A36). When what you hold fills the cap, the Craft tab shows the recipe as made: a green check and "made", with a note to post it (A36). Your own auctions count like the units you hold, until their end passes, and the made note says "×1 on the AH · ends in 11 h" (A42). The addon reads them into `ownAuctions` when the AH opens and after a post or a cancel (A41). UNVERIFIED on Forever: `C_AuctionHouse.QueryOwnedAuctions`, `GetNumOwnedAuctions`, `GetOwnedAuctionInfo` (fields `itemLink`, `itemKey`, `status`, `quantity`, `timeLeftSeconds`, `timeLeft`, `buyoutAmount`), `OWNED_AUCTIONS_UPDATED`, `AUCTION_HOUSE_AUCTION_CREATED` and `AUCTION_CANCELED`; the diagnostics probe them. Without the current supply the cap uses the item's typical (median daily) supply from the market file (EX10, A57); with neither there is no cap. The supply pattern over the window is applied too (EX13, A62): for shortlisted items the market file carries `S[key]`, 24 characters, one per hour from the refresh, each the factor `window_supply` gives for a posting-now window starting that hour (mean supply over the window ÷ supply at its start), in 5% steps (`char(35 + floor(f × 20 + 0.5))`, 0–2.5); the cap multiplies the supply by the factor for the current hour (the curve's row rule). The full 168-slot pattern per item doesn't fit the file's 2 MB budget, so other items get no factor (1).
  - **Disenchant** (A23) and **Craft** (A27) tabs: every target or known recipe that pays, each on its own. The Craft tab first lists the crafts AHSync's plan has for the character (A63: source "Plan", the chain's mat buys as lines, its profit and when to sell), so it agrees with Shopping's CRAFT buys; the addon's own options follow, for recipes the plan doesn't craft, with their mats within the character's live cap (A64, rewritten for the exposure budget in M12; §3.4 Risk): min(its live gold, the snapshot's remaining capacity − what the funded plan reserves for the other characters), the same cap the live reselection spends (`Adapt.Cap`, below); without a portfolio snapshot, its live gold. In the mockup, Amaya with 4 g 55 s may spend 2 g 76 s 40 c on an own craft's mats.
  - **Flip** (A28): the plan's flips, re-checked live. The addon also offers flips of its own, one reference stack each, from the market file's flip offers (EX11, A59): during the full refresh AHSync works out, market-wide, each flip candidate's expected value per unit at its best window (`flips.flip_offer`, the plan's `slot_price` and `expected_post` with the posting-now chance); the Flip tab lists an offer when the cheapest price seen now is at or below `floor(expected ÷ (1 + min_flip_margin))`, for items AHSync's plan doesn't flip and no rule blocks. **Flip-now offers** (A69, FN1) need nothing new in the market file: once per complete live scan, `Live.FlipCandidates` lists the items whose two cheapest price levels pass `(second − 1) × (1 − cut) ≥ (1 + min_flip_margin) × cheapest` (the test with a chance of 1); for each with market data the Flip tab prices buying the cheapest level (its units, at most one reference stack) and relisting now at `Prices.PostPrice(second, med7, p90)`, with `P` from the curve row for this hour at 0 units ahead (after the buy you are the cheapest), or `SellProb.DefaultChance` for the cheapest position without a curve (volume unknown). `Prices.FlipNow` (the twin of `flips.flip_now_offer`) gives `E_post(P, units, relist, merchant price, deposit)`, offered when `expected − cost ≥ min_flip_margin × cost`. The row reads "buy at 10c, relist now at 49c", When "now"; an item with both kinds of offer shows the better paying one. Sizing beyond one stack still needs the queue simulation, which only AHSync runs (§4.0).
- **Built (M12, 2026-10-19): the addon reselects the portfolio live** (§3.4D; A76–A78, A80, A83, DG1–DG7, FM39; A79, A82 and A84 came with M13, A81 with M14). The desktop's funded plan is the starting suggestion, and the addon adapts the logged-in character's part of it. `Recommend/Adapt.lua` holds the live state, built the first time anything asks (never at login, §5.1); `Recommend/Reselect.lua` is the pure step from candidates and live state to a selection; `UI/PortfolioView.lua` turns it into the Portfolio tab's data, drawn by `UI/MainWindow.lua` since M13 (§4.3).
  - **Inputs.** From `PortfolioPlan.lua`: the portfolio snapshot (`Plan.Portfolio`: the remaining capacity, the scoring settings, the list limits, the snapshot time), each character's gold and reservation (`Plan.Gold`, `Plan.Reserved`, `Plan.Characters`) and the positions. From `MarketIntelligence.lua`: this character's candidates in `K` (`Market.Candidate`; other characters' rows never enter the live state) and the dependency graph `G` (`Market.Graph`).
  - **Live state**: the character's gold (`Adapter.Money`, `PLAYER_MONEY`); what it holds in bags, bank and mail (a candidate's inputs from its own stock claim `own:<char>:<key>`, limited to what it holds now; before the first inventory snapshot they are not checked); its professions as last captured (§2.2; without a snapshot the exported profession status stands); the last complete scan's book and search results (`Live.Item`); and recipes captured when a profession window opens.
  - **Repricing through the graph** (`Engine/Graph.lua`; its route costs and candidate deltas are the twins of `engine.graph.route_costs` and `repriced`, shared scenario DG1). `Graph.Load` loads `G` into recipes, the reverse index and the order. Route costs are computed when the state is built, once at the market file's prices (what the export was valued at) and once at the live ones. An item's route is the cheaper of buying it (the live book's cheapest, else the file's `cur`) and crafting it from reagents earlier in the order (Σ count × reagent cost × 1000 ÷ yield1000); a tie buys (DG3). A changed price marks its item dirty: `Graph.Reprice` walks the reverse index every level down, each item once, sorts what it reached by the exported order and recomputes each route once, intermediates first (DG2); unreached items keep their cached routes (DG4: a Silk change visits no copper item). A candidate that uses a reached item is recomputed: its gold needed changes by Σ count × (live cost − exported cost) over its inputs from the AH or crafted, rounded half up, and its expected net by the same delta (owned inputs keep their hold value, vendor prices don't move). A flip's or buy-for-later's buy is priced from the book: a buy due now costs what the book asks for its units, up or down, and a buy whose units are gone drops the row. A sale now is repriced as the window prices it (`Prices.RelistPrice` against the cheapest other listing, for a flip the level after the units it buys; `P` from the curve row at the units ahead, else `SellProb.DefaultChance`), by the delta of its `E_post` to the file's. Window sales keep their exported value: windows are reference data. Bursts are batched: a complete scan (`Replicate.OnBook`, after the scan is saved) and a search result (`Live.OnSearch`) only say that prices may have changed, and one debounced pass (`Events.Debounce`, so never in combat) compares the prices of the items that matter (the graph's and this character's candidates', not the whole book) with the last pass and reprices only those that changed (DG7: an ore change visits 3 recipes and the Runed Copper Rod's candidate; two 9,000-item scans and a search are one pass). The work is counted in the graph's `work` and in `Adapt.Perf()` for the tests and `/ahp perf`.
  - **Reselection** is a separate step, run only when a change could add or remove a funded row (`Reselect.NeedsReselect`, DG5): a funded row's gold or net moved, or a row excluded for a reason a price can clear moved (the selection's own reasons, a stale price, an empty book); a row excluded for a profession, a thin forecast, an unknown sales rate, liquidity or missing materials doesn't trigger one. A gold, bags or recipe change always does (debounced). It is the desktop's selection (§3.4D.4, `Engine/Portfolio.lua`, with the premium of `Engine/Timing.lua`, both in the .toc since M12) over this character's candidates. Each candidate's list (Today when its wait is immediate) and adjusted profit are worked out at now from the snapshot's settings. Its static reason is re-checked live where live state can change it (a profession against the captured skill, PF10; a stale price or an empty book against the live book); the other codes stand, shown as "Forecast too uncertain", "Sales volume unknown", "Insufficient liquidity" and "Missing materials". K carries only the code, so since M13 `PortfolioView.Reason` adds the numbers the desktop writes from the market file ("Insufficient liquidity: 3/day, 40 planned" from the item's sales rate and the batch's units; "Forecast too uncertain (1 week of data)" from its top sell window's weeks, or "(4 closes in 30 d, needs 24)" for a window sale on thin data) and `PortfolioView.Detail` the D.6 detail line (the twin of `portfolio_view.reason_detail`). A row that no longer pays (net ≤ 0) or whose buy is gone leaves the pool, and a later batch never stays without its predecessor. **The cap** (`Reselect.Cap`) is min(the character's live gold, the snapshot's remaining capacity − Σ the other characters' `reserved`): the selection's capacity is the second, the character's spendable gold its live gold. The other characters appear only with their cached gold − reserved, for the "all characters hold" check; none of their rows is selected. In the mockup Amaya's cap is 6g 49s 40c − Brugan's 3g 73s = 2g 76s 40c, and with 1 g live it is 1 g (A80). The list limits are the snapshot's; `swap_candidates` is its default, 50 (it isn't exported).
  - **Never another character's gold.** Other characters' rows and every cross-character total stay as cached in the snapshot, and are shown with its time: the top line "portfolio 35g 58s · 30% risk · capacity 6g 49s 40c · 24s 40c unallocated · snapshot 10:38", amber after 24 h, and hovering it says the totals are a cached copy (A78). The footer's unallocated is this character's live capacity left: the cap's capacity − its funded gold (A77: 24s 40c + Linen's freed 7s 60c = 32s).
  - **Live opportunities outside windows.** Windows are reference data. A buy that waits for a window (its route's buy part `h<hour>`) moves to now when the book sells its units for at most its exported gold (its expected price a unit is gold ÷ qty): it keeps its sale, its gold becomes the book's cost and its net rises by the difference. The row reads "buy now · sell Thu 19:00", and the tooltip's Best time to buy line is `PortfolioView.BuyTime`: "now · cheaper than Tue 20–23 (5s 36c)" (A76). Above the expected price the buy stays in its window.
  - **A newly learned recipe.** The profession window already captures recipes (§2.2), and `Professions.OnLearned` tells `Adapt` which ones a capture newly marks known. A recipe the graph doesn't have, whose product none of the character's candidates already crafts, is added to the in-memory graph from its captured definition (`Graph.AddRecipe`: its edges and a valid order again; an edge that would close a loop is cut as C13 cuts it). Its crafts are valued at posting now with the addon's own craft option within the live cap (`Options.CraftOptions`, Crafts above) and become one candidate (`Reselect.LearnedCandidate`, id `craft:<product>:<char>:<reagents>/-/now:1`), and reselection runs, outside combat. It has no history and no window. AHSync's next export includes the recipe in `G`, since the capture saved it (A83).
  - **Never overwrite newer state with an older snapshot** (built, M14). Events the addon recorded after the snapshot's `basedOnUntil` (a purchase that opened a position, a post, a sale) win over the snapshot until AHSync's next export includes them (FM18 for actions, FM38 for positions; A81). `Plan.LocalPositions` turns each confirmed purchase or craft after the character's `ingestedUntil` that matches one of the plan's open buy or craft actions into a **local position** (one per action, its id the first event's id, the id AHSync gives it; cost the purchase's qty × price, a craft's 0 until AHSync costs its reagents; next action "local · AHSync adds it at its next read"); one the export already carries is left out, so AHSync's next export replaces it. `Plan.LivePositions` is the exported positions, a post of an item after the plan making its position Listed and a sold auction mail Partially sold (PO8), then the local ones. The events stay in SavedVariables until AHSync ingests them (`DB.Prune`, A4), so a /reload with the same plan file keeps all of it (FM38). `Adapt.Snapshot` adds the local positions' gold to committed and takes it off the remaining capacity (the cap shrinks), and `Adapt.Unexecuted` takes the candidates a local position executed (a buy's flip, buy for later or disenchant of that item; a craft's craft of that product) out of the reselection (A81: the Linen row leaves).
  - **The addon never discovers the graph.** It is exported by the desktop. `Graph.Load` refuses a `G` whose `order` isn't topological (an item listed twice or missing, or a reagent after what it makes through a kept edge) with the status line "dependency graph invalid: using AHSync's numbers" (`Adapt.Problem`). Then nothing is repriced: the candidates keep their exported values and reselection still runs on them (DG6, FM39).
  - **Built (M13): the Portfolio tab shows the live reselection** and replaces Today and Week (§4.3). At export time the funded set and the plan's actions are the same rows, so the AH panel's Shopping and Post lists keep the plan's actions as their funded rows and add the reselection's exclusions as unfunded ones (A82). Without a portfolio snapshot the Portfolio tab shows the plan's actions due now and the addon's own options, as Today did.
  - **Performance.** `/ahp perf` prints the last scan's slowest step and the last repricing and reselection times with their work counts, against the 2 ms state-change budget (§5.1). Tests count the work (recipes and items visited, candidates recomputed, passes) instead of timing it (§5.2).
- **When state changes in game**, the addon does only O(1) work per changed item:
  1. a new price → re-read `P` from the curve row for the current hour (§3.1.1); posting-time advice comes from the precomputed top windows, never from per-slot profiles;
  2. recompute that item's options with the small ported formulas (fees, `E_post`, DE EV, craft cost);
  3. re-pick the best option;
  4. re-sort only the visible list.

  That's how it "recommends new paths" live: for example, an item gets bought out → its flip row drops; a cheaper mat appears → the craft's cost and rank update.
- The addon runs the **same small formulas in Lua** against live prices. They are ported from the `ahsync/engine/` modules listed under `Engine/` in §5, follow the numeric rules in §3.3, and must give the same numbers as Python on the shared scenarios in `tests/scenarios/`.
- Engine errors in game are caught with `pcall` at the UI boundary and shown as a status line, not as a Lua error popup.

When you're at the AH:

- Opening the AH runs a quick targeted search for every item on the current lists (the addon can also run a full scan). If Forever throttles searches or requires a hardware click for each one (§6), this becomes a "next search" button that steps through the list.
- Each result updates that item's price at once, re-ranks the lists, and drops targets that are gone or no longer profitable.
- Buying an item removes it, spends from the budget, and adds the item to inventory, which feeds the sell plan.
- Buying or posting marks the matching plan action done right away (§3.4.1). This in-game mark is provisional; AHSync's matching in `engine/tracking` is the record, so the matcher is not ported to Lua. An expiry or sale seen in the mailbox updates your sell record for the rest of the session.
- AHSync stays the second trigger. After a `/reload`, the addon picks up fresh baselines and everything your session recorded.

### 4.1 Capture

Everything in §2.2. Recipe definitions are keyed by profession, so they are stored once per account. Which character knows which recipe is stored per character. A future option is a static recipe database for every profession (from wago.tools), so the app can suggest recipes worth learning.

**Gathering loot** (**built M17**: `Capture/Gather.lua`, `Adapter.LootCast`, `Adapter.LootSourceKind`, `Adapter.ReadLoot`, the initial login in `Capture/Characters.lua`; §3.4E.3, §2.2.1 `gather`; A85–A91, FM42): `Capture/Gather.lua` records one `gather` event per loot window opened after a Mining, Herb Gathering, Skinning or Fishing cast, or on a corpse, with the source tag; nothing else. Every WoW call (the spell name of a cast, the loot slot and loot source reads) goes through `Core/Adapter.lua`, each marked `-- UNVERIFIED:` until checked on Forever (§6); the events (`UNIT_SPELLCAST_SUCCEEDED`, `UNIT_SPELLCAST_CHANNEL_START`, `LOOT_OPENED`, `LOOT_CLOSED`, `PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD` with its initial-login flag) come through `Core/Events.lua`. Budget (§5.1): one table write per loot window, no allocation outside it (the loot read runs protected through a named function, no closure), nothing in combat beyond that write (looting a corpse happens out of combat), no timer (a cast's window is a time comparison at `LOOT_OPENED`), and **no per-loot analytics**: sessions and rates are AHSync's. `/ahp perf` prints the last and the slowest window's handling time.

### 4.2 Scan

**Full scans are shared, not requested** (verified 2026-10-16). Forever has `C_AuctionHouse.ReplicateItems`, which returns the whole auction house (about 95k auctions on our realm). The AuctionHouseFYI addon already calls it when the AH opens, at most every 20 minutes, backing off when the server throttles it (3 to 17 minutes). The results arrive as the `REPLICATE_ITEM_LIST_UPDATE` event, which any addon can read.
- **Listen.** While the AH window is open, AHProfit listens for `REPLICATE_ITEM_LIST_UPDATE` and reads every auction (`GetNumReplicateItems`, `GetReplicateItemInfo`, `GetReplicateItemLink`, `GetReplicateItemTimeLeft`). It costs no request of its own, so it never competes with AuctionHouseFYI for the throttle. Reading is read-only.
- **Fall back.** When no scan result has arrived 30 s after the AH opened, and AHProfit hasn't called `ReplicateItems` in the last 20 minutes this session, it calls it itself (same back-off: a call that gets no answer doubles the wait, up to 2 h; any answer resets it to 20 min; A7, A10).
- **Store.** Auctions are grouped as `key, unit price, stack, band → count` (unit price = `floor(buyout ÷ stack)`) and saved as one `scans` entry (§2.2.1) with kind `full` and coverage 1. Closing the AH, a disconnect or a `/reload` before the end saves it as `partial` with the coverage reached (`floor(read × 1000 ÷ total) ÷ 1000`, FM19); partial scans never feed volume (§3.3.1). At most 12 un-ingested full scans are kept, enough for a scan every hour through a 12-hour session without a `/reload`; the oldest is dropped first, and the addon's status line says "run AHSync to sync" (FM22). Partial scans don't count toward the 12.
- **Left out, and counted in the diagnostics** (A11): battle pets, auctions without a buyout, values the client marks secret, and a time left that isn't 1–4 (volume needs it). The diagnostics also hold a histogram of every raw time left.
- **Item key:** from the link (§2.2.1), parsed once per distinct link per scan. An auction whose link isn't available yet (the item isn't cached) is keyed by its item id, or `−itemId` when it is a weapon or armor, or its class is unknown, since only those can roll a random suffix (A13).
- **Reading** follows AuctionHouseFYI's reader (0-based indexes, owner and bidder fields never read), except for time left: Forever returns 1 short … 4 very long, as Classic did, not retail's 0–3 (verified 2026-10-17: no 0 in 41,062 auctions; A12). It runs in steps of at most 4 ms and pauses in combat (A8). Each step aims to stop at 3 ms: it stops when the next row, at twice the step's average row cost, would pass that, which leaves 1 ms for what averages can't foresee, such as a garbage-collector step (measured in game: 4.5 ms when aiming at 4). Reading, sorting the item keys, encoding (each step joins its own rows) and saving each take their own frames, so no phase adds to another's step. An update arriving within 60 s of the last scan's start, or while one is still being read, starts no second scan.
- **AHSync** turns each stored scan into source `local` data: `current` (each key's lowest unit price and the units at it) and `candles_h1` (that price as the hour's close, with the units listed as its quantity). For each pair of consecutive complete local scans of a market at most 6 h apart, the order-book diff (§3.3.1) gives `books` volume, summed per day and written whole for that day, so re-reading a file changes nothing.
- A scan of a search or page (the Browse list) is not captured in v1.

The scan's results also update the addon's prices for this session, which matters for disenchant and flip targets (M5).

### 4.3 Show

The look is defined by the mockup [docs/mockups/addon.html](mockups/addon.html) and the wireframes below. Open the mockup in a browser and click the tabs, or use a direct link such as `addon.html#Craft`. The visual tests (§8.4) check the real addon against it.

The window's tabs, each shown in the mockup:

| Tab | Shows |
|---|---|
| Portfolio (built, M13: `UI/PortfolioView.lua` over the live reselection, §4.0, its lines gathered by `UI/WindowLines.lua` and drawn by `UI/MainWindow.lua`; A76–A80, A84, UI16, UI19) | **replaces Today and Week** (D76's addon half: the tab bar reads Portfolio, Disenchant, Craft, Flip, Inventory, Reservations); the character you're playing's share of AHSync's one shared plan, reselected live (§4.0), in three sections, as in the mockup (A79, UI16). A top line, the only place the addon shows cross-character totals, cached from AHSync's last snapshot: "portfolio 35g 58s · 30% risk · capacity 6g 49s 40c · 24s 40c unallocated · snapshot 10:38". **Positions** (`Plan.LivePositions`, local ones included since M14): Item · Qty · Status (the stage badge) · Net · Next, then "committed 70s · est. +10s 72c · +9% on committed capital · % leaves out Wool Cloth (found stock only)" (D.8); with local positions the foot says "committed 77s 60c (7s 60c local) · …" and the top line "portfolio 35g 58s · 30% risk · committed 4g 18s + 7s 60c local · capacity 6g 41s 80c · …" (A81). **Today**: action · Item · Qty · Each · Total · Net · Chance · Volume · Risk · Status, with chain lines under each row; funded rows first, then the unfunded ones greyed with their reason in Status (no red line in the addon), then "funded: cost 20s 60c · profit +12s 8c · 59% on cash". As on the old Today tab, a row your sell record changed has its record line under it, a **Needs a decision** section for "not selling" items follows the Today section (right-click: Blacklist or Keep), and the **Min chance** dropdown filters Today's rows for view only: a hidden funded row stays funded and the footer counts it (A84). **Next 7 days**: action · Item (×qty) · Plan ("buy Tue 20:00 · sell Thu 19:00") · Net · Adjusted ("→ +41s 75c" over "3.35 d · 31.75%") · Status, then its totals line. Footer: "funded +92s 62c · cost 2g 52s · 37% on cash" and "capacity 6g 49s 40c · 24s 40c unallocated". The list scrolls inside the window: the sections, their column headings and totals lines are lines of one pooled list, and a row is 30 px tall where its Status wraps (within 150 px) or, in Next 7 days, for the Adjusted sub-line. Excluded rows follow the funded ones in the dashboard's order (left out only for gold, capacity or a list limit first, then D.6's order, then adjusted profit). Hovering an excluded row shows its tooltip: "Not funded today (Brugan)", the row ("Lesser Mana Potion · CRAFT ×10"), its reason ("Exceeds risk tolerance: needs 45s, 24s 40c left") and detail line, Expected net, then the snapshot's Risk limit ("10g 67s 40c · committed 4g 18s") and Funded before it ("6g 25s of 6g 49s 40c"); right-clicking one lists every excluded row of its section that way (UI19). Empty state (D.7, `PortfolioView.EmptyBlock`): "Nothing funded: risk budget exhausted" with "10g 88s committed of a 10g 67s 40c limit. New rows come back as positions sell.", positions still shown, the top line led by the character's live gold ("Amaya 14g 30s · portfolio 35g 58s · 30% risk · capacity 0c · snapshot 10:38") and the footer "funded 0c" · "3 positions" (UI19). Without a portfolio snapshot (no `PortfolioPlan.lua`, or none for this market) the tab shows the plan's actions due now and the addon's own options, as the old Today tab did (`UI/PlanView.lua`; A15, A52, A71, A73, A74), so it is never blank |
| Disenchant | **always shown**, even with no enchanter on the account: an alt may be the one (A23). Item, ilvl, the Enchanting skill it needs, buy (the live scan's cheapest, else the file's), EV, profit, Chance · Volume (of the main mat), and a note with the expected mats; "no enchanter yet" when nobody on the account has Enchanting, "needs Enchanting 225 (best 150)" (greyed) when your best is too low. Only targets that pay. Live search state (✓ / ✗ / …) comes with the AH panel |
| Craft | Dashboard: product, who knows it, **source** (Owned / AH / Vendor / Mixed, plus "+ crafted" for chains; filterable), crafts, mat gold each and total ("—" when you own every mat), profit, Chance · Volume, risk, cooldown; a note lists which mats are owned and which are bought. The addon's tab is the character you're playing: product, Source, ×, Mats (gold for the mats to buy, "—" when you own them all), profit, Chance · Volume, risk, and a note of the mats used and bought. Each known recipe is costed on its own against what you carry less what you keep; only recipes that pay are listed (A27, UI11). Source is Owned, AH, Vendor or Mixed (C17, C18), and the note has one grey line per mat (A34): "use 4 owned Simple Wood", "buy 3 Coarse Thread from a vendor · 30c", "buy 2 Copper Bar on the AH · 20s" (A30, A34) |
| Flip | Dashboard: item, quantity, buy each and total, expected sell price, sell slot, profit, Chance · Volume, risk %. The addon's tab lists the flips AHSync's plan has for the character you're playing (a buy whose chain ends in a post): item, Qty, Buy (at or below), Sell, When (the post's peak), Profit (the chain's), Chance (its first post's), Risk, and a note with the post time and the live cheapest price. A live scan drops a flip that is gone or now costs more than its buy price, and the footer counts it (A28, UI13) |
| Inventory | what the character you're playing holds: item, where (bags, bank, mail), quantity, worth, Chance · Volume, and its plan as a note under the row (AHSync's, marked "(AHSync)", or the addon's own: "Post now at 45c", "For crafting Copper Rod", "Bound · not planned"), best worth first (A22). Paid joins it with tracking (M6b), and listed auctions with On the AH |
| Inventory (your auctions) | the character's active auctions as rows with Where "AH": units, worth if sold (price × units × 0.95), note "listed at 80c each · ends in 11 h", from the last own-auctions snapshot; one past its end is left out (A50) |
| Reservations | what the character keeps for itself (§3.4.3): item, Keeping (All or a number), Held, Free; click a row to change or clear it (A26) |

Right-clicking a row opens a menu: Search the AH, Mark as skipped, Blacklist: don't buy or craft, Blacklist: ignore completely, and Keep (start fresh) on a "not selling" item.

**Main window** (`/ahp`), default 640 × 440 px, minimum 560 × 360:

```
┌ AHProfit ── Grommash · Horde PvP ───────────────────────── data 2 h old ── [x] ┐
│ [Portfolio] [Disenchant] [Craft] [Flip] [Inventory] [Reservations] [50%▾]    │
│ portfolio 35g 58s · 30% risk · capacity 6g 49s 40c · 24s 40c unall. · 10:38   │
│───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────│
│ Positions                                              this character · 3     │
│ Item              Qty        Status           Net     Next                    │
│ Mageweave Cloth   ×10 of 25  Partially sold   +6s     post now                │
│ committed 70s · est. +10s 72c · +9% on committed capital · % leaves out Wool  │
│ Today                                  realized today · funded, then unfunded │
│     Item       Qty Each Total Net      Chance Volume Risk Status              │
│ DE  Scouting…  ×1  8s   8s    +6s 25c  90% ●   —     0.3% Funded              │
│     disenchant now · sell today 18:00                                         │
│ DE  Grunt's…   ×1  15s  15s   +6s      90% ●   —     0.5% Required profession │
│                                                           unavailable: …      │
│ funded: cost 20s 60c · profit +12s 8c · 59% on cash · 1 hidden by Min chance  │
│ Needs a decision                                                              │
│ Next 7 days                                net → adjusted · wait · premium    │
│ BUY Thick Leather ×30  buy now · sell Thu 19:00  +55s  → +41s 75c  Funded    │
│                                                        3.35 d · 31.75%        │
│ ...                                                                    ▲▼     │
│───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────│
│ funded +92s 62c · cost 2g 52s · 37% on cash   capacity 6g 49s 40c · 24s 40c … │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

The dot after each chance is its confidence. Hovering "85% ◐" shows "Confidence 63%".

- **The window is about the character you are playing.** It shows only that character's actions, so it has no Who column; the dashboard shows every character, with one.
- **The plan's Today rows are at the plan's level** (`UI/PlanView.lua`, the Lua twin of `plan_chains` and the Today part of `plan_views`; A71, A73, A74; the Portfolio tab without a snapshot and the AH panel's Post list show them): one row per chain, its same-character buys and intermediate crafts as note lines under it, then its incoming mail ("6 Copper Bar from Brugan · not mailed yet"; the plan file carries only open mail, so a sent one simply goes), then "then: post Sun 11:00, for the 19:00 peak"; a craft row's Each and Total are its chain's mat gold per craft and in all, "—" when every mat is owned (§3.4 money table). Holds and vendor sales, AHSync's or the addon's own, are no rows (they are on Inventory). The sending character sees the MAIL row ("to Amaya"); the crafter sees the line. Clicking a MAIL row does nothing special in v1: the mailbox is not filled in for you.
- Each row shows: action, item (in its quality colour), quantity, **each** and **total** (§3.4), expected net (green when positive, red when negative), then the standard pair **Chance · Volume**, then **risk %** (measured against all your characters' gold).
- **The pair is identical on every addon tab.** (The dashboard shows the fuller market activity columns, §3.5.)
  - **Chance** is `P'` (§3.3.2) with the **confidence dot** inside the cell (● ≥ 75%, ◐ ≥ 45%, ○ below; §3.3.5). Hover shows the exact confidence %.
  - **Volume** is units sold per day. The dashboard also shows the source and the units on the AH.
  - Rows with nothing to sell (mat buys, mail) show "—" in both.
- **Every column header shows a tooltip on hover** (`GameTooltip`) saying what the column means, with the same texts as the dashboard where the column is the same (§3.5). The texts live in `UI/ViewModel.lua`.
- Your sell record (§3.3.3) appears as a second line under the row, but only when it changed the recommendation.
- Long item names are cut off with "…". The list scrolls, and the window never grows past its size.
- Money uses gold, silver and copper coin icons.
- The window is movable and resizable, remembers its position, and closes with Escape.
- **Empty states:**
  - With no `MarketIntelligence.lua` yet: "Run AHSync, then /reload".
  - With nothing to do: "Nothing to do in the next hour." (the Portfolio tab without a snapshot); with one and nothing funded, the D.7 block (UI19).
  - Data more than 24 h old: the age badge turns amber.
- Disenchant and Craft numbers are re-checked live against the fresh in-game scan.

**AH side panel**, docked to the right of the auction house window, 300 px wide:

```
┌ AHProfit at the AH ──────────────┐
│ Shopping                 3 of 5  │
│ ✓ Linen Cloth   38c ≤ 45c   ×20  │
│ ✓ Scouting Gl.  8s ≤ 9s     ×1   │
│ ✗ Copper Bar    gone             │
│ … Iron Ore      checking         │
│ Post                             │
│ ○ Copper Rod    41s  ×5          │
│ [Next search]        [Full scan] │
│ 2 found · budget left 31g 40s    │
└──────────────────────────────────┘
```

Clicking a row searches for that item.

**Built (M13): funded first, then unfunded** (A82, UI18; `ViewModel.AHPanel`, `UI/AHPanel.lua`). Both lists show the funded rows first, then the rows the portfolio left out, muted with an "unfunded" tag and the reason on hover ("Unfunded: Insufficient liquidity: 3/day, 40 planned.
