Click to search anyway"; Post: "… Click to fill the Sell tab anyway"). They stay clickable, for when you choose to go past your risk tolerance. The section headers count them: "3 of 5 + 1 unfunded", "1 + 1 unfunded". The unfunded rows are the live reselection's exclusions (§4.0; none without a portfolio snapshot): Shopping gets each excluded flip, buy for later or disenchant whose item isn't already a funded row, its units at most gold ÷ units each; Post gets each excluded row whose item is in the bags, at most its units, at the item's post price with its net scaled to them. The tag sits in the Item column (8 px, 36 px wide), so the panel stays 300 px.

In the addon (`UI/AHPanel.lua`, rows from `ViewModel.AHPanel`; UI7, A24, FM24): it opens docked to the AH window's right edge on `AUCTION_HOUSE_SHOW` and hides on close. **Shopping** is the character's planned buys, then disenchant targets your best enchanter can do (bought at most at their EV), up to 10. A row is ✓ when the live data (the last full scan, or an AH search's result if newer) has its quantity at or below its price ("38c ≤ 45c"), ✗ "gone" when not, and … "checking" until one has been seen; the states are WoW's ready-check icons, since its fonts have no ✓ ✗ glyphs. "3 of 5" counts the rows checked. Each section has labelled columns whose headers explain themselves on hover: Shopping **Item · For · Price · Max · Qty**, For being why it is bought: CRAFT (a mat for a planned craft), FLIP (to resell later) or DE (to disenchant), from what follows the buy in its plan chain (A40) (Price is the cheapest listing seen now, or "gone" / "checking"; Max is the most worth paying each), Post **Item · List price · Qty · Est. profit · Chance** (Est. profit is the post's own expected net for all its units, even when a flip counts its profit on the buy). **Post** lists the character's Today posts (AHSync's posts due now and the addon's own "post now" options), with Today's Min chance filter, with price and chance. Shopping has one row per item, totalling every planned buy of it at the lowest of their price caps (A65). Shopping lists only what to buy on the AH: a vendor buy stays on Today, marked "from a vendor" (A32). AHSync's posts, vendor sales and disenchants of stock (no parent) are never shown for more than the character holds in bags, bank and mail above what it keeps, so a posted item leaves Today as soon as the bags are read again; a chain's later step stays (A49). Only items in your bags are listed in Post: a post for an item you don't hold yet is a later step of a chain (a craft or a flip) and waits until you have it (A30). Each row is capped at the units you hold (shared in order across rows of the same item), with its Est. profit scaled to match, and the panel and the window redraw whenever the bags have been read again, so a posted item drops off or shrinks at once (A31). **Clicking a Post row fills the AH's Sell tab** (A29): the item from your bags (the first unbound stack), the quantity (at most what you can post) and the unit price; you check it and press the AH's own Post button, so a wrong suggestion never lists by itself. When the AH fills in its own price from a search right after (within 10 s), the suggested price is put back once. Without the Sell tab's API the status line says so (FM30). Right-click keeps the item for yourself (§3.4.3). The auction duration is set too (A56): the plan's post carries it (EX9), an own "post now" row uses the shortest duration covering the posting-now window; without the AH's duration control the status line says which duration to pick. UNVERIFIED on Forever: `AuctionHouseFrame:SetPostItem`, `ItemLocation:CreateFromBagAndSlot`, the sell frames' price and quantity inputs, `C_AuctionHouse.GetItemCommodityStatus`, `GetAvailablePostCount`, `IsSellItemValid` and the search-result events; the diagnostics probe them. An empty Shopping list says why. A click searches through the AH window's search bar (a hardware event); the status line says what it searched and how far this visit has got ("searched Linen Cloth · 2 of 5"); a search whose results don't list the item marks it ✗ gone, and a result is matched to a random-suffix item by its item id (A38). A buyout or commodity purchase seen this session (hooks that only observe `C_AuctionHouse.ConfirmCommoditiesPurchase` and buyout-sized `PlaceBid`; a commodity purchase counts once `COMMODITY_PURCHASE_SUCCEEDED` fires and is dropped on `COMMODITY_PURCHASE_FAILED`) lowers that row's quantity, and a row whose quantity is bought drops off; a bid changes nothing (A39). The purchases are saved as events, so this holds after a /reload too, until AHSync's next plan includes them (FM18); **Full scan** asks for a ReplicateItems scan within the same 20-minute gap as the fallback and says how long to wait otherwise. A progress bar under the status line follows every scan, whoever asked for it: waiting for the server, reading ("reading 23,400 of 47,667", up to 85% of the bar), saving, then "full scan done · 47,667 auctions"; it updates every 10 steps, never per frame. An own request with no answer after 60 s says "no answer: another addon may have scanned recently" (A43). Search results (`AUCTION_HOUSE_BROWSE_RESULTS_UPDATED`, `C_AuctionHouse.GetBrowseResults`) update that item's live price for the session. Closing the AH drops the search queue (FM24). UNVERIFIED on Forever: the search bar's `SetSearchText`/`StartSearch`, the browse event and results; the diagnostics probe them.

**Tooltip** (built, M13: `UI/TooltipView.lua` makes the lines, `UI/Tooltip.lua` gathers the facts and draws them; TT8–TT21, UI17; it replaced the six-line block). An **AHProfit** block under the item's own lines, drawn with two-column lines (`AddDoubleLine`, label left, value right), at most 320 px wide, every price per unit. Section 3 of the mockup shows all 14 variants. A bag item with a planned post:

```
AHProfit                                 data 2 h old ●
Do: POST 5 now at 41s          +63s 6c · 58% chance
                                          then nothing
Market               42s (2 h) · 45s 30-day (+7%)
Best time to sell     Fri 18–21 · 45s (+7%) · pattern
Best time to buy      Mon 03–07 · 38s (−10%) · early
Activity            6/day · 14 listed · 2.3 d supply
```

| Line | Shows | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Header | "AHProfit", then the age of `MarketIntelligence.lua` with a dot; the age and dot turn amber past 24 h | §3.5 |
| Do | what to do with this item, always the first line after the header: the plan's action for the character you're on (`PortfolioPlan.lua`: its funded row, or its unfunded alternative), else the addon's own best option (§4.0). The verb has its badge colour: POST gold, BUY blue, CRAFT orange, DISENCHANT purple, VENDOR and HOLD grey, MAIL light blue, KEPT and UNFUNDED muted. The value is what the action makes, then its chance; the chance belongs to the action (for a craft, its product's). A second, value-only line carries what doesn't fit: the next step ("then post Sat 10:00", "then nothing") or a reason ("needs 45s, 24s 40c left"). An AH listing on your Shopping list reads "BUY ≤ 10s · for Copper Rod" with "funded · 6 needed"; an AH listing on none of your lists has no Do line | §3.4D, §4.0 |
| Market | the lowest price now with its age ("now" while you browse the AH, else how old the scan is), then the 30-day median and its delta; "none listed · 10c 30-day" when nothing is listed | §3.3 |
| Disenchant | what disenchanting one is worth, and its delta. Only shown when one of your characters can disenchant the item | §3.4A |
| Best time to sell | the top selling window ("Sat 17–21"), its expected price (the plan's `slot_price`), its delta and its strength: *early* (under 3 weeks of data), *pattern* (3–5) or *established* (6 or more). When now falls inside it, it reads "now (Fri 18–21) · 45s · pattern", and the Do line adds "best time to sell is now" | §3.3.4, §3.4C |
| Best time to buy | the top buying window (ranked by actionable value, §3.3.6), with its expected price, delta and strength. When the price now is below every buy window's expected price, it reads "now · cheaper than Tue 20–23 (7s 10c)", shortened to "now · under Tue 20–23 (7s 10c)" when it would pass 320 px; on a Shopping-list AH row the Do line then adds "best time to buy is now" | §3.3.6 |
| Activity | in muted text: sales per day, units listed now and days of supply ("6/day · 14 listed · 2.3 d supply"; "0/day · 0 listed" when nothing is listed) | §3.3.6 |

- **A best-time line is never dropped to make room.** Hour ranges are written "Sat 17–21", and a long Do value continues on a second line with an empty label (variants 1, 2, 4, 8, 9 and 14). An item with no windows at all (W7) has neither best-time line (variant 10).
- **No market data:** two lines only, the header and "no market data yet" (variant 11). This replaces A21's "adds nothing".
- **Delta** works as built (TT1–TT5): `(value − cur) ÷ cur × 100`, rounded half up to a whole percent and shown with a sign, green above the price now and red below, left out when there is no current price. A positive delta means the item is worth more than it costs right now.
- `UI/TooltipView.lua` builds the lines (§8.5); its deltas keep `views.delta_percent`'s rule (shared scenarios TT1–TT5), and the two-column text is the addon's own (the dashboard has the item page). `UI/Tooltip.lua` gathers the facts and only draws them.

**Built (M13)** (A21, TT4, TT6–TT23, R21, UI17). `UI/Tooltip.lua` reads the market file, and a newer in-game scan's book replaces `cur` and the units listed ("40c now" while the scan is fresh or the AH is open; else its age, "(2 h)"), with the deltas following it. The Do line is the first that applies (`TooltipView.DoOf`): kept for yourself (a reservation and units held, TT16); on an AH listing (the AH open and none in the bags) the Shopping list's buy of it (its units, the lowest cap and what follows it in its chain, TT14), else an unfunded buy, else no Do line (TT20); this character's first open plan action on it (a post now or later, a hold, a vendor sale, a disenchant, a mail with what the recipient crafts, or a craft whose recipe uses it, from the exported graph or the captured recipes, with the chain's mat gold, first post's chance and post time; TT8–TT13); the live reselection's unfunded row for it, its reason split into words and numbers (TT15); the addon's own option for what it holds (`Options.SellOne`, TT17). A best sell window that holds now (TT21) and a price below every buy window's expected price (TT14, TT16) add their notes. Window prices are the plan's `slot_price`: `level × how × (1 + trend7 ÷ 100 × days until the window's best hour)` (`med7`, else `med30`; trend clamped to ±5%/day and the factor to `1 ± max_trend_total`; kept within the last 30 days' range; TT6, TT7, TT22, TT23), ported as `Engine/Prices.SlotPrice`, for the sell and the buy window alike. The addon has no config channel, so `Prices.MAX_TREND_TOTAL` holds the default 0.15, as `min_flip_margin` is held in `UI/FlipView.lua`; change both sides together. The Disenchant line uses the ported disenchant table with the item's quality, level and class from the client and your best Enchanting skill. Widths are estimated as the layout snapshots do (characters × the font size × 0.55, a coin counting two), at the small tooltip font (10 px): `Adapter.AddTooltipLines` sets `GameTooltipTextSmall` on each added line (UNVERIFIED on Forever: `GameTooltip:NumLines`, `GameTooltipTextLeft<n>`/`TextRight<n>` and `GameTooltipTextSmall`; the diagnostics probe them). The header's dot is an inline texture, since WoW's fonts have no ● glyph. A failure never breaks the tooltip. The hook is `TooltipDataProcessor` where the client has it, else `GameTooltip`'s `OnTooltipSetItem` (UNVERIFIED on Forever).

**Structure.** `UI/ViewModel.lua` turns the addon files, the addon's own recommendations and the live state into plain tables of rows, labels, colours and formatted money. The frame code (`Window`, `AHPanel`, `Tooltip`) only draws those tables. So most UI behaviour is testable as plain Lua, without frames.

`/ahp demo` loads a fixture plan into the real window, so the UI can be checked in game before any real data exists.

Other commands:
- `/ahp perf` shows the addon's memory and timing against its budgets (§5.1).
- `/ahp refresh` asks AHSync for a full refresh at the next `/reload` or logout.

### 4.4 Act

Clicking a row runs an AH search for it, and the shopping and post lists show alongside. You still click to buy or post.

### 4.5 Static data

`Data/Disenchant.lua` holds quality × ilvl band × armor/weapon → material probabilities, plus the Enchanting skill per ilvl. It is seeded from Classic Era values and must be verified for Forever.

## 5. Architecture and tech

### 5.0 Components and boundaries

The system is built as separate components that talk only through versioned contracts, so that **collection and historical analysis can move to the cloud** (for example AWS Lambda, if a Blizzard API ever replaces thin community data) without changes to the addon or the recommendation side.

```text
 Providers (AuctionHouseFYI, AHledger, own in-game scans, future Blizzard API)
        │
        ▼
 COLLECTOR ──► HISTORICAL STORE ──► HISTORICAL ANALYSIS ──► MARKET INTELLIGENCE (versioned)
 collect/        history/             analysis/                intel/
                                                                   │
                                   ┌───────────────────────────────┤
                                   ▼                               ▼
                       EXPORT (MarketIntelligence.lua)   DESKTOP RECOMMENDATIONS ◄── STATE IMPORTER ◄── SavedVariables
                       export/                           recommend/                  state/                (addon)
                                   │                               │
                                   ▼                               ▼
                       ADDON (state, own recommendations,  PORTFOLIO OPTIMIZER ──► DASHBOARD (web/)
                       transaction journal)                 recommend/portfolio.py └► PortfolioPlan.lua (optional advice)
```

| Component | Folder | Owns | Does not own |
|---|---|---|---|
| Collector | `collect/` | acquiring, validating and normalizing observations from interchangeable provider adapters (AHF, AHledger (built M19), §2.3); catch-up checkpoints and the AHledger backfill's; own scans (§4.2); the kind of each source's rows (`collect/provider.py` `SOURCE_KINDS`, §2.2); the hours each source observed completely (`observed_hours`) | statistics, recommendations |
| Historical Store | `history/` | raw observations (candles, current, books, volume rows), retention | business logic |
| Historical Analysis | `analysis/` | market-wide statistics, weekly patterns, seasonality strength, volume estimates, supply, buy/sell windows (§3.3) | anything character-specific |
| Market Intelligence | `intel/` | the versioned analysis results and their store | live character state |
| Export | `export/` | `MarketIntelligence.lua` (the intelligence, trimmed to the shortlist) and `PortfolioPlan.lua` (§3.5) | analysis, recommendations |
| State Importer | `state/` | reading SavedVariables, idempotent import, character snapshots and the transaction journal, freshness per character | recommendations |
| Desktop Recommendations | `recommend/` | per-character Disenchant, Craft, Flip, Sell options from intelligence + state | collection, history |
| Portfolio Optimizer | `recommend/portfolio.py` and `recommend/plan.py` (inputs and rows); built (M9): `engine/portfolio.py`'s contracts (the candidate record, the accounting, the export subset, profit %); built (M10): `engine/candidates.py` and `engine/craft_candidates.py` (stage A), `engine/selection.py` (stage B, validation, reasons), `engine/timing.py` (the premium), `engine/allocation.py` (the pipeline), over `engine/plan.py`, `engine/flips.py`, `engine/craft_plan.py`, `engine/week.py`; built (M11): `engine/graph.py` (the dependency graph), `export/portfolio_lua.py` (its strings); built (M14): `engine/positions.py`, `engine/position_views.py`, `recommend/positions_pass.py` | the cross-character plan: capital, shared market capacity, transfers (§3.4C); the funded portfolio and its alternatives (§3.4D); positions (§3.4D.9) | raw collection |
| Gathering (built M16: `engine/gathering.py`, `engine/gathering_view.py`, `recommend/gathering_page.py`, the data file; built M17: `engine/sessions.py`, `state/gathering.py`, `Capture/Gather.lua`; built M18: `engine/gathering_history.py`) | `engine/gathering.py` (catalog, eligibility, rates, observed categories, unplaced loot), `engine/sessions.py`, `engine/gathering_view.py`, `engine/gathering_history.py` (the historical columns and the detail), `state/gathering.py`, `recommend/gathering_page.py`, the data file `data/gathering_catalog.json`; the addon's `Capture/Gather.lua` | the farmable catalog, eligibility, loot events, farming sessions, rates and manual overrides, the Gathering page (§3.4E) | funding, candidates, positions: farmed items are found stock the optimizer sells like any other |
| Dashboard | `web/` | showing results; writes only through its secured endpoints (§3.5) | any calculation |
| Engine | `engine/` | the pure formulas every component above uses (§3.3, §3.4) | I/O of any kind |
| Lifecycle | `app/` | the scheduler and the order of a refresh: analysis → recommendations → publish | formulas |
| Storage | `db/` | the local SQLite implementation of every store (one file, `data/ahsync.db`) | business logic |

**Contracts.** Every arrow above is a typed, versioned contract with a schema version, owner, timestamp meaning (observation time, never import time), unique ids, an explicit "unavailable" value, validation rules and failure behaviour.

| Producer → consumer | Contract |
|---|---|
| Collector → Historical Store | normalized observations with source and observation time: `collect/provider.py` `SnapshotObservation` (snapshots with auction ids, checked whole by `snapshot_problems`), stored by `collect/observations.ingest_snapshot`. AHF's adapter predates the contract and writes through `collect/catchup.py`; your scans through `collect/local_scans.py`; AHledger's (**built**, M19) through `collect/ahledger.py`: `current`, `candles_h1`/`candles_d1` closes under `ahledger` or `ahledger_h1`, its side table, `source_state` and `observed_hours` |
| Historical Store → Analysis | `history.reads.HistoryReader` (a protocol: closes, current, books, volume, items), read **in bulk per market** (below); one close per (item, hour) by kind, then rank (§2.2), so analysis never names a source |
| Analysis → Intelligence | `intel.contract.MarketIntelligence` (`INTEL_SCHEMA_VERSION`, method versions, `built_ms`, `observations_until_ms`, one `ItemIntel` per item; contents below), JSON-serializable |
| Intelligence → Export / Recommendations | `intel.store.IntelReader` |
| State → Export | the shortlist: item keys on any desktop list, in stock or recipes, or recently scanned (`state.reads.StateReader`) |
| Addon → State Importer | SavedVariables (§2.2.1) |
| State Importer → Recommendations, Portfolio | `state.reads.StateReader` (characters with freshness, skills, recipes, stock, journal) |
| Recommendations → Portfolio → Dashboard | option and plan records; dashboard pages as in §3.5 |
| Export → Addon | `MarketIntelligence.lua`, `PortfolioPlan.lua` (§3.5) |
| Portfolio Optimizer → Export (built M11) | the **candidate universe** (`engine.portfolio.Candidate`, the funded ones and the best alternatives, `candidate_export` in all, §3.4D.3) and the **dependency graph** (`engine.graph.Graph`: recipes your characters know plus disenchant transforms, reverse index, topological order) into `MarketIntelligence.lua`; the **portfolio snapshot** (gold per character, committed capital, capital base, capacity, reservations per character, scoring settings and their config version, the snapshot time) and the **positions** into `PortfolioPlan.lua` (§3.5). Together they stay inside the files' 2 MB and 200 KB budgets (§3.1.2, FM40). Stored between the plan run and the export as `export_candidates` and `portfolio_snapshot` (§3.2), swapped in with the plan; the graph is built when the market file is written, from the known recipes |
| State Importer → Portfolio (built, M14) | positions and their lots (`state/positions.py`, mixed into `Database`), built by the tracking pass from matched purchase and craft events and replayed from the journal (§3.4D.9) |

**Architectural invariants** (checked by AR1–AR4 and the convention test):
1. Collection is independent of analysis; analysis is independent of character state: `analysis/` imports only `engine/`, `history.reads` and `intel.contract`.
2. Analysis runs on any `HistoryReader`, not only SQLite (AR1), and its output depends on nothing else.
3. Export and recommendations never compute history statistics; they read intelligence.
4. The addon builds its own recommendations from intelligence plus live state, and works without the desktop running; the desktop's plan is optional advice.
5. Listing changes never become confirmed sales; seasonality strength, expected profit and sales volume stay separate numbers.
6. Every component is testable on its own with fakes.
7. **The intelligence is complete.** Every number derived from history that any consumer needs is in `MarketIntelligence`, so a consumer never needs a `HistoryReader` (AR5).

**Contents of the intelligence.** Per item (`ItemIntel`), everything market-wide that §3.3 computes from history:
- prices and their ages: `cur`, med7/med30, p10/p90, trend, the latest book;
- volume and supply (§3.3.1, §3.3.6): sales, expiries and listings per day with their sources, current supply, supply/volume, the confidence components;
- weekly patterns (§3.3.4, §3.3.6): `how`, `sales_how` and `supply_how` (168 slots each) with `weeks` per slot, and seasonality strength. **Each profile is stored packed**: 168 comma-separated integers in thousandths, `floor(ratio × 1000 + 0.5)` (§3.3 rounding), and its weeks as 168 comma-separated counts; an empty string means no data (flat 1.0, 0 weeks). Analysis computes the windows and the curve from the packed values, so anyone decoding them gets the same windows (as IU11 does for the curve). Packing keeps a 9k-item market's intelligence within the memory budget (§3.1.2);
- the top 3 selling windows and top 3 buying windows, each with its start slot, length, best slot, average `how` and weeks of data;
- the sell-probability curve: the full 24 × 8 grid, already quantized (§3.1.1), with the hour its first row starts at.

Analysis computes these the same way for every item. It never chooses by your lists, stock or recipes; that trimming belongs to the export.

**Bulk reads.** Analysis reads history **per market, not per item**: `HistoryReader` offers one read that streams every item's closes, books and volume rows for a market in key order. Against local SQLite this is one query per table, each walking the table's primary key `(market, key, …)` so its rows arrive in key order without a sort (a whole-market sort of the hourly tables costs about 10 s per 2M rows), merged by key with one item in memory at a time. Against a remote store it keeps a market to a few round trips instead of several per item (about 40k for 9k items) (AR7).

**Cloud boundary.** The first step is smaller than moving analysis: when a provider with hourly snapshots appears (a Blizzard API, retail-shaped: every auction with an id, quantity, price and time-left band, commodities possibly region-wide), a tiny hourly collector mirrors each raw snapshot to object storage with an index file, a copy of the provider shaped like the snapshot store (`collect/snapshots.py`). AHSync keeps its catch-up model against that mirror, analysis stays local and testable with the same fakes, and no versioned intelligence crosses the network. Before the mirror exists, AHSync can poll the provider itself while it runs and accept the gaps, as it does for AHF public hours. **Built** (2026-10-19, the seams only, no provider yet): the snapshot contract and its checks (`collect/provider.py`: `SnapshotObservation`, `SnapshotProvider`, FM33), the store (V36, FM32), the ingest (`collect/observations.py`: raw file, grouped book, prices, hourly sales, group diffs and id diffs, V36), item keys from bonus lists (`engine/item_key.py`, SRC5), and per-market addresses, primary source and ranking (`[sources]`, SRC1-SRC4). SPEC GAP: the scheduler, pruning and the method-version recompute don't reach the snapshot store yet; they need a provider to run.

Later, the Collector, the Historical Store and the Analysis can run elsewhere, for example as a scheduled AWS Lambda, and publish `MarketIntelligence` as versioned JSON. The desktop then:
- fetches the newest intelligence and checks its version (AR2) and age; when it is missing or too new, it keeps the last good one and shows its age (§5.2);
- runs recommendations, the portfolio and the dashboard from it, with no history store;
- builds `MarketIntelligence.lua` itself, because the trimming needs the shortlist (character state) and the file must land in the local AddOns folder.

Reading SavedVariables and writing the addon files stay on the desktop. Own in-game scans would have to be uploaded to feed cloud analysis.

### 5.0.1 Tech

- **AHSync**: Python 3.12 (installed), standard library only (`urllib`, `gzip`, `json`, `sqlite3`, `tomllib`, `http.server`). The dashboard is one static HTML/JS page served with JSON endpoints. Run from source with `python -m ahsync`; **planned (M22–M27, §3.7)**: a standalone app built with PyInstaller (Windows installer, signed macOS dmg). **Packaging-only exception** (the user's decision, 2026-11-01): the app shell `ahsync_app/` may import `pystray`, `Pillow` and, on macOS, `pyobjc-core` and `pyobjc-framework-Cocoa`, listed in `requirements-app.txt`; `ahsync/` stays standard-library only, and the convention test keeps OS branches inside `ahsync_app/platform/` (DR16).
- **AHProfit**: Lua 5.1 and WoW FrameXML, with no libraries in v1.

Layout. **`tests/` mirrors the source tree one-to-one**: every source module has a test file at the same relative path.

### 5.1 Addon performance (WoW best practices)

The addon must never be noticeable in game: **no work while idle, a few milliseconds at most when something happens, nothing in combat.**

| Rule | How |
|---|---|
| **No `OnUpdate` polling** | Everything is event-driven. Delays use one-shot `C_Timer.After`, never a per-frame handler. |
| **Coalesce bursty events** | `BAG_UPDATE` and similar fire many times at once. Mark the state dirty, then handle it once on `BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED` or after a 0.5 s debounce timer. |
| **Register events only when needed** | AH events are registered on `AUCTION_HOUSE_SHOW` and unregistered on close; mail events likewise for the mailbox. |
| **Pause in combat** | On `PLAYER_REGEN_DISABLED`, queue all non-essential work (scans, re-ranks, UI refreshes) and run it on `PLAYER_REGEN_ENABLED`. Never touch frames in combat. |
| **Lazy UI** | Frames are created on the first `/ahp` or AH open, not at login. A hidden window does no updates. |
| **Pooled, virtual lists** | Only the visible rows exist (about 15 frames per list), reused while scrolling. Rows are never created per item. |
| **Lazy data decode** | `MarketIntelligence.lua` keeps items as compact strings keyed by item id. A row is decoded only when needed (tooltip, list) and kept in a small cache of about 500 entries. Login never builds 9k tables. |
| **Cheap tooltip** | One cache lookup plus a few `AddDoubleLine` calls. No string building beyond formatting the numbers. |
| **Chunked full scans** | A full scan's results (`ReplicateItems`, usually requested by AuctionHouseFYI, §4.2) are read in a coroutine with a **4 ms budget per frame**, stepped by one-shot timers, so it never stalls the game. Reading pauses in combat and resumes after it. AHProfit requests a scan itself only as the §4.2 fallback. |
| **No per-frame allocations** | Reuse tables, cache globals in locals, and avoid string concatenation in loops (already in CLAUDE.md). |
| **Small SavedVariables** | Order books are stored as compact strings. At most 12 un-ingested full scans are kept, about 0.4 MB each (the oldest is dropped first, with a status warning), and the pruning handshake (§2.2) removes ingested data. Target: ≤ 5 MB on disk. |
| **Respect AH throttles** | Searches go through one queue that obeys `CanSendAuctionQuery` or its Forever equivalent (§6). |

**Budgets**, measured in game with `/ahp perf`: memory from `GetAddOnMemoryUsage` and time from `debugprofilestop`.

| What | Budget |
|---|---|
| CPU while idle (no AH, no window) | 0 ms per frame (no handlers running) |
| Memory after login | ≤ 3 MB, plus the intelligence strings (≤ 2 MB) |
| Memory with the window and AH open | ≤ 10 MB |
| One state change (price, buy, post) | ≤ 2 ms total |
| Tooltip | ≤ 0.2 ms |
| Full scan processing | ≤ 4 ms per frame |
| Login / `/reload` added time | ≤ 100 ms |

### 5.2 Failure modes and recovery

Data moves through many hand-offs: AHF and AHledger → AHSync → SQLite → `MarketIntelligence.lua` / `PortfolioPlan.lua` → WoW → SavedVariables → AHSync. Any of them can fail halfway. Six rules make every failure recoverable:

1. **Commit points, then resume.** Every step commits in small units (one hour file, one SavedVariables ingest, one refresh). A crash resumes from the last commit point; nothing restarts from zero.
2. **Idempotent writes.** Every write is an upsert on a natural key, so repeating a step is harmless. Every captured event has a unique id, `charId:timeMs:seq`, so reading the same SavedVariables twice never double-counts.
3. **Never publish half a result.**
   - A full refresh writes into staging tables (`item_stats_new`, `plan_new`, …) and swaps them in with one transaction.
   - The addon files are each written to a temp file, checked, then renamed.
   - On any failure, the previous complete version stays in place.
4. **Last good state survives.** When a step fails, the dashboard and the addon keep the last good numbers, show their age and say what failed: "Refresh failed at 06:02 (disk full) · showing yesterday's numbers".
5. **Never lose your events.**
   - The addon deletes an event from SavedVariables only after AHSync confirms it was **committed** (`ingestedUntil`, §2.2), and AHSync writes `ingestedUntil` only after the database commit.
   - Unconfirmed events are simply sent again next time.
6. **Validate at every boundary, and degrade instead of crashing.**
   - Each file has a format version and a check: AHF responses, AHledger's price table (its `AHL1` header, §2.3), SavedVariables, the market intelligence and the addon files.
   - One bad item is set aside with its error, and everything else carries on.
   - Errors go to `data/ahsync.log` (rotated, never containing the API key) and to the Status page.
   - The data flow is logged at INFO too, so it can be followed: each SavedVariables read (file, whether the `.bak` was used, scans and events stored, whose `ingestedUntil` moved, rows set aside) or why a file wasn't read (S17), the re-plan after it, and each addon file written (name, bytes, `generatedAt`, open actions or items, EX15). Names and counts only, never file contents or secrets.

**AHSync failures:**

| Failure | What happens | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| Network timeout, 5xx, 429 | retry with backoff (1, 2, 4, 8, 16 s: the first try plus 5 retries), honouring `Retry-After` up to 300 s; a longer `Retry-After` skips the run | the next run resumes at `sync_state` |
| 401 (key rejected or expired) | that request is retried once without the key; if the public request works, the run continues on the free path; Status "API key rejected" | fix the key; nothing crashes (FM25) |
| Any other HTTP error on `/files` or `/me` | that market is skipped for this run; Status line | the next run retries |
| AHF file truncated or bad gzip/JSON | not committed; logged; the hour gets a `fetch_failures` row and `last_hour` moves on, so later hours still sync | each later run retries the `fetch_failures` hours first; 3 failures in a row → "bad file" on Status; an hour that ages out of every source while still failing is added to the "lost" report, never dropped silently |
| Premium file refused (`403`) mid-run | that endpoint is disabled for the run; its remaining hours are not requested and are reported as lost (or day-only if day candles cover them) | Status line; re-checked at the next `/me` (FM26) |
| Hour file with an unexpected shape | treated as an API change, not a bad file: the endpoint is disabled, the hour loop stops, and `last_hour` does not advance | Status "AHF format changed: h1"; nothing is lost while the parser is fixed (FM27) |
| Live table (`GET /ah/:market?since=`) | not recorded yet, so unsupported: skipped quietly (one log line, no Status error) | add a fixture and a parser when its format is known |
| AHF response with an unexpected shape (API change) | that endpoint is disabled for the run; the last good data is kept | Status: "AHF format changed: snapshot"; record a new fixture and fix the parser |
| Suspect price (below 10% of `p10_30` or above 10× `p90_30`) | stored but flagged (`ItemIntel.cur_suspect`, FM5): the item is never flipped, its AH book is no craft mat source and it is no disenchant buy | is ordinary again once confirmed by a second observation: two hourly closes in the last 24 h out of range. It is also left out of the statistics (medians, trend, weekly patterns: `prices.drop_suspect`, FM31) until another out-of-range close within 24 h confirms it; the percentiles that define the range use every close. The addon's own mat sourcing skips such listings unless AHSync confirmed the price (A58) |
| Crash or power loss mid catch-up | SQLite rolls back the open transaction | resumes at the next missing hour (S2) |
| Crash mid full refresh | staging tables are dropped on start | the previous `item_stats` and plan stay live; the refresh re-runs |
| One item fails in the refresh (bad data, engine error) | that item is excluded and listed on Status with its error | the rest of the refresh completes |
| Disk full | writes stop; nothing is half-written | Status error; it resumes when space is free |
| Database corrupted | `PRAGMA quick_check` fails on start | restore from the newest of 3 daily backups (SQLite backup API, `data/backups/`); history since then is re-fetched where AHF still has it |
| SavedVariables half-written or unparsable | not ingested; the file has to be unchanged for 2 s first (§2.2) | falls back to WoW's `AHProfit.lua.bak`; if that fails too, it waits for the next write. Nothing is pruned, because `ingestedUntil` doesn't move |
| SavedVariables from a newer addon version | ignored, with a message | "Update AHSync" on Status |
| An addon file write blocked (file locked, antivirus) | retried 3 times over 10 s | the old file stays; Status warns |
| AHSync started twice | the second instance finds `data/ahsync.lock` held and exits with a message | — |
| `data/ahsync.stop` appears (`tools/deploy.py --restart`) | the steady-state loop ends at its next tick and AHSync exits, deleting the file; a stale one is deleted by the instance that takes the lock, so it never stops a start, and a second start that fails on the lock never clears a pending request | `deploy.py --restart` gives up after 60 s if the lock isn't freed, removes the stop file and leaves AHSync running (FM36, FM37) |
| A dashboard client stalls mid-request | requests run on their own daemon threads with a 10 s timeout, so others are answered and exit never waits for it | — (FM28) |
| A stored dashboard page can't be decoded | that request answers `503 bad_view` and is logged | the next full refresh replaces it (FM29) |
| Bad `config.toml` | refuses to start with the line and the reason | — |
| A recipe loop in the dependency graph (desktop half built M11; the addon's check built M12, next row) | the desktop cuts the back edge as chains do (C13), exports an acyclic graph with its order, and logs the loop at INFO | — (FM39) |
| AHledger unreachable (timeout, 5xx, connection refused; **built**, M19) | the poll gives up for this cycle after the usual backoff; the last good table's rows stay, nothing is written, no exception reaches the scheduler; the alert "AHledger: unreachable since Wed 14:00 (3 tries)" counts failed polls since the last success | the next poll, 30 min later; the alert clears on the first success (FM43) |
| AHledger price table with a bad header (not `AHL1`, another market, a non-integer `updated` or row count) | treated as a format change: nothing from that table is written, the ETag isn't kept, so the next poll fetches it whole | Status "AHledger format changed: pricetable"; record a new fixture and fix the parser (FM44) |
| An AHledger row that doesn't parse (a non-integer id or field, too few fields) | that row is skipped and counted in the log line ("AHledger forever.pvp.horde.us: 2,206 rows stored, 1 skipped"); every other row is written | — (FM45) |
| AHledger answers `404` for the market (not in `/v1/markets`, or its address is wrong) | AHledger is skipped for that market until the next start or the next day; no backfill | Status "AHledger has no market forever.pvp.horde.us": set `addresses.ahledger`, or `""` to stop asking (FM46) |
| `429` from AHledger (rate limit) | waits `Retry-After` (up to 300 s) and goes on; a longer one ends the price poll or the backfill for this run; the backfill's checkpoint is already committed | the backfill resumes at the next key on its next run (FM47) |
| The candidate export would push `MarketIntelligence.lua` past 2 MB (built M11) | the lowest-adjusted alternatives are dropped first, never a funded candidate, until it fits; Status "candidate export trimmed to 112 of 150 to fit 2 MB" | lower `candidate_export`, or it clears when the file fits again (FM40) |
| The packaged app is started while another instance holds the lock (**planned** M22, §3.7.6) | the second shell writes `<data>/ahsync.show` and exits 0, and the running one shows its window; with `--autostart` nothing is written | — (FM48) |
| pywebview cannot start the window: no WebView2 runtime, a missing backend (built M24, §3.7.1) | AHSync runs on with the tray; every open goes to the default browser; the Status line `window_unavailable` (warn) and one log line | — (FM56) |
| `wow_path` has no `Interface/AddOns`, or it isn't writable (**planned** M22, §3.7.7) | the addon isn't installed, nothing is half-copied; the Addon line reads "not installed: no Interface/AddOns under <wow_path>" or "not installed: Interface/AddOns not writable" (amber) | fix the folder; the next start installs it (FM49) |
| Setup wrote `config.toml` but the key file can't be written (**planned** M23, §3.7.4) | `500 {"error": "key_not_saved"}` with the reason, never the key; the config is kept; Status line `api_key_not_saved` | enter the key again, or set `AUCTIONHOUSE_APIKEY` (FM50) |
| The Run key or the LaunchAgent can't be written (built M24, §3.7.5) | `POST /api/autostart` answers `500 {"error": "autostart_not_set"}`; Status "Start at login not set"; the toggle shows the real state | try again; the line clears on the next success (FM51) |
| The update check is offline, gets `404` (no release yet), `403` (rate limit) or a body without `tag_name` (built M25, §3.7.8) | no alert, no pill; one log line; the Update line "not checked: offline since Fri 06:00" | the next check, tomorrow (FM52) |
| The update download is cut off, its hash doesn't match `SHA256SUMS`, or `SHA256SUMS` lacks the asset (built M25, §3.7.9) | the file is deleted; "Update to 0.4.0 failed: <reason> · still on 0.3.1 · tries again tomorrow"; the app runs on | tomorrow's check tries again (FM53) |
| `/Applications/AHSync.app` isn't writable by the user (**planned** M27, §3.7.9) | nothing is attempted; "Update downloaded · AHSync can't replace itself here; install it from the release page" with the link | install the dmg by hand (FM54) |
| The swap helper's move or launch fails on macOS, or the silent installer exits non-zero on Windows (**planned** M27, §3.7.9) | the previous app is put back and opened (macOS) or keeps running (Windows); the next start reads "Update to 0.4.0 failed · still on 0.3.1 · tries again tomorrow" | tomorrow's check tries again; the release page works meanwhile (FM55) |

**Addon failures (in game):**

| Failure | What happens | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| `MarketIntelligence.lua` missing | empty state "Run AHSync, then /reload" | — |
| `PortfolioPlan.lua` missing or old | the addon's own recommendations still work; plan rows and cross-character advice are hidden | the next AHSync run writes it |
| An addon file from another schema version, or its check fails | treated as missing, plus "AHSync and AHProfit versions differ" | update either side |
| An addon file from an older AHSync build (`exportVersion` lower than the addon's, or missing) | refused like another schema: no market items or no plan rows, nothing pruned; the footer says "AHSync is older than this addon: restart AHSync, then /reload" | restart AHSync (`tools/deploy.py --restart`), then `/reload` (FM34) |
| An addon file from a newer AHSync build (`exportVersion` higher than the addon's) | refused the same way; the footer says "This addon is older than AHSync's files: run tools/deploy.py, then /reload" | deploy the addon, then `/reload` (FM35) |
| Plan older than your last session (`PortfolioPlan.lua` `basedOnUntil` < your newest event) | the plan is shown as **provisional**: actions already matched by your newer events are marked done locally (`Plan.DoneLocally`, FM18: events after the character's `ingestedUntil`; a confirmed purchase takes its units off the earliest buy of that item, a confirmed post its stack off the earliest post, a craft one craft off the earliest craft of its product; an action with nothing left goes; Today's and Week's footers say "provisional: N done in game since AHSync's plan") | the next AHSync run replaces it |
| Lua error in the engine or UI | caught with `pcall` at the UI boundary; shown as a status line | that list shows its last good state |
| Portfolio snapshot older than the addon's own state (built, M14: `Plan.LivePositions`, `Plan.LocalPositions`) | positions and done actions the addon recorded after the snapshot's `basedOnUntil` stay as the addon has them; the older snapshot never overwrites them, and cross-character numbers show the snapshot's time | the next AHSync export includes the events (FM38) |
| An exported dependency graph whose order isn't topological (built M12: `Graph.Load`, `Adapt.Problem`) | refused with the status line "dependency graph invalid: using AHSync's numbers", never a Lua error; nothing is repriced, candidates keep their exported values and reselection still runs on them | the next AHSync export (FM39) |
| Full scan interrupted (AH closed, disconnect, combat, reload) | saved as `partial` with its coverage %. Partial books are **never** used for volume diffs, which need complete books | the next full scan starts fresh |
| Search queue interrupted (AH closed) | the queue is dropped | rebuilt on the next AH open |
| A buy or post hook fires but the action fails (outbid, sold out, not enough gold, error) | recorded as an **attempt**. It counts only when confirmed: by the "won" message or mail for a buy, by the own-auctions list for a post. Unconfirmed attempts expire after 1 h | not matched, no cost basis change |
| WoW crashes before SavedVariables is written | that session's events are lost | at the next login, the inventory snapshot shows the true state; differences are recorded as "untracked changes" (cost 0, flagged), so nothing is invented. **Nothing asks you to act**: no prompt and no forced `/reload`. Recommendations carry on from the snapshot, with untracked items valued at their opportunity cost like found items (§2.2), so the next deal is found as usual |
| SavedVariables grows past its cap | the oldest un-ingested full scans are dropped first, never events | status warning: "Run AHSync to sync" |
| Addon upgraded with a new SavedVariables layout | migrated forward on load by version, step by step; the original is kept until the migration is saved | a failed migration leaves the old data untouched and shows an error |

**Tests:**
- Every row in the two tables above has a scenario (FM1–FM24, plus UI4 for a missing `MarketIntelligence.lua` and A5 for the Lua error boundary; see [SCENARIOS.md](SCENARIOS.md)), run against `FakeTransport`, a fake filesystem and `wow_stubs.lua`. Fault injection is deterministic: a fake fails "on the 3rd write" or "after 40% of the file", never at random.
- An invariant (FI1, §8.7) checks that a crash at any commit point, followed by a restart, gives the same final database and addon files as an uninterrupted run.

**Addon performance tests:**
- Lua tests count work, not time, so they are deterministic:
  - a price change must touch only its own item's row;
  - a hidden window must not redraw;
  - tooltips must hit the decode cache after the first lookup;
  - no `OnUpdate` script may be set (checked by `wow_stubs.lua`).
- `/ahp perf` reports the real numbers in game against the budgets.

## 6. To verify and to build

1. ~~**API key tier**~~: done 2026-10-16 (§2.1). The key has Premium features through the contributor role, which expires unless scans are uploaded. The `books`, candle, snapshot and items formats were recorded on 2026-10-16 (§2.1 Response formats). Still to check: the `volume` row format, because the file was empty for our market. Re-run `tools/record_fixtures.py` once it has data.
2. **Forever game APIs.** Verified 2026-10-16 on client 1.60.1 70425 through the addon's diagnostics: **Forever uses the modern (retail-style) API, not Classic's.**
   - Present: `GetProfessions`/`GetProfessionInfo`, `C_TradeSkillUI`, `C_MerchantFrame`, `C_Container`, the `GetInbox*` mail functions, `C_AuctionHouse`.
   - Missing: `GetNumSkillLines`/`GetSkillLineInfo` (there is no Classic Skills tab), `GetTradeSkill*`, `GetCraft*`, `GetMerchantItemInfo`, the legacy `GetContainerItemInfo`, `QueryAuctionItems`/`CanSendAuctionQuery`/`GetAuctionItemInfo`. The event `TRADE_SKILL_UPDATE` is unknown.
   - Bags and bank capture work through `C_Container`. Professions and the merchant now use the modern calls (`Adapter.lua` picks them when present).
   - `C_TradeSkillUI` is the Dragonflight-era shape (`GetAllRecipeIDs`, `GetRecipeInfo`, `GetRecipeSchematic`, `GetBaseProfessionInfo`, `IsTradeSkillLinked`, …). Recipe ids are spell ids. The window lists unlearned recipes too, so only `learned` ones are marked known; linked, guild and NPC windows are not recorded. There is no Classic craft window (`CRAFT_SHOW` is unknown); Enchanting uses the trade skill UI.
   - `C_AuctionHouse` is the modern API, including `ReplicateItems`, `GetReplicateItemInfo`, `GetReplicateItemTimeLeft` and `GetTimeLeftBandInfo`, so the full scan of §4.2 is possible. M6 is written against it.
   - The recipe reader was checked in game on 2026-10-16: Cooking 132 and First Aid 32 recipes defined, the 6 learned ones marked known, a Herbalism window (3 Forever recipes, none learned) defined only, no errors.
   - Still to check: the tooltip hook (M5), and the AH scan and the post, buy and mail hooks (M6, M6b).
   - Still to check: whether `GetOwnedAuctionInfo` fills `timeLeftSeconds` (and what `timeLeft` holds) on Forever. Without a number the addon saves no end for that auction (A70); the diagnostics' `ownAuctions` probe and `tools/savedvars_report.py` say which.
3. **Disenchant tables and skill brackets** on Forever.
4. **AH fees and auction durations**: deposit rate per duration and the 5% / 15% cut. The durations are verified: 2, 8 and 24 h (2026-10-18).
5. **Time-left bands**: the bounds of short / medium / long / very long (§3.3.1).
6. **AH search limits**: whether searches are throttled, and whether each one needs a hardware click (§4.0).
7. **Recipe cooldown API** for transmutes and similar recipes.
8. **Tracking hooks**: the post and buy functions to hook, the sold / expired / won system messages, the mail invoice API, and the send-mail hook. Also check whether mail between your own characters arrives instantly or after a delay (it changes how mail steps are spaced in a chain).
9. **wow-ui-sim spike** (§8.4, layer 3): can it load a Classic-style addon (`## Interface: 16001`) and render AHProfit's frames headless through Docker on Windows? It targets the retail client, and Classic support is not documented.
10. **Folder name after launch.**
11. ~~**Item key from a link**~~: done 2026-10-16.
   - Forever's links use the modern format: `item:id:enchant:gem1:gem2:gem3:gem4:suffixID:unique:level:spec:modifiersMask:context:numBonusIDs:bonusID…`. A random suffix is a **bonus ID**; the classic suffix field is empty.
   - AHF's `/items.bonusLists` maps a bonus ID to `[suffix id, stats]`, and AHF's key uses **`v = 2 × suffix id`** (true for all 523 variants in `/items`).
   - Checked on a real link: Aboriginal Gloves of Frost Protection carries bonus 12742 → suffix 14322 → v = 28644 → key 61512521627429, which is AHF's variant of that name.
   - The addon computes it in `Adapter.ItemKey` from `Data/BonusSuffixes.lua`, generated by `tools/gen_bonus_suffixes.py` (re-run it when AHF adds suffixes).
12. **To build: the undercut share `u(p)`** (§3.3.2). `sellprob.undercut_share` is written and tested but nothing calls it; every simulation uses `default_undercut_share` (0.5). It needs the share of newly listed units priced at or below yours, from book history (AHF books or your scans).
13. **To build: price-tier sell-through `ST_tier`** (§3.3.2). Never computed, so `blend` always returns `P_queue` (`e` = 0). It needs sold versus expired auctions per price tier over 30 days, from the order-book diffs (§3.3.1).
14. **Portfolio: learned recipes live** (§4.0). Whether Forever fires an event when a recipe is learned (`NEW_RECIPE_LEARNED` on retail) so the addon can add it to its graph at once, or whether it has to wait for the next profession window.
15. **Portfolio: live gold.** That `GetMoney` and `PLAYER_MONEY` give the character's gold at once after a buy, a post's deposit and a mail payout on Forever (the addon funds from it, §4.0).
16. **Portfolio: benchmarks.** `candidate_export` between 100 and 200 against the 2 MB market file, login time (≤ 100 ms, §5.1) and the 2 ms state-change budget of a reselection; the selection and its swap pass against the full refresh's 120 s and the incremental update's 1 s (§3.1.2). The default 150 is a starting point. Measured at M10: see §3.1.2 (the full refresh is within budget; the re-plan on the generated worst case is not).
17. **Portfolio: the greedy fill with one swap pass** (§3.4D.4) is a heuristic. Compare it against an exhaustive search on small generated worlds before M10 is done; if it misses often, the fallback is a second fill by plain adjusted profit, keeping the better of the two.
18. **Gathering capture** (§3.4E.3, M17): the spell names or ids of Mining, Herb Gathering, Skinning and Fishing on Forever and which event marks each cast done (fishing is channelled, and its loot opens on the bobber click); whether `GetLootSourceInfo` gives a creature GUID for corpse loot; whether `PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD` carries `isInitialLogin`; whether autoloot fires `LOOT_OPENED` twice. Built M17 on Classic's answers (spell names via `GetSpellInfo`, `UNIT_SPELLCAST_CHANNEL_START` as a second Fishing trigger, a 10 s / 35 s window per cast, the GUID's type); `tools/savedvars_report.py` reads the diagnostics' probes of `GetSpellInfo` and `GetLootSourceInfo` and the loot traces to confirm them after a session. And the catalog itself: Forever's customised items and professions (§3.4E.1).

19. **AHledger** (§2.3, M19-M21). **Answered live on 2026-11-01:**
    - **`updated`:** a table with rows carries its newest scan's time; it equals the history's `latest.observedAt`, whose `source` is "Addon scan". An empty table carries its publish time (SRC19).
    - **`Cache-Control`:** the price table sends `max-age=60, s-maxage=60`, `/v1/prices` sends `max-age=60, s-maxage=300`, and `/v1/markets` sends `max-age=300`.
    - **Compression:** the price table comes gzip-compressed when asked (SRC17), with a weak ETag (`W/"…"`), which AHSync sends back as it came.
    - **History depth:** this market's history starts 2026-09-21, so `range=90d` gave 30 daily buckets.
    - **Scans stopped:** every Forever market's newest scan is 21–23 Oct, and the other Forever markets serve empty tables.
    **Still open:**
    - Whether `updated` is AHledger's newest **full** scan or any scan.
    - Whether a `range=24h` series is one point per scan or hourly buckets: it was empty everywhere, as there was no scan in the last day.
    - Whether the table ever lists suffixed items under a base id.
    `tools/record_fixtures.py --ahledger` recorded the fixtures live on 2026-11-01.

20. **Forever's macOS install folder** (§3.7.3, M23/M26): `/Applications/World of Warcraft/_classic_beta_` is inferred from a forum post naming `/Applications/World of Warcraft/_classic_beta_/WTF/` and is **UNVERIFIED**; checked on the user's Apple Silicon Mac during M23 or M26, and `wow_candidates()` corrected if it differs.
21. **Hardened-runtime entitlements** the bundled Python needs (§3.7.12, M26): `com.apple.security.cs.allow-unsigned-executable-memory` is expected, `com.apple.security.cs.disable-library-validation` possibly; settled by the first notarization, whose log names any binary it rejects; M26 ships the first alone. Also to confirm at the first CI run: Inno Setup preinstalled on `windows-latest` (fallback `choco install innosetup`) and Lua 5.1 through `leafo/gh-actions-lua` on both images.
22. **pystray and pywebview sharing one `NSApplication` on macOS** (§3.7.1, M24/M26): pystray's `Icon.run_detached()` with `darwin_nsapplication` under pywebview's loop is documented by pystray but **UNVERIFIED** here, as is cancelling pywebview's `closing` event to hide the window on both backends; checked on the user's Mac, and the Windows half from source in M24 (built: the window, the tray, Quit's close and the hide on close work with Edge WebView2 and pystray on Windows 11).

## 8. Testing

### 8.1 Principles

- **Every core rule has a named scenario.** Each scenario reads Given / When / Then in plain numbers a person can check by hand. The scenario name is the test name, and it starts with the scenario's ID (for example `P12`).
- **One list of scenarios: [SCENARIOS.md](SCENARIOS.md).** `tests/test_conventions.py` keeps it and the tests in step. It fails on a listed ID with no test, a test ID that isn't listed, or a duplicate ID.
  - **Not built yet:** IDs whose feature isn't built yet go in `tests/pending_scenarios.txt`, so the suite stays green while the project grows. The test also fails when a pending ID already has a test, so the list only shrinks, and when it names an ID that isn't listed.
  - **What counts as a test:** a Python test named `test_<ID>_…`, a Lua `it("<ID> …", …)` in a `*_spec.lua`, or an `id` in `tests/scenarios/*.json`.
- **Real data as the template, mocked scenarios on top.** `tools/record_fixtures.py` records real AHF responses (`/me`, `/files`, snapshot, h1, d1, `/items`) and trims them to a small set of real items into `tests/fixtures/ahf/`. Builders load that template and apply the changes a scenario needs ("Copper Bar sells 40/day", "no run for 10 days", "AH listing undercut to 45s").
- **No network, no real clock, no WoW in tests.** HTTP goes through an injected transport (`FakeTransport` serves fixture files and can return 429/404/503). Time comes from an injected `Clock` (`FakeClock`). Lua tests run outside the game against `wow_stubs.lua`.
- **One source of truth for the formulas.** Engine scenarios live in `tests/scenarios/*.json`. Python reads them directly. `tools/gen_lua_fixtures.py` converts them to Lua so the Lua engine port is checked against **the same expected numbers**.
- **Deterministic and fully covered.** Every number on every tab is produced by a tested pure function (§8.5). The engine is deterministic (§8.6), its formulas pass invariant tests (§8.7), and the sell probability is checked against real outcomes (§8.8).
- **Runners**: `pytest` and `coverage.py` (dev-only dependencies; the app itself stays standard-library only) and a small Lua 5.1 runner (`tests/support/lua/runner.lua`, run with a standalone `lua5.1`). `tools/test.ps1` runs both.

### 8.2 Scenario format

```json
{
  "name": "volume: undercut repost counts as one sale plus one new listing",
  "template": "pvp_horde_small",
  "given": { "books": [ {"at": "T0", "entries": [["Linen Cloth", "50c", 20, "long", 1]]},
                        {"at": "T0+2h", "entries": [["Linen Cloth", "45c", 20, "long", 1]]} ] },
  "when":  "estimate_volume",
  "then":  { "Linen Cloth": {"sold": 20, "expired": 0, "listed": 20} },
  "why":   "Known limitation: price changed, so the 50c auction looks gone and the 45c one looks new."
}
```

Builders accept item names, `"1g 20s 5c"` prices and `T0+2h` times, and convert them to keys, copper and ms. A failed test prints the scenario's `why`.

### 8.3 Required scenarios (minimum set)

The minimum set lives in **[SCENARIOS.md](SCENARIOS.md)**, grouped by module, with IDs V*, S*, FM*, SEC*, P*, W*, R*, O*, D*, C*, M*, IU*, CF*, T*, A* and UI*, TT*, K*, the planned portfolio IDs PF*, PO* and DG* (§3.4D), the planned gathering IDs GA* (§3.4E), the AHledger source and data coverage IDs SRC6–SRC16, S18–S20, FM43–FM47 and D89–D96 (§2.3, §3.5; built), the planned distribution and runtime IDs DR1–DR28, FM48–FM55, SEC13–SEC15, D97 and D98 (§3.7), plus the invariants PI*, VI*, CI*, PL*, VW*, LP* and FI* (§8.7).
Spec sections refer to scenarios by ID. A convention test keeps that file and the tests in step (§8.1).

### 8.4 Visual tests (addon UI)

No tool renders a Classic-era addon exactly like the game outside it, so there are four layers. Each one catches what the layer before it can't.

1. **View-model tests** (plain Lua, always run). These check what each tab shows: which rows, in what order, with which labels, colours and formatted money. Most UI bugs are caught here.
2. **Layout snapshots** (our Lua runner, always run).
   - `wow_stubs.lua` frames record their size, anchors, text, font and visibility. `layout.lua` resolves the anchors into rectangles on a 1920 × 1080 screen at UI scale 1. Text width is estimated from the character count × the font's average glyph width.
   - Each scenario writes a **frame-tree snapshot** (indented text: name, rectangle, text, shown). It is compared with `tests/visual/golden/<scenario>.txt`.
   - **Automatic checks:** every shown frame lies inside its parent; text never overflows its box unless it is set to truncate; sibling rows don't overlap; buttons are at least 20 px tall.
   - `python tools/visual.py --update` rewrites the golden files. Review the diff like code.
   - `python tools/visual.py --html` draws the snapshot rectangles and text to `tests/visual/out/<scenario>.html`, for a quick look next to the mockup.
3. **Rendered screenshots** (optional, through wow-ui-sim in Docker). The same scenarios are rendered to PNG and compared with `tests/visual/golden/*.png` within a pixel tolerance. Pillow is a dev-only dependency for the comparison. This depends on the §6 spike. If wow-ui-sim can't load a Classic-style addon, this layer is dropped, and layer 2's HTML view and layer 4 cover it.
4. **In game** (manual, once per milestone): `/ahp demo`, then screenshot each tab and compare with the mockup.

Scenarios UI1–UI19: [SCENARIOS.md §2](SCENARIOS.md#2-visual-tests-ui1ui19). The tooltip is the client's `GameTooltip`, not a frame tree of ours, so UI17's snapshot is its 14 variants' lines with their estimated widths (`Layout.GoldenText`).

### 8.5 Every number on every tab is tested

Each number a tab shows comes from a pure function in `ahsync/engine/`. The dashboard's `web/` and the addon's frame code only display it, and do no arithmetic of their own. Tab totals, sorting, filters and tooltip deltas live in `engine/views.py`, with the Lua twin in `UI/ViewModel.lua`.

The view and tooltip scenarios (D16–D18, TT1–TT21) are in [SCENARIOS.md §3](SCENARIOS.md#3-views-and-tooltip-d16d22-tt1tt21).

### 8.6 Determinism

The engine must give **the same output for the same input, every time and on every machine**, in both languages.

- **No hidden inputs.** The current time is passed in as `now_ms`. There is no randomness. Config values are arguments.
- **Explicit order.** Every sort has a full tie-break that ends in a unique key, such as an item key or a character id. Nothing depends on dict, set or Lua `pairs` order. Lua code walks sorted key lists.
- **Exact numbers.** The numeric rules in §3.3 apply: integer copper, floats only for expected values, `floor(x + 0.5)` at output, and the defined median and percentile. Tests compare rounded output exactly, not "close enough".
- **Whole-plan snapshot** (built: WP1). A fixture world, `tests/fixtures/worlds/w1.json`, holds characters, inventory, recipes, prices, order books, history and `now_ms`, as the database rows catch-up and ingest would write. It is generated by `tools/gen_world.py` (fixed seed; the JSON is committed): two characters, 12 items, 5 with 6 weeks of hourly closes and a weekly pattern and one with an early pattern, three complete scans, a two-step craft chain and a disenchantable green, using the mockups' item names. `tests/tools/test_snapshot.py` (WP1) loads it into a fresh database, runs `refresh_all` at its `now_ms`, writes both addon files, and compares every page payload (the `views` table), the plan's actions and the two files' text, as one sorted-key JSON document, with `tests/fixtures/worlds/w1.expected.json`; running it twice must give byte-identical output. Heat-map cells are kept as digests and a long market file is trimmed to its first lines, so the snapshot stays small. After an intended change, `python tools/snapshot.py --update` rewrites it and prints a diff summary; review the diff. Where the mockups and the engine disagree, the engine's tested numbers win and the mockup is updated.

### 8.7 Invariants: is the logic sound?

Single scenarios check one example. Invariants check that a formula behaves sensibly across many inputs.

- **How cases are made.** `tests/support/gen.py` makes cases from a fixed seed with `random.Random(seed)`. A failure prints the seed and the inputs, so it can be reproduced. The generated cases are saved to `tests/properties/*.json`, so the Lua port is checked against exactly the same cases.
- **Scale.** At least 1,000 cases per invariant.

The invariants (PI1–PI17, VI1–VI4, CI1–CI3, PL1–PL5, VW1, LP1, FI1–FI2, and the portfolio invariants PF25–PF27) are listed in [SCENARIOS.md §5](SCENARIOS.md#5-invariants-spec-87), with the other test IDs, so the convention test (§8.1) sees one list.

### 8.8 Calibration: does the chance to sell match reality?

**Volume backtest.** Where both exist for the same item and day, book-estimated sales, scaled to a whole day by the hours observed, are compared with id-diff volume: the Status page reports the median ratio and the share of days within ±25%, per source, over the last 30 days (K4). A ratio far from 1 shows up before it misleads the plan. **Built** (K4): `books` is compared with every source whose cover kind is `ids` (none until a Blizzard API job exists), by `engine.volume.backtest`, on each market's Status card. Your mail is not a comparison: it sees only your own auctions.

The invariants prove the formula is sensible. Only your real sales can show whether it is *right*. Every post therefore records the `P'` it was planned with (`posts.p_at_post`). `sell_record.calibration` compares that with what actually happened:

- It groups resolved posts into buckets by predicted chance: `[0, 0.2)`, `[0.2, 0.4)`, `[0.4, 0.6)`, `[0.6, 0.8)` and `[0.8, 1]`. Each bucket includes its lower edge, and the last one also includes 1. It shows each bucket's predicted versus actual sell rate.
- It reports the **Brier score**, the mean of `(P' − outcome)²` where outcome is 1 for sold and 0 for expired. Lower is better. Always guessing 50% scores 0.25.
- The **Status** page shows both. A bucket that is off by more than 15 points, with at least 20 posts in it, raises a warning, e.g. "40–60% predictions actually sold 28%". That suggests `default_sell_prob`, `own_record_weight` or the volume source needs adjusting.

**Expected against realized profit** (K7). The chance to sell is one estimate; the expected net is the one the plan is judged by. The calibration section ends with one line, "Expected vs realized profit (last 90 days): realized +4s 7c of expected +5s 67c (72%)", from the same sums as History's tiles (§3.5, `tracking.profit_sums`) over every character's events of the last 90 days. The Status page recomputes them from the journal on request (`app/status_page`), since the stored History view covers only 30 days; the read is the journal's small tables, like the calibration's. With nothing resolved there is no line.

**Built** (K1–K3, K5, K6): `engine/sell_record.calibration` over the last 90 days of sold and expired posts with a `p_at_post`, which the tracking pass copies from the matched action's planned `P'` (`actions.conf`); a post that matched no action, such as one the addon suggested on its own, is left out. The Status page shows it below the cards (`engine/status_view.calibration_view`), with the bucket warnings as alerts in that section.

Scenarios K1–K7: [SCENARIOS.md §4](SCENARIOS.md#4-calibration-k1k3).

### 8.9 Definition of done for any feature

The scenarios for that feature are written first, they are listed in [SCENARIOS.md](SCENARIOS.md), and they pass in both languages where the feature exists in both.

## 9. Out of scope (v1)

Automated buying or posting (not allowed by Blizzard), cross-market arbitrage, planning for the neutral AH (AHF has no market for it), and uploading scans to AHF (the AHF addon already does that).

**Gathering (§3.4E), out of scope for V1:** farming-route recommendations; monster spawn maps; exhaustive drop tables; farming locations; yields from external drop-rate databases; equipment, rare-recipe and pattern farming; an in-game Gathering tab; automated movement or gathering; gear and enchant bonuses to gathering skill; multi-material session gold per hour and a session history view; any effect of farming on the Portfolio (farmed items are found stock).
